### 2.10 Codegen and measurement pitfalls (swarm phase 0, 2026-10-25)

Everything here was measured on the microbenchmark of 2.9, the entry number names the SASS evidence in `$SWARM/TECHNIQUES.md`.

- Three-input min and max are free. ptxas fuses nested `max`, nested `fmaxf`, nested `__hmax2` and `max(v + a, b)` into `VIMNMX3`, `FMNMX3`, `VHMNMX` and `VIADDMNMX` at the same 6 cycles as the two-input form, so write compare networks against two candidates and threshold clamps in that shape, no inline PTX needed (T7). Integer min and max is 6 cycles against 4 for `IMAD` and `FFMA`, so an index select through a predicated `IMAD` can beat a max on the index path.
- 8-bit SIMD min and max (`__vmaxu4`) are a 10-instruction emulation at 28 cycles on sm_100 and sm_103, the native u8x4 form exists only on the sm_107f and sm_120f families. Widen fp8 keys to 16-bit lanes with `prmt` and run the u16x2 network (T8).
- Integer per-half compare masks (`__vcmpgtu2`, `__vcmpgtu4`) are emulated at 20 to 24 cycles, while the bf16x2 masks (`__hgt2_mask`, PTX `set.gt.u32.bf16x2`) and `min.bf16x2` and `max.bf16x2` are single 6-cycle instructions. Keep bf16 keys as bf16 inside packed networks and filters, twiddled u16x2 is fine only for pure min and max (T18). Semantics: `+0.0 > -0.0` in `max.bf16x2` and in `redux.sync.f32`, and a NaN input loses to the other operand unless `.NaN` is given, which is what the study's oracle expects for signed zero.
- `redux.sync` on f32 needs the `sm_100f` build. The mandated `-arch=sm_100` build cannot emit it and the family build runs on compute capability 10.0, 10.3 and 10.7. The family and arch macros (`__CUDA_ARCH_FAMILY_SPECIFIC__`) exist only in the device pass, so host code that decides whether to launch the family kernel needs its own define, otherwise the kernel compiles and is never launched (T2, `INTRINSICS.md` section 1). No CUDA C intrinsic exists, `__reduce_max_sync` is integer only, inline PTX is the only access.
- Lane election and ranks: `__ffs` is `BREV` plus `FLO` at 40 cycles, `__fns` is a 43-instruction emulation at 177, `__popc` is 23 and `bmsk` is native. Rank candidates with `popc(ballot & lanemask_lt)`, elect with `elect.sync` (deterministic, hides behind a redux) or with a redux of the lane id, and never put `__fns` on a critical path (T4, T6).
- `match.any` costs 51 cycles with one distinct value and about 11 more per further distinct value, 392 at 32. Only 2- to 3-bit digits or near-uniform data can afford it, ballot-per-bit counting is cheaper from 4-bit digits on (T5). This closed R6 (section 10.5).
- Independent warp collectives pipeline at about 6 cycles each after the first (ballot 27 then 6 per further, redux 22 then 5.7, shuffle 30 then 4.9), not 4. Model a round of IPT independent ballots as 27 + 6 (IPT - 1), which is 69 at IPT 8 and 124 at IPT 16 (T17). The N1 and W3 cost models were corrected to this on 2026-10-25.
- Per-lane list shifts (T19). In the packed W2 round the winner shifts its sorted list by one. Written as selects (`list[i] = win ? list[i + 1] : list[i]`) the shifts hide behind the next redux up to 8 items per lane (26 cycles per round), written as a branch on the winner they compile to `IMAD.MOV` runs with `BSSY`, `BRA`, `BSYNC` and cost twice as much at 32 items (172 against 89). Measurement pitfall: an earlier version of the row measured 25 cycles at every IPT because the compiler had dropped every shift but the head's as dead code, so a reproduction must fold the whole list into the sink.
- Shared memory atomics (T9 to T11, T26, T27). The with-return form costs 55 uncontended and serializes at about one lane per cycle on one address (250 at 256-way). The no-return form on a provably warp-uniform address is rewritten by ptxas into one `CREDUX.MAX` plus one atomic by an elected lane per warp (T26 and T27 from the B2 cards, T10 and T11 corrected in place on 2026-10-25), so the 74.5-cycle block round issues eight atomics, not 256, and the warp's 22-cycle redux sits on the round's dependent chain. The July reading that the shared memory unit merges the burst was wrong, the compiler does it. A per-lane address defeats the aggregation and the hardware then serializes same-address lanes at 32 to 59 cycles per warp instruction, which is the regime of a shared memory histogram with data-dependent bins, so pre-combine equal keys in registers or spread hits over banked copies. The 64-bit shared max is a CAS loop (148 uncontended, 576 at 32-way, 4544 per 256-thread round), never pack value and index into 64 bits for shared atomics. Pre-reducing with redux before the atomic makes a round slower (149 against 74.5). The best non-atomic block round is redux, smem, redux at 93, not the shuffle tree at 290.
- Sink pitfall (T14). A sink guarded by a `%smid` read inside the timed region hides any chain shorter than about 80 cycles, because the chain runs in the shadow of the `S2R` latency. The first B200 run of the microbenchmark was invalid for this reason and is kept under `$SWARM/state/results/hw-researcher/superseded/`. Hoist the special-register read before the timed loop or make the guard depend on the chain value, and check every latency kernel's SASS for `S2R` or `LDC` inside the clock bracket. The smid guard in `nvbench_helper/device_side_benchmark.cuh` has this shape.
- Reassociation pitfall (T15). ptxas reassociates chains of one associative operation (16 dependent `max` became two `VIMNMX3` over hoisted constants, a chain of adds one `VIADD`, a chain of `prmt` one `prmt`), and an empty `asm volatile` barrier between steps does not stop it. Measure ALU latencies with pair chains interleaved with a dependent `IMAD` and subtract the `IMAD` chain. In real code, read the SASS of a hand-unrolled network before trusting its instruction count.
- Fills (T13). A dependent 4 to 16 KB fill from L2 costs 430 to 640 cycles through LDG plus STS or through `cp.async.bulk` alike, so pick the bulk path for registers and instruction count (one issuing thread), not for time. A pointer not marked read-only produces the generic `LD.E.128`, use `__ldg` or `const __restrict__` for `LDG.E.128.CONSTANT`.
- `movmatrix` transposes packed 16-bit keys at 45 cycles per 4 registers with no shared memory, against 63 for `stmatrix` plus `ldmatrix.trans` and 83 for scalar STS plus LDS, inline PTX only, no `cuda::ptx` wrapper in this checkout (T12).
- Harness loads and controls (B1 reference cards, 10.4). Scalar blocked loads are the wrong baseline for a blocked-arrangement primitive: in the first reference pass they cost 16.9k to 33.4k cycles per call at N 16384 and above (uncoalesced, 32 sectors per warp instruction), as much as the AIR primitive itself, and halved the apparent throughput, vectorized 16-byte loads fixed it. The generate-only control is not perfectly additive at N 16384 and above (it varies by up to 1100 cycles with k at the same N), so block latencies there carry a few percent of tolerance.
- Host toolchain (T20). The aarch64 build of cicc 13.4.92 emits branches around conditional swaps where the x86 build emits selects. ptxas re-predicates both, but the aarch64 SASS keeps 16 more instructions, 3 more registers and a three-step dependent chain per stage, 3 to 4 percent on the fp32 warp bitonic reference (583 against 605 cycles at N 32, 9970 against 10281 at N 512). Never compare cycles of a compare-based primitive between an x86-hosted and an aarch64-hosted build, and write swaps as explicit selects or min and max. This resolved the GB200 register puzzle of 10.4. Intrinsic-based compares (`__hgt`, redux, atomics) are unaffected. On the CUB header path of the packed bf16 word the same effect is 1.17x to 1.26x (1521 against 1205 cycles at N 128 k 32, a longer `ISETP` chain without branches), while explicitly written exchanges compile identically on both hosts (card `W1x_codegen_b200_20261025_1`), so the GB200 W1 cards overstate the gap of opt-W1's network over CUB by that factor and the B200 numbers are the ones to quote.
- Uniform-address atomics (T23, corrected by T39). nvcc 13.4 warp-aggregates a shared `atomicAdd` whose address it can prove uniform, inserting `S2R SR_LANEID` and `S2R SR_LTMASK` per site. The `B1_det` epilogue with constant-address tickets costs 344 cycles more than the class-indexed epilogue at fp32 N 1024 k 16, but the two kernels differ in more than the address, so the attribution to the aggregation is a hypothesis. The documented fix, an empty `asm volatile` on a constant index, is a measured no-op: ptxas re-derives the uniform address (identical SASS and 2137.4 against 2138.0 cycles). A same-kernel A/B with an index ptxas cannot constant-fold is still owed.
- Hoisted per-pass recompute (T24). A cheap per-item function of the key and block-uniform parameters recomputed in every pass can be CSE'd across passes into one live register per item, which doubled the register footprint of the N6 keys at IPT 128 (1.5 KB against 8.6 KB of spill between two builds that differed by one instruction per item). Pin the uniform parameters with an empty asm at the start of every pass.
- Schedule fragility of unrolled shuffle networks (T28). The same fp32 bitonic network (MaxK 32, four tiles) measured 1649 cycles at k 8 and k 16 and 2004 at k 32, the only source difference an epilogue store predicate, because the ptxas list scheduler reordered a basic block of 1100 instructions with 300 shuffles in flight. Treat a difference below about 20 percent between network variants as unproven until several cells and the SASS schedule agree, and measure every (N, k) separately even when MaxK makes them the same network.
- Guarded atomics on long tiles (T32). At 32 items per thread and above ptxas wraps every per-item `if (match) atomicAdd` in a `BSSY`, `BRA`, `BSYNC` region (516 regions in the B1 reference at IPT 64, 764 in B3), also when written as predicated `red.shared` in inline PTX. Guarded stores stay predicated. The options are an unconditional atomic into a per-thread dummy word (not measured) or fewer guarded items, which is what compaction buys.
- Local memory without reported spills (T34, T36). nvcc 13.4 stops unrolling a compile-time compare-exchange table somewhere between 543 and 1471 entries, and a register array that escapes into a lambda or a non-unrolled loop lands in local memory. In both cases `ptxas -v` reports 0 spill bytes and only the stack frame line, `STL` and `LDL` show it. B2 at IPT 128 ran 50 times slower than B1 with a 12800-byte stack frame, the first W1 level network ran 14871 instead of 3565 cycles with an 80-byte frame. The harness records `stack_bytes`, `stl_count` and `ldl_count` per kernel, the card schema shows spill bytes only. Keep every unrolled loop under about 500 iterations and every array index a compile-time constant through template recursion.
- Composite comparators (T35). A key-then-index comparator written with `||` and `&&` inside a predicated network becomes a branch region per exchange (3448 instructions with 300 `BSSY` for the fp32 N 128 k 32 kernel), 4.4x to 7.3x the cycles of the single-key reference. Written with `|` and `&` it stays predicated and runs 3.5x to 6.1x faster. This applies to `cub::detail::WarpBitonicTopK`, `WarpBitonicSort` and `WarpMergeSort` with user comparators over structs.
- Sub-warp collectives (T37, duplicated as T40). `redux.sync` on a per-group member mask is lowered to a `WARPSYNC` loop over the distinct masks, one pass per logical warp: 174, 327 and 649 cycles per round at 2, 4 and 8 logical warps per hardware warp against 25 at full width. One full-warp redux per group with the identity in the other lanes and a select costs 34 to 46 at 2 and 4 groups and 88 at 8, whole cells 4x to 7x faster. The same lowering reaches `cub::WarpReduce` at a logical width below 32 on nvcc 13.4 and should be checked there (BSSY and WARPSYNC around a CREDUX in the SASS). Ballot and shuffle with per-group masks were not measured.
- List pruning, a positive (T38, duplicated as T41). With fully unrolled rounds, a compile-time k and shifts written as selects, the compiler drops the shifts of list positions that cannot reach the head, so a per-lane sorted list of depth min(k, items per lane) costs the 8-item round at any N as long as k is at most 16 (W2 at N 1024 k 8 from 32 items per lane: 1061 cycles at 41 registers, where T19's full-list round predicted 89 per round). A rolled round loop or a branchy shift loses it.
- Candidate buffers in shared memory (T31). A blocked candidate run of a power-of-two stride per thread loads with up to 32-way bank conflicts, 1.33x to 1.34x at N 32768 for B3. Pad the stride by one word.
- Per-slot aggregated tickets (T22, R16). One ballot per tile slot and warp, one atomic with return by lane 0 and a popc rank cost about 150 cycles per slot in a dependent chain, 24 to 73 percent slower than one atomic per selected item and 3.6x at IPT 128. The `if (ballot != 0)` guard serializes the slots.
- Harness self-test defect. Harness 0.3 and earlier recorded a false negative of the oracle self-test at k equal to N over 2, because the self-test's own signed-zero input then has 2k equal to N zeros and no non-top element for its "wrong set" case. The per-cell oracle was intact. Cells with the flag (W1_packed, N2_level, W2_packed at k equal to N over 2, B3 at N 2048 k 1024) keep their numbers with a note, harness 0.4 fixes the self-test.
- Node-to-node variance of large unrolled kernels. Identical binaries, identical controls, identical canaries: the 128-thread B2 kernel at IPT 32 and k 64 (5008 to 6552 SASS instructions, one warp per scheduler) ran 25 and 33 percent slower on umbriel-b200-020 than on umbriel-b200-047 (7831 against 6254 and 8267 against 6219 cycles), while its 256-thread siblings and every k 4 and k 16 cell agreed to 0.1 percent (card `B2_packed_b200_20261025_3`, two cells refuted). W2_packed binaries at k 32 to 64 with 32 to 64 items per lane ran 10 to 25 percent slower on umbriel-b200-091 than on 047 (bf16 N 2048 k 64 11011 against 9884), every cell up to k 16 to the cycle. The B1 reference control was 3.3 to 4.6 percent slower on 020 at 122 to 255 registers. Third sighting: the B5 k 64 kernels run 8 percent slower on umbriel-b200-020 than on 091 with the B2 control identical to the cycle (cards `B5_hier_b200_20261025_1` and `_2`, both verified), and the `W2_packed_sw_v2` k 64 cells at N 128 are 8 to 13 percent slower on 091 than on 047 with the masked control within 1.1 percent. Fourth sighting: the fully unrolled k 64 two-level B2 kernels read 3.3 to 6.5 percent below their card on umbriel-b200-068 and 3 to 4 percent above it on 020 with identical SASS, registers and barrier counts, about plus or minus 5 percent across B200 nodes with no sign change, while the k 4 cells and every `B2_packed` control agree within 1.5 percent (verify-B2A). Under investigation by the hardware researcher (reported through the lead on 2026-10-25, probes under `$SWARM/state/results/hw-researcher/icache/`, no catalogue entry yet): the two hosts are identical in SKU, VBIOS, clocks, ECC and driver, the per-call cost on 020 is a steady 1.24x of 047 at every chain length while the generate-only control is identical, so the remaining candidate is per-SM memory latency on the per-call path, since the data-dependent latency kernel loads fresh input per call. The instruction fetch hypothesis of the first sighting is not confirmed. Rule until it is settled: cross-node comparisons of long dependent chains are not safe at the 3 percent level, a card's absolute latency at large k and large IPT holds for its node only, the final evaluation runs on one node in one session, and B200 nodes are not interchangeable for cycle-level latency of such kernels. Closed as T50 and T58: a stable property of umbriel-b200-020 on 128-thread kernels above about 50 KB of SASS (1.02x to 2.04x, the N8 two-node card verified on a third node, every synthetic identical on 148 SMs of four nodes, hosts identical in every inventory item), three other B200 nodes agree with each other. The decision (020 retired and excluded from the final evaluation, node caveats on the cards measured there, ratios inside one lock section valid) and the node rules are stated once, in 10.6. T58 was updated after the phase 3 pre-flights with the GB200 finding of 2.12 (the same 128-thread k 64 configuration is 3 to 15 percent slower on both GB200 hosts than on the healthy B200s, a generation property, not a node).
- Harness chain-16 artifact at large N (harness 0.6, 2026-10-25 17:05 UTC, found by verify-B2A). Before 0.6 every chain length cycled through the same 16 fresh inputs, so a chain of 16 left L1 where a chain of 8 stayed resident and the chain-16 point of the latency fit lifted off the line at N 8192 and above. 0.6 rotates the inputs per repetition and launches the generate-only control with the variant's dynamic shared memory size. The B1 reference re-measured under 0.6 on umbriel-b200-020 moves under 1 percent at N 1024, 7 percent at N 4096 bf16 and 2 to 6 percent at N 16384 and 32768 (card `B1_reference_b200_20261025_1`, now the 0.6 card). Ratios inside a lock section are unaffected because variant and control shared the artifact, absolute large-N latencies of every card measured under harness 0.3 to 0.5 (all wave 1 block cards, the N8 cards at N 8192 and above) carry it, and the final evaluation under 0.6 on one node removes it. verify-N8's scope statement: harness 0.4 and 0.5 latency numbers at fp32 N 4096 and above or bf16 N 8192 and above carry the bias, it does not touch card against rerun comparisons or ratio directions, and absolute large-N cycle numbers should be quoted from 0.6 measurements. In this card's reruns 14 to 16 of 24 to 40 large-N latency cells moved by more than 3 percent under a 1 to 8 fit (bf16 128 threads N 8192 k 4 795 instead of 988). Harness 0.9 adds per-regime graceful failure for launches that do not fit, and 0.10 (commit 91fac66081, frozen for phase 3) streams every harness load past L1 in both regimes with deterministic strided input subsets per chain length and no rotation, which removes the chain-length dependence of the generate-only control that verify-B4 found under 0.6 to 0.8 (the control is now flat across chain lengths, 719 to 670 cycles per call at chain 1 to 16 for a striped bf16 N 4096 kernel on a GB200, fits R squared at or above 0.9996, and the striped B4_v4 at bf16 N 4096 reads its 0.4 value again, 3808 against 3818, not the 3461 of 0.8). Against 0.6 the B1_reference nets move by under 1 percent at N 1024, by minus 3.5 percent (bf16) and minus 2 percent (fp32) at N 4096 and by plus 12 percent (bf16) and plus 3 percent (fp32) at N 16384, where the variant's loads had partly hit L1 and now stream like the control's, and the throughput ceilings drop to the L2 streaming rate (about 4 to 4.6 Telem/s at N 1024, 1.2 Telem/s for fp32 at N 16384), which is what a kernel sees for a tile it loads once. `harness/README.md` section 3.1 tabulates the version effects 0.2 to 0.10 for verifiers: a card is reproduced to the cycle with the harness version it was made with and compared with the current one.
- Threshold logic on twiddled keys must treat signed zeros explicitly, and the old battery did not catch it (verify-W5, card `W5_v3` refuted on contract, numbers intact). W5_v3's flag was the sign bit of a minus t on raw float keys while the threshold t was read back from W2's twiddle, which maps minus zero onto plus zero, so an item equal to minus zero was dropped when the threshold was plus zero although it ties the threshold under the float order the contract promises. The battery's single `signed_zero` seed under `not_guaranteed` holds exactly k plus zeros, a valid set, so it passed. With 32 signed-zero seeds under `prefer_smaller_index` all 12 cells fail the exact index check, v2 and v4 pass all 42 cases, and a host emulation shows the threshold comes out as plus zero in 3 to 41 of 64 seeds per cell. v4's `x + 0.0f` at load fixes it as a side effect. Harness 0.7 (commit 2e1a3ebbe2) strengthens the battery: five `signed_zero` seeds varying the share of plus zeros among the 2k zeros (so fewer than k plus zeros fails under `not_guaranteed` too), `signed_zero_boundary` with the k-th rank inside mixed-sign zeros, `pivot_tie_zero`, 23 battery cases per cell, and a batch must hold both requirements to cover the set check and the exact check. The lead asked for a rerun on every adapter with threshold or tie logic on fp32. Pass list (B200 umbriel-b200-018, both key types, both requirements, the focus grid, 23 cases per cell with the five `signed_zero` seeds, `signed_zero_boundary` and `pivot_tie_zero`): `W5_v4` 36 of 36 supported cells, `W2_packed_v2` 76 of 76, `W2_packed_v3` 76 of 76, `B2_tiecheck` 56 of 56, `B1_reference` 42 of 42, `W1_reference` 38 of 38, zero oracle failures anywhere, `N10_auto` rerunning after a missing include (verify-N10 already ran the 0.8 battery on its headline cells with all passing), and the final adapters of N6w, W3 and N1 passed the 0.8 battery on all 120 headline cells (section 22).
- A cold network under a warp-uniform branch inside an unrolled round loop is replicated per iteration (T48). N7's refill network under `if (__any_sync(need))` after every block of rounds was inlined once per block: 3737 `VIMNMX` against 382 for W2 in the throughput kernel, 11368 instructions (about 180 KB) in the latency kernel, and the round cost 134 to 185 cycles (bf16 N 1024 k 64 9903 against W2's 7308). Rolling the block loop (`#pragma unroll 1`) around the branch brought the kernel to 1000 instructions and the cell to 7182. Invisible in `-Xptxas -v` (no spills, no stack), visible as `sass_instructions` several times the control's with the same opcode mix per round. A plain W2 list must stay unrolled for the T38 pruning, a refilled head loses nothing by rolling.
- A lookahead refill test fires after one win unless the head holds four blocks of rounds (T49). With a head of 8 and blocks of 4 rounds the test "fewer than 2 B valid head words" is true for any lane that has won once, so the refill ran in nearly every block and the per-round cost scaled with the tile (70, 106, 190 cycles at IPT 16, 32, 64). Blocks of 2 made the refill rare and left the block control at about 18 cycles per round (45 to 74 against 27 for W2's pruned list). Size the head at least four times the block of rounds between votes, or avoid per-block control altogether (W5).
- Predicate pressure, not instruction count, set the cost of W5's filter phases. Every per-item float compare produces a predicate, there are seven predicate registers, and ptxas serializes compare and consume per item with `P2R` moves: compare and count cost 314 cycles for 32 items per lane (about 10 cycles per item for 3 to 5 instructions). Sign-bit flags (`a - t` as an integer, the sign is the flag), an integer count tree and unconditional stores to a per-lane dummy slot cut the phase to 98 cycles, and one `FADD` (`x + 0.0f`) at load replaced the compare and select per gathered word of the minus-zero normalization plus the position predicate and took 13 to 15 percent off every bf16 cell (W5 report section 3, phase probes `approaches/W5/phase_probe.cu` and `phase_probe3.cu`, cards `W5_v3`, whose numbers verify-W5 reproduced to the cycle while refuting its contract, see the signed-zero pitfall, and `W5_v4`, verified). Removing 300 instructions per call from the same filter (v2) moved nothing: on one warp the filter is latency bound.
- A shared store instruction costs about 10 cycles on one warp whether or not its lanes are active. W5's scatter costs 184 to 191 cycles for 16 stores, 332 to 352 for 32, 611 to 668 for 64 at every N, predicated (v2) or unconditional (v3), consistent with T12's four stores plus four loads at 82. The store count is the unit of cost of a compaction, so the levers are two candidates per 64-bit store or a prefix-free private-run gather (18.5).
- Independent ballots do not reach the pipelined rate when their consumers are interleaved. N10's two-class recovery issues two ballots per slot with popc and add chains between them and ptxas interleaves the second ballot of a slot with the earlier slots' chains, so the pass costs about 48 cycles per slot from IPT 4 on against the 6 cycles per further ballot of T17 (card `N10_u32`, SASS in T52). The one-ballot form issues its eight ballots back to back and matches the model. Check the SASS for `VOTE` instructions issued consecutively, and try an asm barrier between the ballot block and the prefix block (19.5, untested).
- The packed bf16x2 exchange has one good spelling (T53, opt-N5, static SASS only). `k = keep ? hmax2(k, o) : hmin2(k, o)` with the SIMD calls inside the ternary compiles to two predicated `HMNMX2` writing one register (144 instructions for a 32-stage chain against 128 for the u32 route), computing both results and selecting costs a third instruction per stage (152, the header `__hmax2` and `__hmin2` give the same), a predicate inside one asm block is worst (424), and no operand negation is folded. At sub-warp instantiations the ternary form can come out as four instructions per stage word (two predicated `HMNMX2` into different registers plus two moves), where the explicit select form is the better of the two. Re-read the SASS after every structural change (T28).
- Block size is a measured dimension, not a constant (N8, section 17). The same B2 source at 64, 128 and 256 threads differs by 1.2x in latency (1422, 1182, 1380 cycles at bf16 N 1024 k 16) and by 1.66x in one-wave throughput in the opposite direction, and the same B1_idx source at 512 against 256 threads gains 1.24x to 1.45x at N 32768. A card measured at one block size says nothing about the primitive's best shape, and the latency and throughput regimes disagree on it (T44).
- A uniform branch per register slot serializes the slot bodies (T56, B4). `for j in slots: if (j < n) body(j)` with a run-time n over an unrolled register array ran 16 dependent chains of about 60 cycles back to back in B4's base finish, about 240 cycles per slot whether the slot was live or not, and the SASS instruction count did not predict it. Guard groups of 4 to 8 slots with one uniform branch (or predicate every slot when the body is short), hoist per-slot invariants into registers when the slots are few, and profile with clock64 stamps between phases behind a macro before restructuring: the harness measures whole calls.
- The T39 question is closed by a same-kernel A/B (T57, N9): a constant-address shared `atomicAdd` with return from an unrolled item loop costs 48 to 1070 cycles per call more than one atomic on a class-indexed counter the compiler cannot prove uniform (B1_idx's spelling), so T23's attribution of about 80 cycles per aggregated ticket site stands, the empty-asm fix of T23 is a no-op (T39), and the class index is the working form. For fire-and-forget increments the aggregation is wanted (T26, T27), the cost is specific to tickets whose return feeds a store. T32's dummy-word proposal follows the same line: branch-free pass atomics saved B4 200 to 270 cycles, branch-free tickets with return cost 170 to 270 more (sixteen `atomicAdd` with return per thread, ten to dummy words, the return on the critical path).
- Do not sort to select, and do not read a wavefront-bound kernel off its instruction count (T54, B6). A stock `cub::BlockRadixSort` of the packed word is 2.1x to 2.35x slower than B3_tuned for bf16 and 5.0x to 6.5x for fp32 at every k from N over 8 to N over 2 and every N 1024 to 8192, in a kernel of 1008 instructions with a rolled pass loop: the cost is 4 to 8 ranking passes each with a counter chain through aliasing shared pointers, a random-rank scatter with 3 to 4 way bank conflicts and a full exchange, 740 + 82 x IPT cycles per pass. The idea card had half the pass count and a quarter of the pass cost (one block scan instead of a ranking pass).
- Never-executed code still costs (T28, N5). The fast recovery of N5 compiles different fallbacks for the two requirements, neither runs on random input, and the `prefer_smaller_index` kernel carries 3 to 16 more registers and 2 to 4 percent lower throughput (up to 9 percent at LW 8 N 128, plus 8 percent latency at N 256). The exact form, one kernel for both, measures identical to 0.1 percent.
- The B200 reference moved with the harness: `B1_reference_b200_20261025_1` was re-measured a second time under harness 0.8 on umbriel-b200-068 (commit 40ccd445ae, after the 020 retirement), throughput identical to the 020 run, latency within 3 percent in 32 of 42 cells and 3 to 5 percent lower in ten large-N cells, consistent with T58. Section 16 quotes the 068 values for the reference cells marked u.
- A value bracket whose width overflows gives a scale of plus zero, and inf times zero puts infinite keys into the wrong bin (T60, N6w). With lo clamped to minus FLT_MAX and hi to FLT_MAX the width is inf and the scale 32 over inf is plus zero, `fmaf(d, 0, 2^23)` with d infinite is NaN, whose bit pattern clamps to the top bin, so minus infinity lands with plus infinity and the bin map stops being monotone (inf_denorm failed on four k 1024 cells, duplicate index, two minus-inf items counted above the pivot and never emitted). A first fix that tested the width and the scale against FLT_MAX passed both. Guard the scale, not the width (`scale = (scale > 0 && scale < FLT_MAX) ? scale : FLT_MIN`, with FTZ on a normal value such as 2^-100), and keep a host model of the exact device arithmetic on the oracle battery next to any bracket code (`approaches/N6w/model_device.py` found it in seconds). T60 names the block N6, B4 and N9 brackets as in scope.
- Three N6w bugs were caught by the oracle battery and none by random input: lanes beyond 2^d buckets double counting in a 4-bit digit pass (an infinite loop), items above the range dropped by the W3 compaction, and the degenerate bracket above. Random input is the weakest test of a selection primitive.
- Chunked ballot issue is a no-op (N6w_v2 against v1, T28): the scheduler already interleaves independent ballots every five to six instructions, hand-chunked issue arrays only cost registers (15 to 30 percent slower at bf16 N 2048). What pays against a ballot histogram is a different instruction, the shared atomic (T59), not a different schedule.
- The register wall at IPT 128 in a float-tile warp primitive (T36 class): every N6w variant at N 4096 holds 255 registers, a 4 to 5 KB stack frame, 600 to 800 bytes of spill, about 400 `STL` and 700 `LDL`, and runs 60 to 80 thousand cycles against W2's 6.5 to 48 thousand, a local-memory artifact of the float tile plus the per-item bins plus the finish arrays, not the algorithm. At IPT 64 the schedule of a full register file decides between variants in both directions.
- A merge loop whose body is a complete network over a register array must stay rolled (T62, W4). The fully unrolled four chunks per level made 20k to 40k instruction kernels (about 620 KB of code for two copies) that ran 1.6x to 2.7x slower at one warp with the same instruction mix, and the unroller kept several chunks' arrays live (a 200-byte stack frame with 53 `STL` at 255 registers). When the iterations are a dependent chain anyway (the next chunk's addresses need this chunk's win count) unrolling buys no overlap, so `#pragma unroll 1` on the chunk loop with the body indexed by compile-time constants, read `sass_instructions` in the batch JSON before reading cycles, and replace serial flag sums on the critical path by a tree. The same mechanism as T48 (a replicated cold refill), here on the hot path.
- At one warp the address arithmetic of a shared memory swizzle costs more than the bank conflicts it removes (W4 v4): an xor swizzle is two extra instructions per access and exact-count loads a predicate each, linear addressing with an odd run stride and unconditional loads into a zero pad after every run cut the warp merge by 1.1x to 1.7x and halved the registers. The block form keeps the swizzle, where 8 warps share the pipe and the lanes of a pair stride through one run. A swizzle on the run-relative position (v1) put the 16 lane pairs of a level on the same banks, swizzle on the absolute buffer position.  (card `W4_wmerge`, 23.4).
- A 4-ary merge path loses at block scope at a range of 2048 as the k-ary search lost at warp scope in July (W4_chunk4q, 0.84x and 0.89x of the binary search): three probes per side per step through a shared memory pipe that 8 warps share, plus the select cascade and three address computations, cost more than the six saved dependent steps.  (card `W4_chunk4q`, 23.4).
- Inverting a bucket image to a value threshold: validate the edge with the image itself and step it by the magnitude of the base, not by ulps of the edge (T63, N3). The image is `fmaf(v - lo, scale, 2^23)` and the subtraction rounds to the ulp of the difference (4.8e-7 at a difference of 4.3), which is 8 ulps of an edge near 0.65, so stepping the edge by `nextafterf` leaves the bin unchanged, the validation fails, the threshold falls back to the bracket minimum and the buffer takes 12384 instead of 11148 entries (threshold minus 0.033 where the exact k-th largest was 0.655). Step by max(|t|, |lo|) times 2^-22, quadrupling per retry, with the bracket minimum as the fallback, and guard the degenerate scales first (T60). The host model of the device arithmetic (`approaches/N3/model.py`) found it before any GPU time, as `model_device.py` did for T60.
- Never leave a sweep over a shared buffer rolled when the entries per thread are in the tens (T64, N3): the finish's two rolled sweeps cost 184 to 319 cycles per iteration and were half the call, 12 to 20 times the per-slot cost of an unrolled register sweep, written rolled to spare registers next to a tile that is dead by the time the finish runs. Unroll by 8 with the loads first. And cut the number of data-dependent shared atomics rather than the branch around them: one per register slot costs about 100 cycles per slot at two warps per scheduler whether guarded or sent to a dummy word (N3 issues 128 atomics per thread where 45 over the compacted candidates would do), T32's branch-free form does not make a per-slot histogram cheap.
- A control measured under an older harness version is not comparable to a variant under a newer one when the harness changed what the no-op control does (verify-B4). Since harness 0.6 the no-op control launches with the variant's dynamic shared memory size, so a shared-memory-heavy control's net latency moved: B3_tuned reads 8.7 to 11.4 percent lower at fp32 N 16384 and 32768 under 0.8 than in the 0.4-era cards while B1_idx (2.1 KB of shared memory) is unchanged, and three B4 cards were refuted on the control alone (B4's margin 2.60x instead of 2.87x at fp32 N 32768 k 256, every conclusion intact). The 0.8 harness wrapper also compiles the bf16 N 4096 B4 latency kernels to 119 instead of 80 registers with a striped-load no-op slope of 654 instead of 254 cycles (a 0.4 build on the same node reproduces the cards to 0.1 percent). Variant and control must come from one harness version and one session, which the final evaluation guarantees, and a ratio quoted across harness versions carries the version of each side. The ruling that follows from it (a card is judged under its own harness version, current-version ratios recorded next to the card's) is stated once, in section 9, and applied to the three B4 cards in 21.
- A sorted-output contract needs a sorted-output check, and the harness had none until verify-W4 (harness gap closed in 0.10). Through 0.8 the oracle checked the selected set and the exact index set but not the order of the k slots, so a primitive claiming sorted output was taken at its word. The verifier added an exact key-descending index-ascending check over all k slots and ran it oracle-only over the 22-case battery: every W4 variant and B6_radix pass in every cell, and B3_tuned (unsorted by contract) fails 20 of 44 cases as the negative control, so the check discriminates. It is the `--check-sorted` flag of harness 0.10 and phase 3b runs under it. The sorted-runs input of `--runs M` was also checked to be a genuine merge input (runs non-increasing, ascents only at the run boundaries, the whole row never sorted).
- Under a tight register target ptxas recomputes the CTA shared window base in front of every shared atomic or store reached through a generic pointer (T66, B1_det3). At the 48-register target the harness latency kernel carried 37 `S2UR SR_CgaCtaId`, `UMOV`, `ULEA`, `UIADD3` sequences, one per ticket site, where det2 at 64 registers kept the base in one uniform register, 300 to 1100 cycles per call and a wrong A/B (the first det3 batch read 2 to 8 percent slower than det2 while the one-block profile had it 700 cycles ahead). Taking the shared address once did not help (the conversion itself was rematerialized), pinning the 32-bit address through `asm volatile("" : "+r"(addr))` and issuing `atom.shared.add.u32` on it brought the count to 6. Check `grep -c SR_CgaCtaId` per kernel, a handful is normal, one per site is the pitfall, and check the control too, since an A/B between variants on different register targets measures the pitfall and not the variant.
- `__ffsll` is a ten-instruction emulation on sm_100 (negate, mask, `FLO`, two subtracts, select), `__ffs` on a 32-bit word is three (T65): loop over 32-bit halves of a 64-bit mask. Two scan rounds that run back to back (round 2's loads after round 1's base store) interleave for free when written side by side (B1_det3's tie scan 947 to 694 cycles at IPT 64).
- Card provenance. Three wave 1 cards were built from uncommitted or mislabeled sources and the verifiers caught them: `B1_n6_b200_20261025_1` measured an intermediate between two commits (refuted and superseded), all seven B1 cards recorded the harness clone commit instead of the adapter commit (fixed, `make_card.py` gained `--source-commit`), the W2 slope batch ran from an uncommitted source and its card names one node for cells measured on another. Verification by rebuild from the named commit is what makes a number citable, and code drift during a session can change a verdict: B3's commit 50039daee3 restructured its pass loop and moved the fp32 fallback cells by 7 to 10 percent, so the `B3_straight` finding holds for its own commit only.
- Sorted output is a contract to check, not a card claim (phase 3b, 16). W5_v4's fp32 finish word replaces the low 7 to 9 twiddled key bits by the inverted buffer position and its tie rewrite covers only the class that straddles the k-th slot, so equal keys inside the top k come out in buffer-position order and the sorted check refutes its fp32 prefer_smaller_index output on the quantized battery rows (six cells, k 16 and 64 at N 512 to 4096), with a latent ordering hole under not_guaranteed when two distinct keys agree in the kept bits. The set is exact and the unsorted winners stand. The fix is the tie rewrite generalized to every class run inside the top k, in the emission. N5's forms are unsorted by contract (greater class in lane order) and were never sorted routes.
- An oracle kernel that cannot launch reads as a failure (phase 3b). B6_radix at fp32 N 32768 needs 270 KB of dynamic shared memory for the 64-bit sort against the 227 KB opt-in limit, so the oracle kernel never ran and the harness counted oracle_failed where an unlaunchable regime counts as unsupported. Classify as unsupported, reject the shape in the adapter, and make the two paths read the same (TK-P13).

### 2.11 Predictions before wave 1, with the scorecard

Written before wave 1 and kept as the record the cards were checked against. The measured findings are in 2.12. Scorecard after wave 1 (details in sections 11 to 16): the packed key-index word held (W2 round 24.9 to 29.6 cycles inside the primitive, B2 round 83.5, both exact and tie-break free), W2 owns more of the warp than predicted (every verified cell up to k 64 from N 128 on, against the predicted hand-over to W5 and N1 at k 32), B2 against B1 crossed at k 22, 34, 36 to 40 and 45 to 50 for N 1024 to 16384 (predicted 18 at N 1024 and about 40 at N 8192), N6 is real at fp32 N 4096 and loses throughput through registers, the N 1024 k 16 band went to B2 (1380) with B5 at 1194 but closed on throughput (R13), N2 was killed on identical code (R12) while a differently written level network beat the CUB network by 1.5x to 2.5x (attributed by exp-W1codegen to the exchange form, the code structure and the tile order, 11.4 and 11.7), and the arch prediction held except for the aarch64 toolchain effect (T20). Original text follows.

This subsection was the placeholder for the cross-approach findings the result cards would produce (when a threshold filter pays, when packing the index into the key pays, where k over N flips the winner to sorting). Until then it records the algorithm researcher's predictions from `$SWARM/ideas/PRIORITIES.md`, revised 2026-10-25 with the measured intrinsic costs of 2.9 while sort stages, AIR phases and rounds are still July 2026 placeholders at one design point. Every item below is a prediction awaiting a card.

- Prediction, the packed key-index word, with its premise now measured. With a unique word (key in the high half, inverted index in the low half) the W2 round needs no ballot, no ffs and no elect, the winner lane recognizes itself by comparing its head with the redux result. The microbenchmark measured that round with a per-lane sorted list at 26 cycles up to 8 items per lane, 37 at 16 and 89 at 32 (T19, both archs), against 74.5 for a block atomic round. On the model this makes W2 the owner of every router cell (N 256 to 1024, k 4 to 32) and revives B5 (two W2 phases of k rounds at IPT 8, about 52 k, against B2's 74 k before the merge). Still a prediction until the W2 card measures whole calls in the harness, including the list fill and sort that the round excludes, and that kill experiment decides W2, B5 and N4's host at once.
- Prediction, warp flip bands (bf16, fp32 the same with N4). N 128 to 512: W2 up to k 32, then W5 (k 64 at N 512 and above) or N1 (k 64 to 128 at N 128 to 256), the W2 to N1 crossover near k 39 at N 256. N 1024: W2 up to k 32, W5 at k 32 to 64, W3 from k 128 (bf16) or k 256 (fp32). N 2048 to 4096: W5 from k 16, W3 from k 128 to 256, W4 only near k N over 2. N at most 64: W2 at k 8 and below, N2 or W1 at k 16 to 32 only if the W2 round measures above about 45 cycles.
- Prediction, block flip bands. N 1024 bf16 k 16 is the densest band of the study (B5 about 1300, N6 1500, B2 1640, B1 1700 on the model), k 32 and above goes to B1 or N6, B2 against B1 crosses near k 18 and B5 against B1 near k 26. N 4096: k 16 B5 or B2, k 32 to 256 N6 or B1, the B4 bound starts to pay at k 64. N 8192 and above: B4 on random input, B3 on sorted input or a loose bound, N6 as the one-pass robust option, B1 as the fallback everyone pays on tie floods. k at least N over 4 (chunk merges, H2O, SnapKV): B1 unless the inputs are sorted runs, then W4 or B6's multiway merge.
- Prediction, requirement. `prefer_smaller_index` is free for the packed W2, B2 and N4 and for slot-major emission in N1 and W3, about one compare per stage for W1 and N2 (free with the bf16 index-in-key word), about 1 percent for B1 (measured in 4.5). No flips expected. Scored 2026-10-25 19:00 UTC (B1_det2 and B1_det3, verified): the about 1 percent for B1 holds for fp32 at N 1024 and 4096 once the ticket is spelled on a class-indexed counter (minus 0.7 to plus 1.0 percent), and does not hold for bf16 (5 to 53 percent, the per-slot tie phase on the 28 to 91 percent of rows with excess ties) or at IPT 64 (two registers per slot). The wave 1 figure of 19 to 51 percent was the ticket spelling plus the tie phase.
- Prediction, regime. B2 at 19 registers and B1 at 32 registers both reach 8 blocks per SM, so the regime should not flip the block winner. At warp scope the data-oblivious networks (W1, N2, N5) have no shared memory and win throughput at small N even where W2 wins latency, the pattern of the July sort studies.
- Measured already, arch. The B2 round (T11), redux, ballot and shuffle costs agree to the cycle between B200 and GB200 (T16), and the reference cards agree within 1 percent in cycles on every B1 cell (10.4), so no arch flip is expected inside the focus set. B300 and Rubin are not covered.
- Measured already, key width (reference cards, 10.4). bf16 is not cheaper than fp32 for the radix block primitive at equal N and k, and it is slower than fp32 in every cell of the scalar warp bitonic network. A 16-bit key pays off through packing (two keys per lane, or the index inside the key word) or through occupancy, not through the compare itself.
- Structural fact, not a prediction. bf16 keys are tie-heavy by construction (10.2), which weakens early exit for radix and bit-serial methods (W3, N1, B1) and makes the free tie-break of the packed key-plus-inverted-index word a real advantage on this data.
- Model corrections forced by the phase 0 measurements. Ballots pipeline at 6 not 4 cycles (N1 moved from the k 32 band to k 64 to 128, W3 to k 128 and above). `redux.sync` f32 is sm_100f only (fp32 extraction goes through N4 or a 40-cycle twiddled round). `match.any` and `fns` are rejected on the cost model (R6, N1 emission). bf16x2 min, max and masks are 6-cycle single instructions (N5's model holds). B2's GB200 risk is retired.

Wave 2 scorecard so far (2026-10-25 17:05 UTC, from the cards of opt-N8, opt-W5 and opt-N10, all verified by 17:50 UTC the same day except `W5_v3`, refuted on contract, sections 17 to 19). The W5 prediction held on its band and failed at N 512: W5 takes bf16 from k 16 at N 1024 and 2048 and fp32 from k 32 at N 1024 and k 16 at N 2048 (predicted k 32 to 64 at N 512 to 1024 and k 16 from N 2048), and loses every N 512 latency cell to W2's pruned list. N7's prediction (2370 against 3300 at bf16 N 1024 k 64) failed at 4623, two builds behind it were codegen (T48) and refill policy (T49), and the block control of a two-level warp list is the structural reason. N8's prediction of a 58-cycle round and 1060 cycles for a 2-warp B2 block failed (77 and 1422): the barrier is the only per-warp term and the list shift costs it back (T43, T46). N8's other half held and was the larger finding: the radix incumbent wanted 512 threads at N 16384 and 32768 (1.24x to 1.45x, more than any wave 1 B1 variant gained), and the one-wave throughput regime inverts the latency verdict on block size (T44, T45). N10's model held: bf16 network cost plus a recovery, modeled at about 200 cycles at N 128 against 1131 for the u64 word, measured 960 against 1918 with a 221-cycle recovery at IPT 4, and the wave 1 statement that `prefer_smaller_index` costs fp32 W1 13 to 30 percent is now reversed, the exact route beats the best `not_guaranteed` route in every warp cell. Added 18:30 UTC (opt-N5's cards verified by verify-N5 at 18:25 UTC, opt-B4's and opt-B6's unverified): the B4 prediction held ("the winner at N 8192 and above on random input"): B4_v4 is 2.4x bf16 and 2.9x fp32 under B3_tuned at N 32768 k 256 (2.26x and 2.60x under harness 0.8, verified on two nodes), 2.1x to 2.2x at N 16384 k 64, 1.35x to 1.42x at N 8192 k 64, a tie at N 4096 and a loss at N 1024 and at k 2048, so the band starts at N 8192 as predicted and ends at k 1024. The B6 prediction failed by 2.1x to 6.5x (R17): the model had half the pass count and a quarter of the pass cost. N5's "up to 2x per row where networks win" came out as 1.22x to 1.44x at k at most 32 and 1.43x to 1.82x at k 64 at warp scope (T47's shared pipe), and its "no latency gain" was wrong at sub-warp scope (1.14x to 2.06x per call from N 64). N9 failed its k 16 kill cells (N6b already exits after one pass there) and survives as N6's finish from k 64 and under `prefer_smaller_index`. The wave 1 prediction that B1's requirement price is about 1 percent is now settled by T57: the 19 to 51 percent of `B1_det` is the constant-address ticket, and the class-indexed spelling removes it. Added 18:50 UTC (opt-N6w's seven cards, GB200, verified on B200 to the cycle by verify-N6w): the warp prediction of 2.11 had W5 at k 32 to 64 and N1 or W3 above, and N6 at warp scope was not in the warp table at all. Measured, N6 with a shared atomic histogram over the exact bracket takes k 64 to 1024 at N 256 to 2048 from W2 (bf16 N 512 k 64 1879 against 2704, N 2048 k 1024 17741 against 152618), W3 is a fallback with no cell of its own on random input below k over N of one half (1.5x to 2.1x behind N6w where one bracket pass suffices, ahead only at k 1024 and the spilling N 4096 k 64), and N1 has no warp cell of its own (the d 1 row as predicted, ties N 128 k 64, loses k 16 by 4x). The W3 band "N 1024 and above with k 128 and above" and the N1 band "N 128 to 256 with k 64 to 128" both went to N6w. Added 19:10 UTC (opt-W4's five cards, verified by verify-W4 within 0.6 percent with the sorted order checked): the W4 prediction ("loses everywhere except the sorted-input multiway chunk merge and k near N over 2") held and then some. The chunk merge of 4 sorted runs of 2048 is 1.52x (bf16) and 1.08x (fp32) under B3_tuned's unsorted selection over the same candidates and 3.2x and 5.0x under B6's full sort, the cheapest sorted route at every merge cell, and the warp truncated merge sort passed its 2x bar at 7.6x and 3.6x over W2 at N 2048 k 1024 and takes every k 256 sorted cell, losing to W5 at k 64 as predicted. The B6 verdict (R17) stands and the sorted-output pass of phase 3b now has three measured routes (section 16). Added 19:20 UTC (opt-N3, one card, unverified): the N3 card ("streaming contract only", bar 16k at fp32 N 32768 k 2048, 1.3x under B3_tuned) failed by 2x (43.2k against 20.8k), as the resident-selection comparison predicted: nothing in the streaming structure is cheaper than its resident equivalent, the saving of the k-sized merges is real but the per-chunk atomics and scans and the two rolled finish sweeps cost more. Retired after one cell, the contract note is in 10.5 and 2.12.

### 2.12 Algorithmic findings that hold across approaches (measured, wave 1)

B200, nvcc 13.4.92, `-arch=sm_100`, 2026-10-25, latency in cycles per call net of the generate-only control, every number with its control in one lock section, card names in sections 11 to 15 where verification status, node and file are given per number.

- The packed key-plus-inverted-index word is the single most valuable idea of wave 1. One 32-bit word orders exactly like (key descending, index ascending), so every extraction round is an exact arg max with no ballot, ffs or election, and `prefer_smaller_index` costs nothing. Warp scope: the W2 round is 24.9 to 29.6 cycles inside the primitive, W2 beats the warp bitonic reference in every bf16 warp cell up to k 256 (6.3x at N 32 k 4, 14x at N 512 k 16, 29x at N 2048 k 16). W1 with the bf16 word through the same network is 1.36x to 2.5x faster than the pairs network in latency and 2.0x to 2.7x in throughput. Block scope: B2's round is 83.5 cycles and B2 beats B1 at k 4 to 16 at every N from 1024 to 16384 (1.36x to 5.1x). fp32 goes through the truncated-key word of N4 (top 32 minus log2 N key bits plus the index, exact tie finish): 20 to 38 percent fewer cycles than value rounds with an index epilogue at block scope, and within 62 to 98 cycles of bf16 at 8 to 16 items per lane at warp scope with the next-word tie check, with the finish running on 0.1 to 4 percent of random inputs below N 16384 (host simulation, 2000 seeds) and 20 to 50 percent at N 32768 with 17 key bits.
- On a packed unsigned word, write the network's compare-exchange as min and max (T42): two predicated `VIMNMX` per stage against two `ISETP` and a `SEL`, 1.15x to 1.6x latency and 1.10x to 1.24x throughput on the same network and loop (N 256 k 64 bf16: 1322 against 2104 cycles), host independent, 32-bit words only. A bool comparator object hides this form from the compiler, so the CUB network needs a min max customization point to get it. Together with the recursion's code structure (1.03x to 1.41x over the CUB header for the same algorithm) the eager loop with this exchange is 1.18x to 2.24x faster than `WarpBitonicTopK` on B200 (card `W1x_codegen_b200_20261025_1`, verified by verify-W1x to the cycle on a second B200 host and a second GB200 host, 11.4). This is direct measured evidence for issue #12160.
- The block round has a floor. At 256 threads it is the ptxas-aggregated post (one redux plus one elected atomic per warp), the barrier and the read back, about 83 cycles at IPT 4. Pre-reducing per warp (R14, 0.54x to 0.97x), speculative posting with per-lane addresses (R15, 1.08x to 2.46x slower) and a per-warp store with refresh redux (R15, 137 per round) all lose, because the redux is on the chain and ptxas moves CREDUX across BAR.SYNC freely. Nothing issued before a `__syncthreads` arrival is in its shadow. The levers left are fewer warps per block (69.5 at 128 threads), fewer rounds, and a true split barrier that no card has tried. B5 (two warp phases of packed W2 rounds) reached 1.16x over B2 at one cell and 0.73x of its throughput and is killed (R13): the merge list over 8 k words grows with k and one warp works while seven wait.
- Collectives are budgeted per scheduler (T33): about 5.5 cycles per redux, 7 per ballot and 4 per shuffle, so two redux per warp per round at 256 threads cost 23 scheduler cycles that hide inside an 80-cycle round, and a redux throughput limit was never the cause of a slow round in wave 1.
- A per-lane sorted list costs its depth, not the input size (T38): depth min(k, items per lane), the selection network in the prologue carries the N dependence (W2 bf16 k 16: 450 cycles at N 32, 889 at N 512, 2629 at N 2048 in total, round unchanged), and above the list depth every round shifts the whole list (50, 89, 147 cycles at depth 16, 32, 64). Above 16 to 32 items per lane the prologue is the cost to attack (two-level lists, N7, a prefilter, W5 and B4). B2's two-level list (sorted head of 4, unsorted rest) carries B2 to N 32768 at k 4 and 16 for bf16 (4774 cycles against B1's 19709, verified, fp32 holds k 4 and is even at k 16) and wins throughput at every IPT from 16. The win below k 16 comes from the cheaper prologue, not the round: in the latency regime the two-level round costs more than the full-list round at 16 to 64 items per lane (159 against 112, 140 against 113, 189 against 164 cycles) because of the divergent refill branch, and is cheaper only against the control's rolled loop at k 256 and in the throughput regime.
- Index emission has two good forms and one bad one. One independent atomic ticket per selected item (T21) is right for small k, removes B1's exchange buffer, three barriers and the memoized key copy (5 to 19 percent, temp storage independent of N, no spill at IPT 128). One packed block scan over per-thread class counts (T29) is k-flat and gives positions in index order, which makes `prefer_smaller_index` free, and it is the whole of B3's 1.3x to 2.0x at k at least N over 16 (B1's ticket pair epilogue grows by 5k to 13k cycles with k). Per-slot ballot aggregation of tickets (T22, R16) serializes the slots and loses everywhere. Closed in wave 2 by the same-kernel A/B of the ticket spelling (T57 in N9, T61 inside the AIR kernel, verified): the constant-address ticket was the whole fp32 price of the deterministic AIR path at N 4096 and below (13.6).
- Radix first passes on float keys waste discrimination (T30): an 8-bit digit pairs two binades, so the pivot bucket holds 29 percent of N at k over N between 1 over 16 and 1 over 8 on normal data and a 9-bit first digit caps it at 14 percent. A fixed-point image over the tile's value bracket (T25, N6) leaves 1.1 to 7.3 candidates after one 256-bin pass for every k at most 256 at N 1024 to 32768, and is exact after two passes in 97 to 100 percent of random rows where the fp32 8-bit schedule needs three passes in 24 to 89 percent. N6 wins where it saves a pass (fp32 N 4096 k 16 to 256 by 11 to 24 percent, N 1024 k 16 by 2 to 5 percent) and loses where it does not (bf16 k 64 and above at N 1024, 19 to 37 percent), and it carries 46 against 32 registers at IPT 4 and 7256 bytes of spill at IPT 128, so it loses throughput everywhere. Compaction of the pivot bucket (B3) pays only when it removes at least two tile sweeps, from 16 items per thread for fp32 and 32 for bf16, never more than 1.37x at small k. At warp scope the same bracket with an exact per-lane bound takes k 64 to 1024 at N 256 to 2048 from W2 (section 22, T59).
- bf16 is cheaper than fp32 only where the word is cheaper: by 60 to 115 cycles per call for W2 at IPT 8 to 16 (the tie check), by 270 to 670 fixed cycles for B2 (twiddle, pack, tie count, one barrier), not at all for B1 (two 8-bit passes without early exit against two to four with), and for W1 only through the packed word (the native `__hgt` path is slower than fp32 in every cell, 10.4). N6's bracket makes the two types cost the same.
- Latency and throughput disagree in sign in three measured places: N6 (ahead in latency at fp32 N 4096, behind in throughput at 3 against 5 blocks per SM), B5 (1.16x latency, 0.73x throughput), and B2's two-level list at IPT 16 to 32 for k 16 to 64 (0.73x latency, 1.66x throughput, the barrier-bound chain pays the reconvergence, the issue-bound regime pays the full-list shifts). Every block card carries registers and occupancy next to its cycles, B2's 21 registers and B1_idx's 32 are the bars at 8 blocks per SM. Two more in wave 2 (both verified): N5 is a throughput primitive at warp scope (1.22x to 1.82x per call in one-wave throughput, 0.6x to 0.75x per call in latency against the one-word network) and a latency win only at sub-warp scope, and B4 runs one block per SM from IPT 64 (255 registers) against B1_idx's two and still leads throughput 2.1x to 2.7x at N 32768 because its per-call work is a third. And W4_chunk (verified): fp32 N 8192 M 4 is 1.08x ahead of B3_tuned in latency and 0.94x behind in throughput, bf16 M 8 1.22x and 1.00x (2 blocks per SM with 50 to 75 KB of temp storage).
- Sub-warp primitives need full-warp collectives with the identity in the other lanes (T37), 4x to 7x over the masked form, and the harness's sub-warp kernels carry `BSSY` regions in variant and control alike (a harness property that does not bias the comparison).
- The measured price of `prefer_smaller_index` per family (details and cards in the determinism subsection of each lane): W2 zero (12.6), B2 zero (14.6), B3 zero (15.6), the W1 bf16 word zero and fp32 through N10 at a negative price against the best `not_guaranteed` route (11.6, 19.6), W5 zero (18.6), N6 at warp scope, W3 and N1 zero (22.6), W4 zero (23.6), N5 zero in the exact form and 2 to 4 percent in the fast form (20.6), B4 plus 4 to 17 percent (21.6), B1 free on fp32 and 4 to 24 percent on bf16 after the class-indexed ticket and the per-candidate lookup (13.6, from 19 to 51 percent in wave 1), N9 negative against N6's warp-redundant rank (21.4). B6 zero through the stable sort (10.5).
- Flip bands. The wave 1 picture (cards): at warp scope W2 wins every verified cell with k at most 64 from N 128 on and the W1 family keeps N at most 64 at k 16 to 32, at block scope B2 wins k 4 to 16 at every N to 16384, B1_idx or N6 take k 32 to 1024 up to N 4096 and N 32768, B3_tuned takes k at least N over 16 from N 4096, and the B2 against B1 crossover sits near k 22, 34, 36 to 40 and 45 to 50 at N 1024, 4096, 8192 and 16384 for bf16 (19, 33, 50, 45 fp32) against B1_reference. Wave 2 moved the warp owners (W5 from k 16 at N 1024 and 2048, N6 at warp scope from k 64 at N 256 to 2048 and the k 256 and 1024 cells, N10 for fp32) and the block shapes (128 threads for B2 below N 8192, 512 for the radix select above). The phase 3 block pass (one session, harness 0.10, umbriel-b200-047) is the record now: B4_v4 takes 32 of 77 `not_guaranteed` latency cells (every N 8192 and above from k 32 or 64 to k 1024, median plus 160 percent over the reference and plus 52 percent over the runner-up) and 32 of 75 under `prefer_smaller_index`, the two-level B2 list keeps k 4 and 16 at N 16384 and 32768, B3_tuned keeps k 4096 and takes 15 deterministic latency cells, the 128-thread B2 (12) and N9_w2fin2 (13) share N 1024 to 4096, B1_idx (20 plus 9 at 512 threads) and B1_det2 (11, 16 deterministic) take the mid-N throughput cells, 144 of the 310 head-to-head block cells are close calls, a runner-up within 15 percent: nothing in the study settles a close call by speed (same-node reruns reproduce to 0.1 percent, the margin is real but below the 15 percent the study treats as a decision), either adapter is an acceptable choice on speed, and the choice falls to the other columns, the tie_heavy flip list of 16 (robustness on the layout-aligned tie input), register count and spills, the deterministic tie-break cost and code size, with cross-node agreement as the only measurement evidence added (the 48-cell B4_v4 confirmation on umbriel-b200-068, ratios 0.965 to 1.043, no winner flips). Section 16 holds the cells and the definition. The phase 3 warp and sub-warp pass (version 29, same node, second session) sets the warp bands: W2 holds k 4 to 32 at N 32 to 512 (fp32 N 512 k 32 to N6w) and bf16 k 4 at N 2048 and 4096, the W2 to W5 flip sits at N 1024 (bf16 k 16, fp32 k 8) and W5 takes every k 4 to 64 cell at N 2048 and 4096 under fp32 and k 16 to 64 under bf16, N6w holds k 48 and above at N 256 and 512, yields N 1024 bf16 k 48 and 64 and the N 2048 k 64 cells to W5 and keeps k 256 and 1024 at N 4096, W3_d4c takes k 1024 at N 2048 (a close call), W4_wmerge bf16 N 2048 k 256, and N10 keeps two close-call fp32 cells (N 32 k 16, N 128 k 64) and loses the other fp32 small-N cells to W2, so the wave 2 claim of N10 for fp32 at small N did not survive the same-session comparison. At sub-warp scope the 16-lane W2 form takes bf16 latency from N 32 and N10 at 16 lanes the fp32 k 16 and 64 cells, throughput goes to 4 or 8 lanes (16). 97 warp and 23 sub-warp cells are close calls. The tie_heavy pass as generated is partly a layout test (its period 64 aligned with the stripe, T67, T68), the shuffled variant (harness 0.11, umbriel-b200-094, 16) is the tie-flood reading: B4_v4 returns to 1.15x its random time at fp32 N 16384 k 32 (5316 cycles against 19153 on the aligned pattern and 4626 on random) and leads B3_tuned there (18582), stays behind B3_tuned at k N over 4 as on random (fp32 N 16384 k 4096 19860 against 10889), and B1_n6b's aligned-pattern wins shrink (fp32 N 8192 k 32 7378 against 9306 aligned and 4499 random), so the alignment helped the per-value binning as much as it hurt the thread-max bound.
- A certified lower bound plus compaction beats the per-lane list from 32 items per lane (W5, section 18, verified on the optimizer's node). Grouped lane statistics give a threshold with no retry, one compare per item, a shared scatter and gather of at most D per lane, and W2's rounds on the short list: 1.35x over W2 at the bf16 N 1024 k 32 kill cell (1837 against 2481), 2.49x at k 64, 1.92x at fp32 N 2048 k 16, 1.31x to 2.79x in throughput from k 16 at N 512, a loss at 16 items per lane and at k 8. The cost is the compaction and not the bound (a shared store instruction costs about 10 cycles on one warp), the two-level per-lane list at the same cells (N7) was killed at 1.58x against a 2x bar (R18, T49). Phase profile and cells in 18.3 and 18.4.
- For fp32 at warp scope, run the network on keys only and recover the indices by rank (N10, section 19, verified on a second node). The twiddled key goes through the W1x min and max network at the bf16 word's cost, the final merge stops at stride k with one `redux.sync.min` (T51), one ballot per slot in slot-major index order ranks the items (T52): 0.84x to 0.29x of the u64 index-in-key word from N 32 to 256 (661 against 934 at N 64 k 32, 960 against 1918 at N 128 k 16) and 0.99x to 0.27x of the best `not_guaranteed` route, so the requirement's price on fp32 is negative in the W1 family, the sub-warp router shape 1409 against 5299. Not served: sorted output, N 8, logical warp 4 at N 32. Decomposition and cells in 19.3 and 19.4.
- Block size is a tuning dimension of every block primitive and the two regimes disagree on it (N8, section 17, all seven cards verified, two with node dependent verdicts marked). The B2 round is the barrier plus a flat post plus the list shift (T43, T46), so 4 warps are the B2 latency shape at N 1024 to 4096 (1182 against 1380 at bf16 N 1024 k 16) and 2 warps gain nothing, the radix passes want 512 threads from N 8192 (1.24x to 1.45x at N 32768 with 100 to 103 registers and no spill, T45), and at equal threads per SM 64-thread blocks give 1.2x to 1.66x one-wave throughput at N at most 2048 (T44). Recommendation tables in 17.4.
- An exact bound plus compaction beats every histogram pass from N 8192 on at block scope (B4, section 21, eight cards verified on two nodes). The minimum of the per-thread maxima admits about 1500 candidates at every N from 4096, one image pass and a warp finish resolve them: 2.26x bf16 and 2.60x fp32 under B3_tuned at N 32768 k 256 under harness 0.8 (2.37x and 2.87x in the card), 1.32x to 1.38x at N 8192 k 64, a tie at N 4096, a loss at N 1024 and at k 2048, and in the phase 3 pass 32 of 77 block latency cells. The cost is about 3300 cycles of finish plus 37 per item of the tile for three register sweeps, and the three structural costs of the first builds were found by clock64 phase stamps and not by instruction counts (T56). Profile and cells in 21.3 and 21.4.
- Two bf16 keys per word double the network's work per instruction and gain 1.2x to 1.8x in one-wave throughput, not 2x (N5, section 20, verified). `max.bf16x2` and `min.bf16x2` are the compare-exchange for both halves and one shuffle moves both, but the direction predicate rebuilt per stage (T53) and the index recovery land on the same half-rate pipe as the exchange (T47): 1.22x to 1.44x at k at most 32 and 1.43x to 1.82x at k 64 over the one-word network, 1.54x over W2 at N 256 k 32 and 2.5x to 3.2x at k 64, a loss at k 16, a latency win at sub-warp scope only. Tables in 20.3 and 20.4.
- A full sort never selects (B6, killed, R17, T54, T55, unverified by rerun): `cub::BlockRadixSort` on the packed word costs 740 + 82 x IPT cycles per pass and is 2.1x to 2.35x behind B3_tuned for bf16 and 5.0x to 6.5x for fp32 at every k from N over 8 to N over 2, k-flat on both sides. What survives is the keys-only sort of the packed word (T55, ties by index for free) and the sorted-output premium as the post-sort route, stated once in 23.4 with the tables in section 16.
- An exact value bracket plus one 32-bin pass plus a packed finish is the warp answer for k 64 and above up to N 2048 (N6 at warp scope, section 22, verified on both archs). The per-lane m-th largest bounds the k-th exactly for k at most 256, the 32 equal-width bins leave 8 to 39 items in the pivot bin on random input, the histogram costs one shared atomic per item (T59) and the finish is W2's rounds on at most 64 compacted words: 1.44x over W2 at the bf16 N 512 k 64 kill cell, 2.5x to 9.5x at k 256 and 1024, W2 keeps k 16, N 2048 k 64 and N 4096. W3 is the fallback with no cell of its own (partial), N1 has none (killed as a primitive), and the lane's three correctness bugs were all caught by the battery and never by random input (T60). Decomposition and cells in 22.3 and 22.4.
- Sorted output is cheapest as a k-truncated merge of sorted runs, and the merge beats the unsorted radix select at four runs (W4, section 23, verified with the sorted order checked): 4266 bf16 and 6571 fp32 for 4 sorted runs of 2048 against B3_tuned's unsorted 6501 and 7100 and B6's 13511 and 32578, at warp scope 19786 and 42598 at N 2048 k 1024 against W2's sorted 150078 and 154818, 0.29x to 0.54x of W5_v4 at k 64. The route per scope is stated once in 23.4, the tables are in section 16, the codegen lessons (the rolled chunk loop T62, linear addressing over the swizzle) in 2.10.
- The streaming contract note (N3, killed after one cell, one card on umbriel-b200-068, harness 0.7, unverified): on resident data a streaming "insert chunk, keep k" primitive built on a histogram-carried threshold costs 2.1x to 2.3x B3_tuned's resident selection at N 32768 k 2048 as built (43.2k against 20.8k fp32 at 256 threads) and would land at parity to 1.3x after fixing its finish (projected, not measured), never under B4's k 1024 numbers, so it is the right tool only when the row is not resident, at about 2.3k to 2.8k cycles per chunk of 4096 (about 1.4k projected). The real streaming kernel is unmeasured and needs a harness arrangement that loads chunks inside the call. Numbers and the phase profile in 10.5.
- Arch: B200 and GB200 agree to the cycle on every SM primitive (T16) and the only cross-host effect seen is the aarch64 cicc codegen on compare networks (T20), but B200 nodes differ by 5 to 33 percent on large unrolled kernels at large k in four independent sightings (B2 at 128 threads, W2 at k 32 to 64, B5 at k 64, the two-level B2 kernels at k 64), a steady 1.24x per call on one node pair with identical hardware and an identical control, under investigation (2.10). At N 128 to 256 (chains of 900 to 3000 cycles) the two B200 hosts umbriel-b200-020 and 047 agree to the cycle on all six kernels of the codegen experiment, consistent with the node term appearing only on long dependent chains. Rule: W1-family numbers are never compared across hosts, and cross-node comparisons of long dependent chains are not safe at the 3 percent level. The node term is closed as T58 (a property of one GPU on large 128-thread kernels, 10.6), and the N6w cards measured on GB200 reproduce on B200 to the cycle up to N 2048 (22), so T16 holds for ballot, redux, shuffle, shared atomic and min max code and the T20 gap is specific to compare networks compiled on an aarch64 host. GB200 finding from the phase 3 pre-flights (hw-researcher, T58 updated, notes under `state/results/hw-researcher/gb200_rerun_gb200-nvl4-ts2-99/` and `_ts2-105/`): two GB200 hosts agree within 1.6 percent on 25 cells, and both run one kernel configuration slower than the healthy B200s, 128 threads at k 64 with 32 to 64 items per thread (bf16 N 4096 k 64 plus 8 percent, fp32 plus 15 percent, N 8192 k 64 plus 3 to 6 percent), while every other 128-thread cell including the 116 KB kernels matches the B200 within 0.5 percent, so it is neither code size nor a node but a generation property of that configuration (one warp per scheduler with a long register-resident list, the same configuration that deviated on umbriel-b200-020). The GB200 pass values stand as measured, the opt-N6w GB200 cards carry no node caveat, and umbriel-b200-020 remains the only node outlier of the study. The GB200 pass (16, gb200-nvl4-ts2-99, harness 0.10, 2026-10-26, warp, sub-warp and the block cells at N 1024 and 4096) names the same winner as the B200 pass in 492 of 518 cells seen through the adapters both hosts measured, the others lane-count ties, B1_idx against B1_det2 throughput ties or the named sub-warp cells GB200 ran at every lane count, warp latency within 2 percent on 126 of 159 same-winner cells with the long-list cells at N 2048 to 4096 from k 64 up 3 to 10 percent slower (the generation property above), warp throughput a uniform 7.5 percent higher including the W1_reference control, so a B200 winner is a GB200 winner in every scope measured.

## 3. Cluster agent of `DeviceBatchedTopK`

### 3.1 How it works

One thread-block cluster of up to 16 CTAs handles one segment of at most 2^21 keys on SM 9.0 and later. A segment of up to 8K keys that fits one CTA runs there with `__syncthreads` in place of cluster barriers, a larger segment gets the fully resident cluster width with the fewest waves, and if nothing fits it gets the widest cluster plus a streamed overflow. Keys arrive in chunks (16 KB by default, 8 to 31 KB when tuned) through TMA bulk copies into shared memory. Chunks that do not fit stay in global memory and are re-streamed every pass. The selection is a most-significant-digit-first radix select with 11-bit digits by default and pass 0 fused into the load. Each pass builds a block-private histogram, non-leader CTAs `red.add` their non-empty buckets into the leader over distributed shared memory, the leader picks the bucket that holds the k-th key for everyone, and early stop fires when the splitter bucket holds exactly the remaining k. A segment pays eight cluster barriers at 11 bits (one initial, two per pass, one in the filter), idle ranks included. The final filter is one sweep that classifies every key three ways and places it into a front or tie region, seeded by a cross-CTA prefix. Non-deterministic ties are placed in arrival order. Deterministic ties are resolved on the one CTA that straddles the cut by index-ordered scans, which pins the selected set but not the output order. Structural references: `agent_batched_topk_cluster.cuh` header comment and lines around 2913 to 3130 (histogram passes), `dispatch_batched_topk.cuh` lines 333 to 480 (launch shape), `PR9224_code_review.md` section 6.3, `agent_batched_topk_cluster_review.md` section 4, `CLUSTER_TOPK_B200_PERF_IDEAS.md` sections 2 and 4.

### 3.2 Where the time goes (B200, umb-b200-237, 2026-10-10, deterministic pairs, K=512, random keys)

| Topic | Measurement | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Launch floor | 7.7 us on every cell, the same for the non-cluster baseline kernel. In-kernel 5.3 to 6.0 us, SM active 1.6 to 2.2 us. | `CLUSTER_TOPK_B200_PERF_IDEAS.md` section 1 finding 1, section 3.9 |
| Work net of the floor, one segment | 2.7 us at 1K, 6.7 at 8K, 6.6 at 64K, 10.7 at 256K, 19 at the 1M row (a 490K-key segment, sizes are uniform in [K, MSS]). Tie patterns add up to 45% at 1M. | section 1 finding 1, sections 2 and 3.1 |
| Stall shares at 64K, 256K, 1M (one segment) and 256K (32 segments) | cluster barriers 42, 28, 21, 40%. Sweep bodies 13, 17, 34, 25%. Prologue 18, 29, 21, 13%. Pass skeleton 12, 6, 6, 9%. Filter control 6, 14, 11, 6%. | section 3.3 |

Baseline for the prototypes is main plus the negate twiddle (NT), 2026-10-10, same node (`CLUSTER_TOPK_B200_IDEA_EVALUATIONS.md`, branches `perf/cluster-topk-allgather`, `perf/cluster-topk-groupmax`, `perf/cluster-topk-groupmax-nt`).

The gather moved the reduction from DSMEM atomics into about 1,900 extra warp instructions per pass per CTA (28 to 63% more instructions, the DSMEM atomic reduction it replaced was nearly free) and 36 KB of static shared memory changed the launch shape at 32 segments. Predicted 10 to 20% faster. Tests pass. | dead end as built |
| B1 per-group skip test inside the sweeps (group-max summaries) | +27% vs NT. A warp handles a 32-key group in 10 to 15 instructions and the test costs the same (15% of all instructions). | dead end |
| B1 refined: per-pass compacted list of surviving groups, histogram passes only | +8.2% mean vs NT, -7 to -9% at 1M, -2 to -3% at 512K. | not net positive |

Averaged over K and patterns the 512K and 1M gain is about zero: random, quantized, relu and tie_heavy -1.4 to -4.2%, pivot_tie +11.5% (nothing skippable, full overhead). Summaries alone cost +1.64% (0.5 to 1% at 1M). Tests pass. | promising but not landed, needs the filter sweep and a cheap "nothing skippable" bail-out |
