## 12. W2, redux based iterative extraction on packed words (wave 1)

### 12.1 What it is

Every item becomes one unique 32-bit word (twiddled bf16 key over a 16-bit inverted index, or for fp32 the top 32 minus log2 N twiddled key bits over the index, idea N4). Each lane packs its items, selects its L largest in sorted order with a per-lane network (L the next power of two of min(k, items per lane), sorted blocks of L and pairwise top-L merges), and the group runs k rounds of `redux.sync.max` over the list heads. The lane whose head equals the maximum is the unique winner and shifts its list by one with predicated selects. The winner's index is decoded from the uniform maximum and stored by lane r mod T. For fp32 a tie pass after the rounds restores the exact result inside the tie class of the k-th truncated key. Variants: v1 (bitonic blocks, counting tie pass), v2 (next-word tie check: one redux of the largest word not emitted), v3 (v2 plus Batcher odd-even merge sort blocks, 20 percent fewer comparators), sw and sw_v2 at sub-warp scope (masked redux against one full-warp redux per logical warp), and the B5 block composition (closed, R13). Optimizer opt-W2, branch `exp/topk-swarm/W2`, seven cards, six verified by verify-W2 on umbriel-b200-091 (headline cells to the cycle, the three warp cards by the first instance, the sub-warp fix and the two B5 cards by the second), the masked sub-warp baseline `W2_packed_sw` not verified as a result card because its only control is an A/A run (it reproduces to the cycle and stands as the in-section baseline of the fix), no GB200 card. The lead appended a verification notes section with four corrections to `approaches/W2/REPORT.md`, carried into 12.4 and 12.7.

### 12.2 Contract

Input blocked in registers (lane t holds items t IPT to t IPT plus IPT minus 1), N a power of two multiple of the group size up to 65536 (bf16) or 32768 (fp32), k in [1, N over 2], rounds unrolled up to k 64 and rolled above. Output: k indices in shared memory in descending key order with ties by smaller index (the fp32 tie pass may rewrite the last slots of a tie class in key then index order). No temp storage at warp and sub-warp scope. Both requirements run the same code and measured identical cycles in all 56 cell pairs (124 against 124 at N 32 k 4 to 150107 against 150109 at N 2048 k 1024). Sync: `redux.sync` only. NaN: a NaN with the sign bit clear twiddles above plus infinity and is selected first, one with the sign bit set is never selected, the all-ones pattern may collide with the empty-list sentinel. Minus zero mapped onto plus zero before packing.

### 12.3 Where the time goes

- The round: 24.9 cycles per round at IPT 8 inside the primitive (slope over k 16 to 32 at bf16 N 256), 29.6 from k 8 to 16, against T19's 25.8 in isolation. Exactly one `CREDUX` per round, no `VOTE`, no `FLO`, the emission store off the critical path. Verified to the cycle (`W2_packed_b200_20261025_1`).
- List pruning (T38): the round stays at 25 to 27 cycles for any items per lane as long as k is at most 16 (N 1024 k 8 from 32 items per lane: 1061 cycles at 41 registers). Above the list depth every round shifts the whole list: 50 cycles per round at depth 16 (N 512 k 256), 147 at depth 64 (N 2048 k 1024).
- The prologue dominates above IPT 8: bf16 k 16 costs 450 (N 32), 476 (N 128), 889 (N 512), 1607 (N 1024), 2629 (N 2048) in total with bitonic blocks, 820, 1485, 2377 at N 512 to 2048 with odd-even blocks (v3). At N 1024 k 16 the throughput kernel has 960 instructions of which 390 (v1) or 322 (v3) are `VIMNMX`, issue bound at about one instruction per cycle on one warp.
- fp32 through N4: v1's counting tie pass cost 234 to 304 cycles at IPT 8 and 343 to 1319 at IPT 32 to 64 and two to three times the bf16 registers (179 at N 1024 k 32). v2's next-word check brings fp32 to bf16 plus 62 to 98 cycles at IPT 8 to 16 (N 256 k 4 314 against 252, N 1024 k 16 1684 against 1607) and the registers to 104 at N 1024 k 32 (occupancy 2 against 1). The host simulation (`n4_tie_sim.out`): the slow path runs on 0.0 to 0.3 percent of inputs at N 256 to 2048 (24 to 21 key bits), 3 percent at N 4096 k 16, 25 to 53 percent at N 32768 with 17 bits, and the packed result alone is wrong on 1 to 14 percent of inputs at 16 key bits, so the pass is needed.
- Sub-warp: the masked redux round costs 174, 327 and 649 cycles at 2, 4 and 8 logical warps per hardware warp (a `WARPSYNC` loop over the distinct masks, T37), the full-warp redux per group 34, 46 and 88. Verified (rerun slopes 172, 334, 654 and 34, 44, 88).
- Against the references: W2 v1 beats `WarpBitonicTopK` in every bf16 warp cell up to k 256, 6.3x at N 32 k 4 (124 against 778), 14x at N 128 k 4 (162 against 2267), 30x at N 512 k 4 (425 against 12606), 14x at N 512 k 16 (889 against 12606), 29x at N 2048 k 16 (2629 against 76075), 1.85x at N 512 k 256, 1.25x at N 2048 k 1024, and loses one warp cell, fp32 N 2048 k 1024 (154812 against 131668). Throughput bf16 1061 Gelem/s at N 128 k 4 (W1 105), 1381 at N 512 k 4 (97), 1513 at N 2048 k 4 (78), fp32 at N 512 and above held at occupancy 1 to 2 by the tie pass registers (v1) and 2 to 4 (v2). Verified.  (card `W2_packed`, verified).

Correction: the later reducer refactor (c8193dc96e) left the v1 instruction stream identical for bf16 only, the fp32 `W2_packed` kernels differ from the card build (fp32 N 1024 k 16 throughput kernel 2032 instructions and 172 registers against 1768 and 146, 565.6 against 537.4 Gelem/s) with latencies within 0.7 percent because the change sits in a slow path |

Cards `B5_hier_b200_20261025_1` (091, against B1_reference) and `_2` (020, against B2_packed), both verified by the second verify-W2 instance on 091: the kill cell 1193.8 against 1380.0 and 358.7 against 491.0 Gelem/s to the cycle, 94 of 104 cells within 2.5 percent, the ten k 64 cells 3 to 8 percent faster on 091 than on the card's 020 with B2 identical (the third node-variance sighting, the 0.65x to 0.71x loss at k 64 holds either way) |

### 12.5 Ideas not yet tried

- v4: pin the key registers at the start of `run` with an empty asm (T24) so ptxas cannot sink the loads below the prologue, and store the uniform winner from all lanes without a lane predicate, then remeasure the three fp32 regressions against v3.
- The slow path without its per-item arrays (recompute the twiddled key per slow round, member flags as a bitmask), or reload the keys from memory in the slow path, to cut the fp32 register footprint.
- A two-level list or a prefilter for IPT above 16 (N 1024 k 32 and above, N 2048 and 4096 at any k): idea card N7 (wave 2a, with W5), predicted 2370 against 3300 at N 1024 k 64 bf16. Both built in wave 2 (section 18, verified to the cycle by verify-W5 on the optimizer's node): N7 reached 4623 at bf16 N 1024 k 64 and failed its 3300 threshold (R18), W5_v4 reached 2936 there and takes bf16 k 16 to 64 at N 1024 to 2048 and fp32 from k 32 at N 1024 and k 16 at N 2048 from W2, which keeps k 8 and every N 512 latency cell.
- sw_v3 (built, not measured): sw_v2 plus v2 and v3. Two candidates per round at IPT at most 8 (about 1.2x on the model). The N 4096 cells (built, never measured, the k 256 and 1024 translation units compile for more than 20 minutes). A GB200 card. An `sm_100f` variant has no lever here (the rounds reduce packed u32 words).

### 12.6 Determinism and ordering

Inherent: the packed word is unique, every round is an exact arg max, the output is sorted by key descending and index ascending, the result is deterministic and identical under both requirements. `prefer_smaller_index` costs nothing (56 cell pairs identical, verified with identical SASS).

### 12.7 Open items

- Sorted output (section 23, verified): W2 is sorted by construction but at k 256 and above the warp truncated merge sort beats it by 1.24x to 7.6x (bf16 N 2048 k 1024 19786 against W2_packed's 150078, k 256 13538 against 40422), so W2's sorted cells are k at most 32 at N at most 512 and the k 64 cells go to W5_v4's sorted finish.
- Wave 2c (section 22, verified on B200 to the cycle): N6 at warp scope with a shared atomic histogram takes k 64 to 1024 at N 256 to 2048 from W2_packed_v3 in one session (bf16 N 512 k 64 1879 against 2704, k 256 2564 against 14404, N 1024 k 128 4010 against 11872, N 2048 k 1024 17741 against 152618, fp32 N 2048 k 1024 15880 against 151563), W2 keeps k 16 everywhere (568 against 1096 at N 256), N 2048 k 64 (10071 against 11854) and all of N 4096. The W2 rounds remain the finish inside N6w (at most 64 compacted words), so the packed round of T19 is where W2 lives at k 64 and above.
- Wave 2b (section 20, verified by verify-N5 with the W2 batch reproduced): N5's two-row bf16x2 network beats W2_packed_v3 in one-wave throughput from k 32 (N 128 k 32 480 against 252 Gelem/s, k 64 434 against 136, N 256 k 32 511 against 331, k 64 452 against 178) and loses k 16 (N 256 512 against 578), with latency per call 0.25x to 0.63x of W2 at k at most 32, so W2 keeps the latency cells and the throughput cells up to k 16 and the batched bf16 router at k 32 and above goes to N5.
- Wave 2 (section 18, verified to the cycle by verify-W5 on umbriel-b200-028): W5_v4 beats W2_packed_v3 in the same session at bf16 N 1024 k 16 (1337 against 1485), k 32 (1837 against 2481), k 64 (2936 against 7308), N 2048 k 16 to 64 (1861, 2485, 4163 against 2377, 4381, 10071) and fp32 N 2048 k 16 to 64 (2293, 3296, 5975 against 4391, 7353, 10188), and in throughput at every cell from k 16 at N 512 (1.31x to 2.79x). W2 keeps k 8, fp32 N 1024 k 16 (1547 against 1774) and the N 512 latency cells. The fp32 v2 regressions above (12.4) are inside the W2 numbers W5 was measured against (fp32 N 2048 k 16 4391 in the W5 session against the W2 card's 3948 for `W2_packed`), so the fp32 N 2048 k 16 margin against the best W2 form is 1.7x, not 1.9x.
- The v2 fp32 schedule anomaly at IPT 32 and 64 (12.4), fix v4.
- Node term at large k (2.10): identical binaries measured 10 to 29 percent more cycles at k 32 to 64 with 32 to 64 items per lane on umbriel-b200-091 than on 047 (bf16 N 2048 k 64 11011 against 9884), variant and control alike with ratios preserved, every cell up to k 16 to the cycle, and the B5 k 64 kernels 8 percent slower on 020 than on 091 with B2 identical. Cross-node statements in the W2 report carry that uncertainty, the k 32 and above latencies of the W2 cards hold for their node only.
- The v2, v3, sw, sw_v2 and first B5 cards ran no `prefer_smaller_index` cells and do not list them as unsupported. The adapters do not read the requirement, so the code is identical, and the `W2_packed` card's cells (identical cycles and SASS) cover the claim.
- The card's node field names umbriel-b200-068 while 112 of 136 cells came from 091, and the slope batch ran from an uncommitted source (one fp32 N 1024 k 32 binary differs from the committed one, 3350 against 3373 cycles). Both noted by the verifier, no claim affected.
- B5 against B2 was measured with B2's adapter copied from umbriel-b200-047, its source commit is opt-B2's and not recorded in the W2 card beyond the copy.

- Catalogue: T37 and T38 contributed (T40 and T41 are their duplicates), T1, T3, T7, T17, T19, T24, T33, T34, T36 adopted. Idea cards `W2_redux_extraction.md`, `N4_truncated_key_packing.md`, `B5_hierarchical.md` (R13), `N7` (wave 2).

## 13. B1, AIR radix select, the block incumbent (wave 1)

### 13.1 What it is

`block_topk_air` (section 4) and six variants on the striped branch's pass machinery, by opt-B1 on umbriel-b200-068, branch `exp/topk-swarm/B1`: `B1_striped` (the striped header, pairs path, the baseline), `B1_idx` (index-only epilogue: selected items write their striped source index to the caller's k slots by ticket, no exchange buffer, no gather, no memoized key copy, raw shift-and-mask digit extractor, no -0.0 normalization), `B1_idxw` (warp-aggregated tickets, negative), `B1_nz` (`B1_idx` plus the prologue -0.0 normalization), `B1_det` (`B1_idx` under `prefer_smaller_index` with the warp-redundant rank of 4.5), `B1_n6` and `B1_n6b` (idea N6, a fixed-point image over the tile's value bracket instead of radix digits in the first passes, with tracking over all candidates or per bucket). Eight cards, seven verified by verify-B1 on umbriel-b200-047, the first N6 card refuted and superseded by one built from the verifier's own batch.

### 13.2 Contract

Input a full striped tile of N = 256 x IPT keys in registers (thread t holds items t plus i x 256), fp32 or bf16, k in [1, N) at run time, select the largest. Output k source indices in the caller's shared array in ticket order. Temp storage 2128 bytes at every N (histogram double buffer, scan storage, pass state, two counters), plus 128 bytes for the tie table under `prefer_smaller_index`, plus 4 KB of per-bucket trackers and bracket partials for N6 (6352 bytes). Sync: `__syncthreads` inside, 2 per pass plus 1 init plus 1 in the tie phase when it runs. Input keys clobbered by the radix variants (twiddled in place), left intact by N6. NaN: the radix variants twiddle it to the largest key, N6 does not support it. Signed zero: `not_guaranteed` variants keep -0.0 below +0.0, `prefer_smaller_index` variants fold -0.0 onto +0.0 once in the prologue. `prefer_smaller_index` supported up to IPT 64 (a 64-bit tie mask per thread). Partial tiles and the blocked arrangement are not in these prototypes.

### 13.3 Where the time goes

Decomposition at N 1024 (IPT 4) from differences between variants in the same sessions, all verified unless noted: the two radix passes plus prologue are about 1770 (bf16 k 16) and 1790 (fp32) of the index-only call. The pairs exchange epilogue costs 112 (bf16) and 127 (fp32) over the index-only one (`B1_striped` 1879 against `B1_idx` 1767, and 1921 against 1794). The k dependence of tickets and scans from k 16 to 256 is 204 (bf16) and 354 (fp32). The prologue -0.0 normalization costs 31 and 25 (`B1_nz` 1796 against 1765). The deterministic epilogue costs 344 at fp32 with no ties (`B1_det` 2138 against 1794), attribution open (T39). The tie phase itself about 70 at IPT 4 on bf16 rows with ties. At N 32768 (IPT 128) the pass sweep dominates and the register file decides: `B1_idx` holds 128 keys in 248 to 250 registers with no spill and runs 14 to 19 percent ahead of the striped pairs path (15164 against 17966 at fp32 k 16, 16441 against 19162 bf16). The pass count on random input (host simulation, 200 rows per cell, `sim_passes.md`): fp32 8-bit digits execute 2 passes in 56 to 73 percent of the rows at N 1024 and 5 to 28 percent at N 32768 (mean 2.94 passes at k 256), 11+11+10 and 12+12+8 execute 2 in 90 to 98 percent, bf16 8+8 executes 2 in 97 to 100 percent and never 1. B1's reference numbers against main are in 10.4.

Card `B1_striped_b200_20261025_1`, verified within 1.5 percent |

The N6 kill experiment (idea card): criterion 1 held on the host simulation (pivot bin 1.1 to 7.3 items after one pass for every k at most 256, exact exit after pass 1 in 11 to 94 percent of rows and after pass 2 in 97 to 100 percent, a single outlier collapses the bracket and triggers the radix fallback), criterion 2 (per-item cost within 10 percent of the radix extractor) was argued from instruction counts (about 4 extra per item per pass, 7 percent of the sweep at IPT 128) and not from the swap measurement the card asked for. At the named cell N 1024 k 64 bf16 the committed N6 is 19 percent behind `B1_idx` and the per-bucket build about 9 percent behind. Verdict: not killed, needs its own optimizer for the register problem and the finish (N9, wave 2a with B4).

### 13.5 Ideas not yet tried

- The deterministic epilogue: a same-kernel A/B of the ticket spelling against the constant-address form (T39), then `prefer_smaller_index` should cost the tie phase (about 70 cycles at IPT 4 on bf16 rows with ties, nothing on fp32 rows without) plus the 1.5 to 6 percent normalization.
- Digit schedule by IPT for fp32: 11+11+10 or 12+12+8 at IPT 64 and 128 where the host simulation saves 0.3 to 0.9 passes per call and the sweep is 85 percent of a pass (the scan cost of wider digits is measured at IPT 4 only, R2). One batch at N 16384 and 32768.
- N6: what holds 128 extra words per thread at IPT 128 with the T24 pin in place, the pass-1 representable-value exit for bf16 (the simulation says one pass at N 4096 and 32768 for every k at most 256), B4's top-m bound for k above 256, a FINISH for pivot bins of at most 32 candidates (N9), IPT above 64 under `prefer_smaller_index`.
- The pairs output form on the index-only epilogue (scatter 8-byte pairs by the same ticket) for the full evaluation. An `sm_100f` variant has no lever here.

### 13.6 Determinism and ordering

Set determinism up to the ties at the k-th key by construction, `not_guaranteed` takes the ties in atomic ticket order. `prefer_smaller_index` (`B1_det`, N6 variants): the warp-redundant rank over the exact ties (per warp and slot a ballot into a table, one barrier, bases by a shuffle scan, taken ties written at total_selected plus rank without an atomic), correct on every battery pattern including signed_zero. Measured price in this build: plus 19 to 23 percent latency at N 1024 k 16 and plus 24 to 51 percent at N 4096, minus 17 to 38 percent throughput (verified). Of this, about 344 cycles per call at fp32 N 1024 are present with no ties at all and were attributed to the compiler's warp aggregation of the constant-address ticket atomic (T23, `S2R SR_LTMASK` and `SR_LANEID` per site), an attribution that is a hypothesis until the same-kernel A/B exists (T39), 25 to 31 cycles are the normalization and about 70 the tie phase itself. The knowledge base 4.5 figure of about plus 1 percent on random keys stands for the tie phase, not for this epilogue spelling. `prefer_larger_index` not built (the rank test flips). Sorted output by a post-sort of k.

Update 2026-10-25 19:00 UTC, opt-B1det (`B1_det2`, cards `B1_det2_b200_20261025_1` against `B1_det` and `_2` against `B1_idx` under `not_guaranteed`, B200 umbriel-b200-068, harness 0.8, note `approaches/B1/DET_TICKET.md`, T61), both cards verified by verify-B1det on the cards' node (full grids within 0.4 percent, the 358-cycle ticket cost at fp32 N 1024 k 16 reproduced exactly, the SASS confirming the aggregation instructions gone and the ticket a plain shared add on a register address, the control-req form real with the control symbols carrying `not_guaranteed` and the variant `prefer_smaller_index`). The hypothesis above is settled inside the AIR kernel: with the ticket spelled as B1_idx's class-indexed counter (T57) and nothing else changed (a copy of `block_topk_b1.cuh` in its own namespace, the ticket hunk the only difference, the aggregation SASS gone: 0 `S2R SR_LTMASK`, 0 `VOTEU.ANY`, 0 `FLO` against 8, 8, 8 at fp32 N 1024 k 16), the deterministic call loses 10.8 to 22.3 percent of its latency (fp32 N 1024 k 16 1802 against 2160, so the 344 cycles attributed across two kernels are 358 inside one kernel pair, about 90 cycles per item slot at IPT 4 and 37 to 62 at IPT 64) and gains 5 to 23 percent in throughput (8 fewer registers at IPT 4, occupancy 6 to 8 blocks per SM). The price of `prefer_smaller_index` after the fix, against `B1_idx` in one section: fp32 minus 0.7 to plus 1.0 percent latency at N 1024 and 4096 (1802 against 1807 at k 16, 4631 against 4638 at N 4096 k 64, was plus 19 to 26 percent), plus 3.5 to 9.6 at N 16384 and plus 5.0 to 9.2 at N 32768 with 512 threads, bf16 plus 5.2 to 10.3 at N 1024 (1868 against 1775 at k 16, was plus 22 to 26), plus 12.2 to 24.3 at N 4096 (was plus 34 to 51), plus 12.1 to 36.7 at N 16384 and plus 27 to 53 at N 32768 with 512 threads. Throughput price fp32 3 to 11 percent at N 1024 and 4096, bf16 13 to 39, both 71 to 100 at N 16384 where the two per-slot register arrays of the rank phase (206 to 227 registers against 122 to 128) halve the occupancy, and at 512 threads and N 32768 the 128-register cap spills them (216 bytes of stack, 74 `STL` and 74 `LDL`). What remains is the tie phase itself, measured by phase stamps (T56 method, `approaches/B1/profile_det_phases.cu`): 526 cycles per run at IPT 4 (192 for the per-slot ballots, table stores and barrier, 334 for the base scan and per-slot ranks), 1004 at IPT 16, 3005 at IPT 64, per item slot and not per tie (64 `VOTE`, 64 `STS`, 64 `SHFL.IDX`, 64 `POPC` per thread at IPT 64, issue bound on the vote and shuffle pipes, T33), running whenever the ties do not exactly fill the remaining slots, which is 28 percent of the bf16 rows at N 1024 k 16 and 91 percent at N 16384 k 256 and never on fp32 random rows, tie density being a property of bf16 (10.2). The run fraction times the phase cost plus the minus-zero normalization (14 cycles at IPT 4, 330 to 550 at IPT 64) minus the cheaper det2 epilogue (42 to 681 cycles under B1_idx's bounded tie store for bf16) reproduces the harness price within 25 percent at every profiled cell. So the deterministic AIR path on fp32 is essentially free at N 4096 and below, the 4.5 figure of about 1 percent holds there, and the bf16 cost is the per-slot rank phase, which `B1_det3` attacks.

Update 2026-10-25 20:15 UTC, opt-B1det3 (`B1_det3`, cards `B1_det3_b200_20261025_1` against `B1_det2` and `_2` against `B1_idx` under `not_guaranteed`, B200 umbriel-b200-018, harness 0.8, source e1c6ed6816, note `approaches/B1/DET_TICKET.md` sections 7 to 12, T65 and T66), both cards verified by verify-B1det on a second node (umbriel-b200-068 against the cards' 018): 48 of 48 cells within 0.4 percent on the variant, the det3 gain at N 16384 k 16 reading 4.3 and 3.7 percent there against 5.2 and 4.8 on the card (inside tolerance, the det2 control 0.6 to 1.3 percent faster on 068), registers 40, 48, 128 and 102 with no spill at 512 threads, the bf16 price 4.4 percent at N 1024 k 16, 19.1 at N 4096 k 64, 7.1 at N 16384 k 16 and 15.3 at N 32768 k 16 at 512 threads, fp32 minus 1.3 to plus 8.5. The per-slot tie phase is replaced by a per-candidate rank lookup from shared tables (T65): one candidate bit per slot in a mask (a `LOP3` tree), above 8 items per thread the warp ORs its masks and ballots only the slots in the union into a (warp, slot) ballot table, one block barrier, warps without a candidate skip the rest, the others scan the slots by shuffles with the rounds of 32 slots interleaved and write the bases to a base table, and the threads with a candidate loop over their set bits per 32-bit half and compute the rank from two shared loads and a popcount, writing the output directly. Up to 8 items per thread the adapter keeps per-slot ballots with the base shuffled from its owning lane, since the dependent lookup chain costs about what the per-slot shuffles cost there. Nothing per slot stays in registers, so registers equal B1_idx's at every items per thread (40, 48, 128, 102 at IPT 4, 16, 64 and 512 threads against det2's 40, 64, 206 to 209 and 128 with 592 spill bytes), the throughput kernel runs two blocks per SM at N 16384 against det2's one, and the 512-thread form no longer spills. Against det2 in one lock section: latency minus 1 to 2 percent at N 1024, minus 1.6 to 4.7 at N 4096, minus 5 to 15 at N 16384, minus 10 to 19 at N 32768 with 512 threads, throughput plus 59 to 70 percent at N 16384, fp32 within 2 percent except minus 2 to 5 at N 16384 with plus 65 to 68 percent throughput. On tie rows the deterministic premium fell 57 to 79 percent by the phase stamps (bf16 N 4096 k 64 from 1227 to 370 cycles, N 16384 k 256 from 2731 to 1168, N 32768 at 512 threads from 7260 to 1489, the three deterministic results digest-identical in every row). Price of `prefer_smaller_index` against `B1_idx` after det3 (before, det2, in parentheses): bf16 latency plus 4.4, 6.5, 7.9 percent at N 1024 k 16, 64, 256 (5.2, 7.8, 10.3), plus 10.5, 19.1, 17.0 at N 4096 (12.2, 24.3, 22.5), plus 6.8, 12.2, 16.4 at N 16384 (12.1, 25.3, 36.7), plus 15.6, 24.1, 24.4 at N 32768 with 512 threads (27.4, 51.2, 53.1), bf16 throughput 9 to 10 percent at N 1024, 11 to 17 at N 4096, 8 to 17 at N 16384 (was 71 to 100), 11 to 19 at N 32768, fp32 within minus 2 to plus 9 percent in latency at every N (minus 1.3 to minus 1.9 at N 1024, where the class-branch epilogue is cheaper than B1_idx's bounded tie store) and 1 to 9 percent in throughput. So the deterministic AIR path is free on fp32, under 8 percent at block N 1024 for bf16, under 20 percent at N 4096 and 16384, and up to 24 percent at N 32768 with 512 threads, where the mask build (about 3.5 instructions per slot) and the scan (136 instructions for two rounds) are still per slot and run on 66 to 91 percent of the rows. Four codegen fixes came from the SASS: 32-bit half loops instead of the ten-instruction `__ffsll` emulation, the two scan rounds interleaved, the exact candidate test, and the rematerialized shared window base pinned (T66, 300 to 1100 cycles per call before the pin). The minus-zero normalization is now the largest fp32 term (1000 to 1250 cycles per call at 512 threads and IPT 64). B1_det3 joins the phase 3 block pass.

- N6 at warp scope (section 22, verified on both archs): the same bracket with an exact per-lane bound, a 32-bin shared atomic histogram (T59) and a packed W2 finish on the pivot bin is a warp primitive that takes k 64 to 1024 at N 256 to 2048 from W2. T60 (the bracket scale guard against a plus-zero scale with infinite keys) names the block N6, B4 and N9 brackets as in scope, their batteries passed inf_denorm at the measured cells, the guard is still worth checking in `block_topk_n6.cuh`.
- Block size (section 17, cards `N8_b1idx` verified by verify-N8 on a second node): B1_idx wants 512 threads from N 8192 in latency and from N 16384 in throughput (1.24x to 1.45x at N 32768, 100 to 103 registers with 0 spill against 248 to 250), 256 at N 4096 and below, never 1024, and 64 threads in one-wave throughput at N 1024 to 2048 (1.47x at bf16 N 1024 k 16, verified). The final-evaluation incumbent at N 16384 and 32768 is the 512-thread form (13530 bf16 and 11797 fp32 at k 16). `B1_det` was not swept. N6b at 512 threads gains throughput at every k and latency from k 64 at N 16384 and nothing at N 32768 (card `B1_n6b_2`, verified after amendment, the k 16 latency gain of 512 threads is node dependent: bf16 1.05x on 020 and 0.99x on 068, fp32 0.96x and 0.89x), and its N 16384 k 16 cells are its largest margin over B1_idx (1.48x and 1.58x at 256 threads on 020, 1.54x and 1.65x on 068).
- The B1_det attribution and fix (13.5), highest value per batch. Closed by T57 (section 21.4, card `N9_w2fin` against `N9_w2fin2`, verified by verify-B4 on umbriel-b200-018 at 52 to 1189 cycles): in one kernel the constant-address ticket costs 48 to 1070 cycles per call more than the class-indexed counter, so the `B1_det` price is the aggregation of T23 and the fix is B1_idx's spelling, not the empty asm of T23 (T39). Done by opt-B1det at 19:00 UTC (13.6, T61, two cards on umbriel-b200-068, verified by verify-B1det within 0.4 percent): the class-indexed ticket removes the whole fp32 price at N 1024 and 4096 (minus 0.7 to plus 1.0 percent) and 14 to 27 points of the bf16 price, the ticket alone was 10.8 to 22.3 percent of the deterministic call. `B1_det3` (20:15 UTC, 13.6, T65, T66, verified on a second node) replaced the per-slot tie phase by a per-candidate lookup: registers back at B1_idx's, two blocks per SM at N 16384, no spill at 512 threads, the bf16 price down to 4 to 8 percent at N 1024, 10 to 19 at N 4096, 7 to 16 at N 16384 and 16 to 24 at N 32768 with 512 threads, fp32 free. Next: a cheaper mask build (per slot, about 3.5 instructions, shared with the epilogue's recompute), the minus-zero normalization folded into the first pass's digit extraction (now the largest fp32 term, 1000 to 1250 cycles at 512 threads and IPT 64), B1_idx adopting det3's class-branch epilogue (cheaper than its bounded tie store at fp32 N 1024 and 4096), and the IPT 16 latency kernel sitting on ptxas's 48-register target with 20 to 72 spill bytes outside the chain. B1_det3 is in the phase 3 block pass.
- N9 as N6's finish (section 21.4, verified): the packed W2 finish on the pivot bin gains nothing at k 16 (N6b exits after one pass in 97 percent of the rows) and 9 to 17 percent at k 64 to 256, 13 to 41 percent under `prefer_smaller_index`, which puts N6 ahead of B1_idx at bf16 N 1024 and 4096 k 64 by the chain (1851 against 1878, 3359 against 3838).
- N6 at IPT 128 is register-bound in two of three builds of the same algorithm (1.5 KB, 8.6 KB, 7.3 KB of spill), the build that did not spill badly ran 1.4 to 1.8x faster than the radix path at N 32768 k 16 and cannot be rebuilt (the refuted card). The pin on the bracket parameters is not enough.
- The striped pairs path is 7 percent slower than the blocked one in latency at N 4096 fp32 k 16 while 7 percent faster in throughput, cause unknown.  (card `B1_striped`, verified).

## 14. B2, atomics based iterative extraction on packed words (wave 1)

### 14.1 What it is

Every thread holds its items as packed words (bf16: twiddled key over a 16-bit inverted index, fp32: the top 32 minus log2 N key bits over the index, idea N4, with an exact tie finish) in a sorted per-thread list. Each round every thread posts its head with a fire-and-forget `atomicMax` into one shared slot, one block barrier, the slot is read back, the one thread whose head won shifts its list. k rounds give the k largest in order, the index half makes `prefer_smaller_index` inherent. Three optimizer instances wrote into one worktree after duplicate launches. Attribution by artifact, as the lead decided: `B2_packed` (the baseline card), `B2_valrounds` (the value-rounds control), `B2_packed_n4kill`, `B2_spec`, `B2_spec3` and the 128-thread and large-N batches come from the instance that owned `B2_packed`, `B2_twolevel`, `B2_redux8` and `B2_tiecheck` from the instance that calls itself A in `NOTE_duplicate_instance.md`. Branch `exp/topk-swarm/B2b`, nodes umbriel-b200-091 and 047. Nine wave 1 cards, every one with a verdict: five verified by verify-B2 on umbriel-b200-020 (with repeats on 047), three of instance A verified by verify-B2A on umbriel-b200-068 from commit 8d2a49e496 under harness 0.5 (every ratio within 0.04 of the card, named controls in the same lock section, canaries flat, oracle 13 cases per cell), one refuted at two cells. opt-N8's block-size sweep of the two-level list (`B2_twolevel_b200_20261025_2`) is wave 2 and not folded here.

### 14.2 Contract

Block scope, 256 threads measured (128 in one card), blocked registers in, k indices to shared memory out, sorted by key then index for bf16 and outside the finished tie class for fp32, both requirements in one binary. k in [1, min(N over 2, 256)] at compile time for the sorted-list form (k rounds, the slot array is k words), [1, N over 2] for the two-level form. N = Threads x IPT with IPT a power of two, IPT at most 64 for both types in practice (the 128-word list goes to local memory, 14.3), N at most 65536 (16 index bits). Temp storage 68 bytes (k words plus counters). Sync: `__syncthreads` 1 after the slot init plus one per round, fp32 adds one for the tie count (or one `__syncthreads_or` in the tie-check form) and the finish barriers on the rare tie path. NaN twiddled bitwise (positive NaN above plus infinity, negative below minus infinity), a negative NaN with all mantissa bits set at the last index packs to the empty-slot word and is never selected. -0.0 normalized to +0.0.

### 14.3 Where the time goes

- The round at 256 threads and IPT 4 is 10 SASS instructions (`BAR.SYNC`, `LDS`, `ISETP`, `SEL`, `SEL`, `CREDUX.MAX`, `SEL`, `SEL`, `IMAD.U32`, `ATOMS.MAX`) and 83.5 cycles: about 28 for the barrier, 22 for the redux, the rest read back, compare and atomic issue. ptxas rewrites the 32 same-address atomics of a warp into one `CREDUX` plus one elected `ATOMS`, so the round never issues a 256-way burst and the redux is on the chain (T26, T27). Slope 110 at IPT 16, 112 to 148 at IPT 32 (growing with k, instruction cache suspected, 5008 SASS instructions at k 64), 162 to 205 at IPT 64, 260 with the rolled round loop (k 256 at IPT 32). fp32 85.8, 103, 103, 136 to 227. Verified (`B2_packed_1` and `_2`).
- The fixed cost (k to 0 extrapolation) is the per-thread sort and pack: bf16 45 at IPT 4, 274 at IPT 16, 960 at IPT 32, about 1280 at IPT 64 (odd-even merge sort of 5, 63, 191, 543 compare-exchanges). fp32 adds 270 to 670 (twiddle, pack, tie count, one more barrier). Verified.
- At 128 threads the round is 69.5 cycles at IPT 8 (4 `CREDUX` and a 4-warp barrier of 20 instead of 8 and 28) and 85 to 91 at IPT 32, and the IPT 32 prologue costs 540 against 960 at 256 threads because one warp per scheduler is latency bound where two are issue bound. The k 4 and k 16 cells and these slopes reproduced on two nodes (`B2_packed_3`).
- Two-level list (sorted head of 4, unsorted rest, refill when the head runs empty): prologue 38, 400, 234 at IPT 16, 32, 64 against 272, 968, 1264, and about 4380 at IPT 128 where the full sort does not fit. The round carries a divergent refill branch (`BSSY` region, `VOTEU`, `CREDUX`, `UFLO` after the reconvergence point): 159 against 112 at IPT 16, 140 against 113 at IPT 32 (k 4 to 16), 189 against 164 at IPT 64. Verified (`B2_twolevel`, verify-B2A): the two-level round is not cheaper than the full-list round in the latency regime at 16 to 64 items per lane, the k 16 and below win is the prologue, and the round wins only against the control's rolled loop at k 256 and in the throughput regime.
- The fp32 tail: the count pass after the rounds costs 75 to 108 cycles at IPT 4, 136 to 227 at IPT 16, 262 to 570 at IPT 32, replaced by one `__syncthreads_or` over the heads in `B2_tiecheck` (verified, savings 76 to 570 cycles reproduced).
- Code generation cliffs: full unrolling above IPT 16 is instruction-cache bound (the unrolled k 64 kernel at IPT 32 costs 227 per round in the two-level form, the k 256 kernel unrolled by 8 costs 111), and at IPT 128 the 128-word list goes to local memory with 0 spill bytes reported (12800-byte stack frame, 11833 `LDL`, 995020 cycles per call against B1's 19479, T34).

### 14.5 Ideas not yet tried

- Unroll policy per IPT (unroll by 8 above IPT 16 for every k), predicted to bring bf16 N 8192 k 64 from 13551 to about 7500 cycles (B2_packed 9869). A head of 8 at IPT 64 and above and a shared-memory resident rest at IPT 128 to get below 128 registers. A combined adapter choosing threads (128 or 256) and list per (N, k), measured against B1 at its best shape in one session (N8, wave 2a).  (a prediction from the `B2_packed_2` numbers, not measured).
- Fewer rounds: two slots per round need two barriers (rejected on the model). A true split barrier (`bar.arrive` then `bar.sync`, or an mbarrier) is the only unmeasured lever on the round (R15).
- Port `B2_packed`'s cheaper fp32 prologue shape into the two-level form (76 cycles at IPT 4), fold the tie count into the last round, `red.shared.max` from one lane in inline PTX against the second aggregation, 64-thread blocks (model close to even). Cut the declared range of the sorted-list form to IPT 64 for both types.

### 14.6 Determinism and ordering

Inherent and free in every variant: the index is the low half of the word, `prefer_smaller_index` and `not_guaranteed` run the same binary and measured within 0.63 percent (verified rerun of `B2_packed`), 0.45 to 0.85 percent (spec, spec3 reruns) and 1.9 percent (twolevel, verified rerun). Output sorted by key descending then index ascending for bf16, and for fp32 outside the finished tie class. Set deterministic by construction.

## 15. B3, compacting radix select at block scope (wave 1)

### 15.1 What it is

Radix select in the style of `block_topk_air` (8-bit or 9-bit digits, fire-and-forget shared atomic histogram, fused scan and pivot choice) with two structural changes. After the first pass the items strictly above the pivot bucket are final and their indices are written at once, and the pivot bucket's items (key and index) are compacted into shared memory in index order, so later passes sweep the compacted candidates in registers instead of the whole tile (fallback to tile sweeps when the pivot bucket exceeds N over 4). Every output position comes from one block scan of per-thread class counts packed into one word (above-pivot items in the high 16 bits, ties in the low 16 bits), never from atomic tickets, so the ties at the k-th key are in index order and `prefer_smaller_index` is the same code as `not_guaranteed`. Keys are mapped to an unsigned word whose ascending order is the key's descending order, -0.0 folded into +0.0 before ranking. The final adapter `B3_tuned` compacts after a 9-bit first digit from 16 items per thread for 32-bit keys and 32 for 16-bit keys, with a padded candidate layout and predicated stores, and below that uses 8-bit digits without compaction, which leaves AIR's passes plus the scan emission. Optimizer opt-B3, branch `exp/topk-swarm/B3`, seven cards on umbriel-b200-091, 020 and 047, all verified by verify-B3 (rebuilt at the card's commit and at HEAD a236c7225a, measured on 047), a host model that passed 1584 oracle runs before the first GPU batch.

### 15.2 Contract

Block scope, 256 threads, blocked input in registers, N = 256 x IPT with IPT 1 to 128, k in [1, N), keys fp32 or bf16, int32 indices from the tile position. Output k source indices in the caller's shared array, unsorted (above-pivot items in index order first, then the candidates in index order). Requirements `not_guaranteed`, `prefer_smaller_index`, `prefer_larger_index` (rank reversed), all the same cost. Temp storage two histograms (256 or 512 bins), scan storage, pass state and the candidate buffer: 3152 bytes at N 1024 fp32 and 69712 at N 32768 fp32 (B1: 4256 to 147488). Sync: `__syncthreads` 1 init, 3 per pass, 2 for the compaction, 1 for the final scan. NaN: a positive NaN orders above plus infinity and is selected first, a negative NaN below minus infinity. Full tiles only.

### 15.3 Where the time goes

Measured by differences between variants in one lock section (no in-kernel phase profile). Pass 1 over the tile is common to B1 and B3 and is the bulk of every call at small k (B3 6083 against B1 6992 at N 8192 k 64 fp32, pass 1 roughly 3.5k). The compaction sweep (two counts per item, one packed scan, two predicated stores per item) plus the candidate loads cost about one filtered AIR pass over the tile, so compaction pays only when it removes at least two later tile sweeps: from 16 items per thread for fp32 (passes 2 to 4 saved), only from 32 for bf16 (one pass saved), and it loses 10 to 18 percent at N 1024. The candidate loads were the largest implementation cost of the baseline: a per-thread stride of N over 1024 words gave 32-way bank conflicts at N 32768, 1.33x to 1.34x once padded (T31). The index emission is one block scan (about 300 cycles) plus one predicated store per selected item, independent of k, while B1's ticket pair epilogue grows by 5k to 13k cycles between k 64 and k equal to N over 16 (T29), which is the whole 1.3x to 2.0x at large k. The filtered histogram atomics of the later passes sit in `BSSY` regions at 32 items per thread and above, for B1 as much as for B3 (T32). Registers: 255 at IPT 128 and 128 at IPT 64 (three code paths and unrolled sweeps keep the tile live), the predicated asm store form costs bf16 two blocks per SM at N 8192 and 16384 (103 and 128 registers).

### 15.4 Ideas tried, with verdicts

Chain of seven batches, each against the previous variant, 96 cells each (12 (N, k) points of `cells_b3.json`, both types, both requirements, both regimes), every card verified by verify-B3 within 0.4 to 1.5 percent latency at its own commit:

| Batch, variant (control) | Result | Verdict and card |
|---|---|---|
| 1 baseline: AIR pass, compaction into an N over 4 buffer, scan positions, three paths (B1_reference) | compaction path 0.80x to 0.99x of B1 at small k, fallback path 1.12x to 1.70x at k equal to N over 16 (bf16 N 8192 k 1024 6502 against 11060) | `B3_baseline_b200_20261025_1`, T29 |
| 2 padded: candidate stride N over 1024 plus 1 words, predicated loads (baseline) | 1.33x to 1.34x at N 32768 (bf16 k 256 20909 against 28059), 1.05x to 1.29x at N 8192 to 16384, neutral below | adopted, `B3_padded_b200_20261025_1`, T31 |
| 3 straight: guarded memory ops as predicated inline PTX (padded) | 1.00x to 1.10x on compaction cells, 0.88x to 0.98x on bf16 fallback cells (registers), ptxas keeps branching around the atomics (482 against 764 `BSSY`, 0 predicated `ATOMS` of 608) | kept for the stores only, `B3_straight_b200_20261025_1`, T32. Verifier caveat: rebuilt at HEAD the fp32 fallback cells are 7 to 10 percent slower than at the card's commit (commit 50039daee3 changed the pass loop), so the fp32 fallback gains hold for commit f89a43fa14 only |

Kill experiment of the idea card (N 8192 k 64, threshold 1.3x): 1.051x (bf16) and 1.149x (fp32) latency, 0.833x and 1.068x throughput. The idea as stated is retired at N 8192 and its own clause holds: confined to N 32768 and fp32 (1.366x at N 32768 k 256 fp32) and to be merged into B1 as an option. The large-k wins belong to the emission.

### 15.5 Ideas not yet tried

- Compaction after an N6 first pass (T25): 1 to 7 candidates instead of 14 percent of N, so later passes run over one item per thread at every N and compaction pays from IPT 4. The scan emission already fits N6's structure. This is B4 with N9 in wave 2a.
- Striped candidate layout with a block-uniform slot count (conflict free without padding, needs the warp-redundant rank of 4.5 for the tie ranks, expected 5 to 10 percent at N 16384 and 32768).
- Unconditional histogram atomics into a per-thread dummy word for the filtered passes (T10, T32), estimated a wash at IPT 64, cheap to measure. The C++ store form with compaction at 32 and 64 items per thread (the asm form costs bf16 two blocks per SM there).
- A packed bf16 compaction sweep with `__hgt2_mask` (T18), a 10-bit first digit (pivot bucket 10 percent of N on the host model), 128 and 512 threads, partial tiles, fp16 and fp8 keys.

### 15.6 Determinism and ordering

Set and index deterministic by construction: positions come from block scans in blocked order, the compacted candidates keep index order, the ties at the k-th key are ranked in index order by the same packed scan (`prefer_smaller_index`) or by the reversed rank (`prefer_larger_index`). Measured cost of `prefer_smaller_index`: 0.997x to 1.003x of `not_guaranteed` in all 48 cell pairs of every batch (verified, one throughput cell at 0.9925x). Output positions are deterministic as well. Sorted output by a post-sort of k.

### 15.7 Open items

- The streaming alternative (N3, 10.5, unverified): on resident data the "insert chunk, keep k" primitive is 2.1x to 2.3x behind B3_tuned at N 32768 k 2048 and would reach parity to 1.3x at best, so B3_tuned keeps the resident k 2048 cells and the streaming form is for rows that are not resident.
- The chunk merge cells (section 23, verified): over 4 sorted runs of 2048 the k-truncated merge emits the sorted top 2048 at 4266 bf16 and 6571 fp32 against B3_tuned's unsorted 6501 and 7100 in one session, so B3_tuned loses the M 4 merge cell even unsorted and keeps the M 8 fp32 cells (8151 against 6714 at N 8192 k 1024, 5230 against 4310 at N 4096 k 512) and every unsorted-run case.
- Wave 2b (section 21, verified on two nodes): B4_v4 beats B3_tuned in one session at N 8192 to 32768 for k 16 to 1024 (2.4x to 3.0x at N 32768 in the card, 2.26x and 2.60x under harness 0.8 at k 256 where the B3_tuned control reads 9 to 11 percent lower, 2.0x to 2.1x at N 16384 k 64, 1.32x to 1.38x at N 8192 k 64) and loses at N 4096 and below and at k 2048 (0.74x and 0.81x at N 32768), so B3_tuned keeps k 2048 and the N 1024 to 4096 cells of its band. B6 (R17) is 2.1x to 6.5x behind B3_tuned at k = N over 2, and the phase 3b sorted-output row compares the full sort against B3_tuned plus a post-sort of k words (10.5).
- Block size (section 17, card `N8_b3tuned` verified by verify-N8): B3_tuned gains 1.12x to 1.15x in latency and 1.00x to 1.12x in throughput at 512 threads over 256 at N 16384 and 32768, keeps its spills under the 128-register cap (64 bytes bf16, 844 fp32 at N 32768) and is slower again at 1024. Against B1_idx at 512 threads B3 keeps a latency edge at k 1024 only (1.03x to 1.22x) and loses every throughput cell, so the pairing of 15.8 at matched block sizes narrows B3's band to k 1024 at N 16384 and 32768 plus `prefer_smaller_index`, where it stays free (within 0.3 percent at 512 threads).
- The final evaluation should pair `B3_tuned` with `B1_idx` (T21), not the pairs reference: the gap at large k then shrinks to the ticket serialization of the index-only epilogue.
- bf16 throughput at N 8192 and 16384 small k (0.83x, 0.91x) is a register tier problem of the asm store form.  (card `B3_straight`).
- The GPU numbers at N 16384 and above carry the few percent of control subtraction tolerance of the B1 reference card. The B3 grid (`cells_b3.json`) has N 8192 and 2048 points outside the focus grid, the focus cells N 1024, 4096, 16384, 32768 are covered at two k each.
- Code drift: the B3 header changed during the session (50039daee3), the verifier rebuilt every card at its own commit (reproduced) and at HEAD (fp32 fallback cells moved, compaction cells unchanged). Verdicts unaffected, the straight finding is commit-bound.

## 16. Winners (the phase 3 pass on B200, warp, sub-warp and block)

How to read this study, for a reader who starts here. The matrices of the first part are one node, one session and one harness version per architecture (B200 umbriel-b200-047, harness 0.10), random input, every adapter measured against the same control in the same lock section, and a cell names the fastest adapter at that shape. A cell marked p is a close call, a margin over the runner-up below 15 percent: same-node reruns reproduce to 0.1 percent, so the ranking is stable but the study does not call it a decision, either adapter is acceptable on speed and the other columns decide (registers and spills, the deterministic tie-break cost, code size, the tie_heavy flip list). The tie_heavy part is a second input distribution, a layout-aligned periodic input measured on another node, read as robustness evidence and never as a tie-breaker for the random matrices. The phase 3b part is a separate contract, sorted output verified by the oracle, with its own winners and the price of sorted output over the unsorted winner of the same cell. The GB200 part is the confirmation on the second architecture, compared through the adapters both hosts measured, and the addendum rows are measurements from another node kept out of the one-node tables with the node named. Numbers from the earlier card-based sections 11 to 23 are not comparable with these (harness 0.10 streams every load past L1).

What this is. The phase 3 final evaluation: one arch (B200), one node (umbriel-b200-047), one harness version (0.10), one lease session per scope group on 2026-10-25 (block, then warp and sub-warp), random input on the focus grid plus the flip bands, the named workload cells in their own table, `WINNERS_b200.md` and `state/phase3/winners_b200.json` from `tools/winners.py`. Every cell names the best adapter and its value (latency in cycles per call net of the generate-only control, throughput in Gelem/s for one occupancy wave), then the runner-up with the margin (latency: how much slower the runner-up is, throughput: how much faster the best is), then the margin over the reference of the scope measured in the winner's own lock section at the winner's thread or lane count (`ref`: `B1_reference` at block scope, `W1_reference` at warp scope, `W1_baseline_sub` at sub-warp scope), and in the block `prefer_smaller_index` cells the margin over `B1_det`, the wave 1 deterministic path, as the second reference (`det`). "p" marks a close call, a margin over the runner-up below 15 percent. Nothing in the study settles a close call by speed: same-node reruns reproduce to 0.1 percent, so the ranking on the node is stable and the margin is real, but below the 15 percent the study treats as a decision, so either adapter is an acceptable choice on speed and the choice falls to the other columns, the tie_heavy flip list below (robustness on the layout-aligned tie input), register count and spills, the deterministic tie-break cost (2.12) and code size, with cross-node agreement as the only measurement evidence added (the 48-cell B4_v4 confirmation on umbriel-b200-068, ratios 0.965 to 1.043 and no winner flips, is that kind of evidence). The close-call count per scope is a headline number of the pass. Pending after this pass: the GB200 block cells at N 1024 and 4096, phase 3b (sorted output, whose card tables stay at the end of this section until then), the shuffled variant of the tie_heavy input. These numbers are not comparable with the wave 1 and 2 card numbers of sections 11 to 23 (harness 0.10 streams every load past L1 and its throughput ceilings are the L2 rate, 2.10). The card-based matrices that stood here before the pass are gone, their numbers stay in the approach sections and the ledger (8) records when each part was replaced.

Warp scope (the second lease session on the same node, measured 20:50 UTC, 57 batches with numbers across the warp and sub-warp scopes and 21 empty by construction since the named cells carry only `not_guaranteed` and the sub-warp scope has no flip bands, 316 cells all head-to-head, 9 adapters, zero oracle failures, `W1_reference` the control in every section, committed at 43ce95b3a9). Close calls: 97 of 316 cells. N 32 to 4096 from the focus grid, N 256 to 2048 from the flip bands, the named cells add N 64, 160, 384, 768 and 896. `N5_rows_f` runs in the throughput regime only by design (two rows per call, section 20). `W1_reference` costs 1.3x (fp32 N 32 k 16) to 65x (bf16 N 4096 k 4) the winner's latency, median 9x and the middle half 5.5x to 18x, as its linear cost per element predicts, so the `ref` margins at this scope are large everywhere and the runner-up margin is the one that ranks.

Sub-warp scope (the same session, 60 cells all head-to-head, 4 adapters at 4, 8 and 16 lanes, `W1_baseline_sub` the control in every section). The lane count is a tuning dimension chosen per cell like the block size at block scope: every adapter ran at every lane count the cell allows (lanes at most N) and the label carries the winner's lane count as `@T4`, `@T8` or `@T16`, the runner-up is often the same adapter at another lane count. Close calls: 23 of 60 cells. N 8, 32 and 128 from the focus grid at k 4 to 64, the named cells add N 16 and 64 and k 2. `N5_rows_fs_sub` runs in the throughput regime only by design. The named sub-warp cells ran at 4 lanes only (their per-cell lists hold no other lane count), while 10.2 specifies logical warps of 8 and 16 for Mixtral, DBRX, gpt-oss and the DeepSeek group select, so those rows are the 4-lane answer and not the shape's, an addendum at the specified lane counts on both arches is pending with the evaluation engineer and the rows stay marked until the matrices regenerate.

Block scope (71 batches plus the W4_chunk8 addendum, 446 cells of which 310 head-to-head on random input and 136 on the sorted-runs track, 13 adapters, zero oracle failures, committed at e62f286f62 and c0e67ea6db). The reference behind `ref` is the control of the winner's own section (`tools/winners.py` takes the winner's control value), so a cell won at 512 or 128 threads carries `B1_reference@T512` or `@T128`, and the 512-thread reference is up to 28 percent above the 256-thread one on some throughput cells (verify-B4's self-test), which is why `ref` margins are not comparable across block sizes. Close calls: 144 of the 310 head-to-head cells. Adapters at a thread count other than 256 carry `@T128` or `@T512`, the reference then runs at the same thread count. `B1_det3` had no card at the phase 3 go and is not in this pass, the deterministic cells use `B1_det2`. Four `W4_chunk4` batches were quarantined (a manifest mistake fed 8 runs to the 4-run adapter, which violates its contract and failed the oracle) and re-measured as `W4_chunk8`, whose 64 cells are the runs 8 columns of the sorted-runs table. Four notes from the algorithm researcher's cold review of these tables (no monotonicity break, no requirement gap, no duplicate spread above 0.5 percent): the B2_packed runner-up values at N 16384 and 32768 are a broken-control artifact, since at 64 and 128 items per thread its fully unrolled list leaves the register file (T34, 14.4), so the plus 68 to 100 percent margins of the two-level list over it at N 16384 and the about 530x at bf16 N 32768 k 4 in the per-cell lists are not competitive margins and the next capable form there is B4_v4 or B1_idx at 512 threads. B4_v4's margin over B3_tuned at fp32 N 16384 k 64 and 256 reads 1.74x and 1.78x here against 2.23x on the cards, the difference is the harness 0.10 load path (B3_tuned is a large-shared-memory control whose net dropped, B4_v4 is striped and rose toward its 0.4 value), both same-session under 0.10 so the ranking stands. The 136 single-candidate rows are the W4 sorted-runs track and not head-to-head winners, and one of them is a loss: W4_chunk8 at fp32 N 8192 k 4096 is 28.6 percent slower than B1_reference's unsorted selection (18527 against 13238 cycles, 34 percent lower throughput), the k 4096 merge touches the whole row. B4_v4 has two verified cards (`B4_v4_b200_20261025_1` and `_2`, rerun by verify-B4 on umbriel-b200-018 under harness 0.8), and verify-B4 confirmed its phase 3 cells on a second node under harness 0.10 (umbriel-b200-068, 48 cells across N 8192 to 32768 and k 32 to 1024 in all four quadrants, no winner flips, the B4_v4 ratio 068 over 047 between 0.983 and 1.023 with median 1.000, runner-ups 0.965 to 1.040, B1_reference 0.995 to 1.043, `state/phase3/b4v4_confirm_umbriel-b200-068.md`): the closest margin, bf16 N 32768 k 32 over B2_twolevel, narrowed from 14.0 to 8.6 percent and held, and the fp32 N 8192 k 32 near tie stays with N9_w2fin2 by 0.6 percent on both nodes, both cells close calls.

What the warp pass says. W2_packed_v3 takes 42 of 89 `not_guaranteed` latency cells (median +825 percent over W1_reference and +61 percent over the runner-up): every k 4 to 32 cell at N 32 to 512 except fp32 N 32 k 16 (N10_auto, a close call) and fp32 N 512 k 32 (N6w_atom32), bf16 N 1024 k 8 and fp32 N 1024 k 16 (both close calls, the fp32 one a 1.2 percent tie with W5_v4), bf16 N 2048 k 4 (a close call) and bf16 N 4096 k 4, and every MoE router cell of the named table. W5_v4 takes 21 (median +2320 percent, +51 percent): bf16 N 1024 k 16 to 64 (k 16 a close call), fp32 N 1024 k 8, 24 and 32 (k 32 a close call), k 16 to 64 at N 2048 and 4096 under both key types, and fp32 k 4 at N 2048 and 4096 (the latter a close call). N6w_atom32 takes 20 (median +454 percent, +21 percent): k 48 and 64 at N 256 (bf16 and fp32 k 64 close calls), N 512 from k 48 (bf16) or k 32 (fp32) up to k 256, fp32 N 1024 k 48 (a close call) and 64, fp32 N 2048 k 256, and k 256 and 1024 at N 4096 under both key types (fp32 k 1024 a close call against W3_d4c at +13 percent). W3_d4c takes k 1024 at N 2048 under both key types (close calls against N6w_atom32), W4_wmerge bf16 N 2048 k 256, N10_auto fp32 N 32 k 16 and N 128 k 64 (both close calls, W2 takes the other fp32 small-N cells N10 held on the cards), W1x_vimnmx_eager bf16 N 128 k 64 (a close call). `prefer_smaller_index` repeats the ranking: every warp adapter runs the same code under both requirements (the deterministic over unsorted ratio per adapter between 0.998 and 1.002 on the 70 cell pairs), so the 70 cells differ from the `not_guaranteed` matrix only by what was measured, W2_packed_v3 26, W5_v4 20, N6w_atom32 18. Throughput: W5_v4 takes 34 of 87 (median +674 percent, +43 percent), every N 1024 to 4096 cell from k 4 to 64 (bf16 and fp32 N 1024 k 64 and N 4096 k 4 close calls), bf16 N 512 k 16 and 24 (close calls) and fp32 N 512 k 4 and 16, W2_packed_v3 21 at k 4 to 16 up to N 512 where N5_rows_f and N10_auto do not take the cell, N6w_atom32 15 (fp32 from k 24 at N 256 and 512 and N 128 k 64, bf16 N 512 k 48 and 64 close calls, N 2048 k 256 under both key types), N5_rows_f 12 bf16 cells (N 32 k 4 and 16, N 128 k 16 a close call and k 64, N 256 k 24 to 64 all close calls, N 512 k 32 and 256 close calls, N 4096 k 256 and 1024 close calls), W3_d4c fp32 k 1024 at N 2048 and 4096, N10_auto fp32 N 32 k 16 and N 4096 k 256 (a close call), W1x_vimnmx_eager bf16 N 2048 k 1024 (a close call). The four cells where the `prefer_smaller_index` throughput winner differs are N5_rows_f cells that N6w_atom32 or W3_d4c take by 0.3 to 11 percent, inside the close-call band. Representative latency cells (`not_guaranteed`): bf16 N 256 k 8 W2_packed_v3 329 (W5_v4 +101 percent, reference 5950), bf16 N 512 k 16 W2_packed_v3 829 (W5_v4 +29 percent), fp32 N 256 k 16 W2_packed_v3 660 (N6w_atom32 +61 percent), fp32 N 512 k 64 N6w_atom32 1899 (N10_auto +23 percent), fp32 N 1024 k 16 W2_packed_v3 1768 (W5_v4 +1.2 percent), fp32 N 2048 k 16 W5_v4 1987 (W2_packed_v3 +127 percent), bf16 N 1024 k 64 W5_v4 2957 (N6w_atom32 +21 percent), fp32 N 4096 k 1024 N6w_atom32 66140 (W3_d4c +13 percent). Named workloads: W2_packed_v3 takes every MoE router latency cell (Step-3 N 64 k 4 179 cycles, GLM-4.5 N 160 k 8 258, Qwen3 N 128 k 8 262, ERNIE-4.5 N 64 k 8 287, the DeepSeek-V3 final top 8 N 128 k 8 314, Kimi K2 N 384 k 8 441, DeepSeek-V3 N 256 k 8 467, Qwen3.5 N 512 k 10 602, LongCat N 768 k 12 952, Nemotron 3 Ultra N 512 k 22 1070, Kimi K3 N 896 k 16 1314, the runner-up N6w_atom32 or W5_v4 from +21 to +265 percent) and their throughput except Nemotron (W5_v4 554, a close call), W5_v4 takes the N 2048 cells (NSA 128K context 1986 cycles, W2 +127 percent) and the PEER sub-key throughput at N 1024 k 16 (970) where the latency is W2's by 1.2 percent, N6w_atom32 the k 64 cells (EAGLE-2 rerank N 1024 k 64 3709, the sampling merge N 512 k 64 1899). Against the wave 2 card picture (2.12): the W2 to W5 flip sits at N 1024 (bf16 k 16, fp32 k 8), W2 to N6w at k 48 (N 256 and 512) or k 32 (fp32 N 512), N6w yields the N 1024 bf16 k 48 and 64 cells and the N 2048 k 64 cells to W5, and N10 keeps two close-call fp32 cells. The 97 close calls stay marked in the final matrix.

What the sub-warp pass says. Latency: W2_packed_sw_v2 at 16 lanes takes every bf16 cell from N 32 (N 32 k 4 206 cycles, N 128 k 4 342, k 16 785, k 64 2593, its own 8-lane form the runner-up at +54 to +58 percent for k 4 and 16, W1_eager_same_sub at 16 lanes at +24 and +61 percent for N 32 k 16 and N 128 k 64) and the fp32 k 4 cells (N 32 363, N 128 578), N10_auto_sub at 16 lanes takes the fp32 k 16 and 64 cells (N 32 k 16 500, N 128 k 16 1133 a close call against W2 at +8 percent, N 128 k 64 1262), and W1_eager_same_sub at 4 lanes takes N 8 k 4 under both key types (200 and 219, its own 8-lane form at +10 to +12 percent, a close call). More lanes per row won every random latency cell where the lane count was free, the 16-lane form leads the 8-lane form of the same adapter by 40 to 58 percent. The named cells are 4-lane rows: W1_eager_same_sub at N 8 (Mixtral k 2 292 a close call, the DeepSeek group select k 4 219), N10_auto_sub at N 16 k 4 (DBRX 536) and N 64 k 4 (MoBA 1099, a close call over W2 at +5 percent), W2_packed_sw_v2 at N 32 (gpt-oss-20b bf16 k 4 581, the DeepSeek group top 2 650). Throughput inverts the lane count: W2_packed_sw_v2 at 4 lanes takes bf16 N 32 k 4 and fp32 N 8 k 4 and every named throughput cell (its 8-lane form within 2 percent on the bf16 focus cell), at 8 lanes bf16 N 32 k 16 and N 128 k 4 and fp32 N 32 k 4 (the other lane counts within 1 to 13 percent), at 16 lanes fp32 N 128 k 4 (717 Gelem/s), N5_rows_fs_sub at 16 lanes the bf16 N 128 k 16 and 64 cells (604 and 458) and at 8 lanes bf16 N 8 k 4, N10_auto_sub the fp32 N 128 k 16 and 64 cells at 8 lanes and N 32 k 16 at 16 lanes, every one a close call against its own other lane count or W2. `prefer_smaller_index` names the same winner as `not_guaranteed` on every sub-warp cell measured under both. `W1_baseline_sub` is 1.04x (fp32 N 32 k 16 throughput) to 11.6x (bf16 N 128 k 4 latency) the winner. The 23 close calls are mostly same-adapter lane-count ties, which the implementer decides by the lane count the surrounding kernel already has.

What the block pass says. B4_v4 takes 32 of 77 `not_guaranteed` latency cells (median +160 percent over the reference and +52 percent over the runner-up) and 32 of 75 `prefer_smaller_index` latency cells, every N 8192 and above from k 32 (N 16384) or k 64 (N 8192) up to k 1024, with the two-level B2 list keeping k 4 and 16 at N 16384 and 32768 and B3_tuned keeping k 4096. N 1024 to 4096 is shared by the 128-thread B2 (12 latency cells, k at most 16, and k 32 at N 8192) and N9_w2fin2, the N6 first pass with the packed finish (13 cells, k 24 to 256, many of them close calls against B1_idx, B1_n6b and B1_det2). Under `prefer_smaller_index` B3_tuned takes 15 latency cells (its scan emission is free of the requirement) and the 128-thread B2 and N9_w2fin2 keep the small N. Throughput: B4_v4 24 and 27 cells at large N, B1_idx 20 and B1_idx at 512 threads 9 under `not_guaranteed` and B1_det2 16 under `prefer_smaller_index` at mid N, the 128-thread B2 and the two-level list at small k. Representative fp32 `not_guaranteed` latency: N 1024 k 16 N8_b2packed at 128 threads 1476 (B2_packed plus 10 percent, the reference at 128 threads 2321), N 1024 k 64 N9_w2fin2 1850 (B1_idx plus 2.8 percent, reference 2010), N 4096 k 16 the 128-thread B2 2237 (reference 5012), N 4096 k 256 N9_w2fin2 4003 (B3_tuned plus 4.1 percent, reference 5334), N 16384 k 256 B4_v4 5198 (N9_w2fin2 plus 75 percent, reference 13360), N 32768 k 16 B4_v4 5327 (B2_twolevel plus 63 percent, reference 19415). The sorted-runs track is W4_chunk4's and W4_chunk8's alone (1.3x to 6.2x under the unsorted reference selection at four runs, one loss at eight runs and k 4096), the phase 3b pass sets it against the other sorted routes. The B4_v4 cells at N 8192 and above are confirmed on a second node under harness 0.10 (no winner flips, ratios within 2.3 percent). The 144 close calls stay marked in the final matrix, the tie_heavy flip list below is the first of their secondary columns.

GB200 pass, warp and sub-warp scope (gb200-nvl4-ts2-99, harness 0.10, one session ending 05:31 UTC on 2026-10-26, 57 batches, zero oracle failures, `WINNERS_gb200.md` and `state/phase3/winners_gb200.json` from `tools/winners.py`, committed at 11f4d5c71e, the batches of gb200-nvl4-ts2-105 taken before the network outage set aside in a superseded bucket). Same cells, adapters and controls as the B200 pass, `W1_reference` and `W1_baseline_sub` measured in the same lock section on the same host, so the `ref` margins are GB200 against GB200. Close calls: 257 of 606 cells across the scopes measured. The named sub-warp cells ran at every lane count the cell allows on this host (4 lanes only on the B200 side until the addendum lands), which is where the two tables differ most. The GB200 block pass covers the focus and flip grids at N 1024 and 4096, the named block workloads and the sorted-runs track.

What the GB200 pass says against the B200 pass. The winner is the same adapter at the same thread or lane count in 492 of the 518 cells both hosts measured. Among the warp and sub-warp differences: six named sub-warp cells where GB200 ran every lane count and the 16-lane W2_packed_sw_v2 form wins (gpt-oss-20b bf16 N 32 k 4 206 cycles against the 4-lane form's 581, DBRX fp32 N 16 k 4 328 against N10_auto_sub at 4 lanes 536, the DeepSeek group top 2 at N 32 k 2 302 against 650, MoBA N 64 k 4 469 against 1099, and the group top 2 and MoBA throughput cells at 8 and 16 lanes), eight sub-warp throughput cells at N 128 that are lane-count ties within W2_packed_sw_v2 or N10_auto_sub on one host or the other (bf16 k 4, fp32 k 16 and 64, under both requirements) with one real flip among them, bf16 N 128 k 16, where N5_rows_fs_sub at 16 lanes drops from 604 Gelem/s on B200 to 418 on GB200 and W2_packed_sw_v2 at 8 lanes takes the cell (519 against 483 on B200), and three warp throughput cells that are B200 ties within 5 percent (N5_rows_f against N6w_atom32 at bf16 N 512 k 64, W1_level against W1x_vimnmx_eager at bf16 N 2048 k 1024). Values on the same-winner cells, GB200 over B200: warp latency median 1.000 with 126 of 159 cells within 2 percent, the exceptions the long-list cells at N 2048 to 4096 from k 64 up (W5_v4 bf16 N 4096 k 64 1.104, W3_d4c k 1024 at N 2048 1.08, N6w_atom32 at N 4096 k 256 and 1024 1.04, the generation property of 2.12 and T58), warp throughput median 1.075 with the middle half 1.072 to 1.077 and the `W1_reference` control at the same 1.078, so the uniform gain is the host and not the adapters (one exception, the Kimi K2 router cell at 0.88), sub-warp latency median 1.000 (the Mixtral N 8 k 2 cell at 0.743 the one outlier, 217 against 292 cycles), sub-warp throughput 0.675 to 1.074 with the 16-lane forms at N 128 lower on GB200 (W2 fp32 k 4 0.675, N5 bf16 k 64 0.878) and the 8-lane forms at N 32 higher (1.07). At block scope (the N 1024 and 4096 grids, the named block workloads and the sorted-runs track) the winner is the same in 127 of 142 comparable cells, the differences B1_det2 on GB200 against B1_idx on B200 in 11 cells, B1_idx on GB200 against B1_det2 on B200 in 4 cells, with the latency ratio median 1.000 (67 of 68 same-winner cells within 2 percent) and the throughput ratio median 1.075. The comparison sees each GB200 cell through the adapters the B200 cell measured, the GB200 pass also ran B1_det3 (58 cells), W2_packed_sw_v2@T8 (12 cells), W1_eager_same_sub@T8 (12 cells), N10_auto_sub@T8 (10 cells), which the GB200 matrices above show.

Addendum rows kept out of the one-node matrices above, by the rulings of 2026-10-26: the random matrices stay one-node (umbriel-b200-047), the 3b sorted-check batches are their own pattern and never enter them, and the two addenda measured under the random label on umbriel-b200-068 are shown here with the node named, 122 cells. Each row names the main winner of the pass, the extra adapters with their values and the nodes the cell mixes, and the margin of the first extra adapter over the main winner, which crosses nodes and is read as evidence and not as a verdict (the only scale for a cross-node margin is the B4_v4 confirmation, ratios 0.965 to 1.043 between the two nodes). The B1_det3 rows (110 prefer_smaller_index block cells on the other node, `state/phase3/det3_cells_b200.md`) read as a deterministic variant at B1_det2's speed: within 2 percent of det2 in latency at N 1024 across nodes, and no winner changes hands on their account, the cells where det3 reads ahead of a 047 winner await a same-node measurement before any matrix changes (the earlier reading of 22 wins among the deterministic block cells was a cross-node artifact and is withdrawn). On the sorted-runs track B1_det3@runs4 is a reference column (an unsorted selection over the presorted runs, not the track's contract), not a candidate. The named sub-warp cells at every lane count, latency, with the GB200 single-host winner beside each as the clean evidence: moe_gpt_oss_20b W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 206 (GB200 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 206), moe_mixtral_8x7b W1_eager_same_sub@T8 245 (GB200 W1_eager_same_sub@T4 217), deepseek_v3_group_select W1_eager_same_sub@T4 219 (GB200 W1_eager_same_sub@T4 226), moe_dbrx W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 328 (GB200 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 328), deepseek_v3_group_top2 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 302 (GB200 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 302), moba_32k_b512_top3 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 469 (GB200 W2_packed_sw_v2@T16 469). The cells where an addendum adapter reads ahead of a 047 winner await a same-node measurement as a follow-up item (the rerun requested on 2026-10-26 did not get the pass node), and these rows stay as published until then.

Robustness under heavy ties, the tie_heavy pass on the tie_heavy input as generated (period 64, unshuffled). B200 umbriel-b200-068, harness 0.10, the close cells of the random matrices rerun with the tie_heavy pattern of the harness battery, v[j] = (j mod 64) / 64 without a shuffle, so 64 distinct values each N / 64 times and the top k one tie class for every k up to N / 64, 490 cells: warp 162, sub-warp 42, block 286, 189 of them with a runner-up within 15 percent. This is a second input distribution and not a tie-breaker for the random matrices, whose close-call marks stand. The matrices below name the best adapter on this input per cell in the format of the random matrices, with the same controls. The tie_heavy manifest carries 17 adapter labels the random pass did not run (listed at the end of the reading paragraph), which stay in these matrices and are kept out of the flip list.

What the tie_heavy pass says, on the input as generated. Of the 404 cells with a random counterpart, 247 keep their winner and 157 change it. The pattern is layout as much as ties: at 128, 256 and 512 threads the period 64 divides the stripe, so every striped thread holds 64 copies of one value and four threads of 256 hold only zeros, while the blocked tile of B3_tuned sees every value once per thread. B4_v4's exact-bound prefilter degenerates on it: the minimum of the per-thread maxima is the tile minimum, every item is a candidate, the count exceeds the 4096-word buffer from N 8192 and the tile fallback runs, 1.10x behind B3_tuned at fp32 N 8192 k 32 (11404 against 10400 cycles), 1.23x at fp32 N 16384 k 32 (19153 against 15517, 4.1x its own random time), 2.72x at bf16 N 32768 k 32 (55578 against 20404, with spill), and within 4 percent of its random time at N 1024 to 4096 where the tile fits the buffer. The guard is correct and the fallback's cost is the loss (T67, TK-S07.2). B1_n6b takes the bf16 N 1024 k 32 to 256 cells (1566 to 1704 cycles, 0.71x to 0.82x of its random time) by its exact-count exit: the fixed-point image bins each of the 64 values separately, 16 items per bin at N 1024, every grid k but 24 is a multiple of 16, so the pivot bin is exact after one pass, and at k 24 it costs 1.33x its random time (T68). Reading flags: a ratio across the two inputs mixes a pattern-wide slowdown (strictly_above_k is 0 in every N 16384 cell, so every radix select runs its maximal pass count, B3_tuned 1.85x and the B1_reference control 1.45x their random time) with each adapter's own premium, the period 64 aligns with the stripe, k against the multiplicity 16 decides the N 1024 rows, the prefer_smaller_index rows of B1_n6b, N9_w2fin2 and B4_v4 carry spills (896 to 2020 bytes), and the tie_heavy rows come from umbriel-b200-068 while the random rows come from 047 (B4_v4 agrees across the two within 2.3 percent). On the sorted-runs track under heavy ties B1_det3@runs4 stands as a reference column on 50 cells (an unsorted selection over the presorted runs, not the track's contract), at a median 1.13x of W4_chunk4's time. The shuffled variant of the same multiset (tie_heavy_shuffled, 166 cells, umbriel-b200-094) confirms the layout reading: with the period broken B4_v4 reads 1.15x its random time at fp32 N 16384 k 32 against 4.14x on the input as generated and stands 3.50x ahead of B3_tuned there, B3_tuned reads 2.22x its random time, and B1_n6b at bf16 N 1024 k 32 to 256 reads 0.75x its random time against 0.75x on the input as generated, so the tie_heavy flips above are evidence about the layout-aligned input and the shuffled matrices below are the robustness reading for ties alone. Adapter labels measured on tie_heavy input only, kept out of the flip list: B1_det2@T128, B1_det2@T512, B1_det3, B1_det3@T128, B1_det3@T512, B1_det@T128, B1_det@T512, B1_idx@T128, B1_n6b@T128, B1_n6b@T512, B2_packed@T512, B2_twolevel@T128, B2_twolevel@T512, B4_v4@T128, B4_v4@T512, N9_w2fin2@T128, N9_w2fin2@T512.

Robustness under heavy ties, the shuffled variant (tie_heavy_shuffled, the same multiset through a shuffle so the period no longer aligns with the stripe, 166 cells, umbriel-b200-094). The pass as generated above is partly a layout test, these tables are the fairer tie-flood reading, and the study keeps both. The matrices name the best adapter per cell on this input, the flip list compares with the random winners of the same cells under the same restriction as above.

Sorted output, the phase 3b pass (B200 umbriel-b200-047, harness 0.10 with the sorted check in the oracle, 18 batches on 2026-10-26, the focus grid, every adapter that declares sorted output or has a documented post-sort: W5_v4, W1_level, W1x_vimnmx_eager and W4_wmerge at warp scope, W1_eager_same_sub at sub-warp scope, B6_radix at block scope, with the W4_chunk4 merge of four presorted runs measured in the same pass and reported in the sorted-runs table above since its input contract differs, and the N5 forms measured but not sorted routes since their contract is unsorted output). The sorted check is the harness battery with the order verified, keys non-increasing along the slots under both requirements and equal keys in ascending index order under prefer_smaller_index. Each cell names the fastest adapter whose sorted output passed, the next passing adapters, the price of sorted output as the margin over the random-pass winner of the same cell (the unsorted matrices above), and the adapters the check refuted or that do not support the shape. The winners file lists the warp and sub-warp cells of this pass as the pattern random@sorted, the block cells of B6_radix come from the batch files. These 3b tables replace the card tables that stood here until version 29.
