What the 3b pass says. Warp: W5_v4 is the fastest sorted route from N 512 at k 4 to 64 under not_guaranteed and for bf16 under both requirements (bf16 N 512 k 16 1067 cycles against the unsorted W2_packed_v3 829, N 2048 k 64 4323 against its own unsorted 3877, N 4096 k 64 10051 against 7789), the W1 level network at N 32 and 128 and at k 256 and 1024 where W4_wmerge does not apply, and W4_wmerge at k 256 and 1024 from N 512 (fp32) or N 2048 (bf16) and at fp32 N 512 and 2048 k 64 under prefer_smaller_index. W5_v4's fp32 sorted output under prefer_smaller_index is refuted as built: at k 16 and 64 of N 512, 2048 and 4096 the check finds equal keys inside the top k in buffer-position order instead of index order (quantized_random and relu_quantized rows, slots 13 and 14 at N 512), because the fp32 finish word replaces the low 7 to 9 twiddled key bits by the inverted buffer position and the tie rewrite covers only the class that straddles the k-th slot. The selected set is exact, the bf16 route is exact and sorted by construction, and W5_v4's unsorted winners stand. The fix is in the emission, the tie rewrite generalized to every class run inside the top k, which also closes a latent ordering hole under not_guaranteed when two distinct keys agree in the kept bits (not observed in the pass, a host simulation puts such a pair in 1 to 16 percent of rows at k 16 to 64). Sub-warp: the W1 network is the only sorted route (W1_eager_same_sub, at 16 lanes on most cells, 4 or 8 lanes at N 8, 220 to 5335 cycles), the N5 forms are unsorted by contract. Block: B6_radix is the only sorted route from an unsorted row, a full block sort whose price over the unsorted winner runs from 2.0x (bf16 N 4096 k 256 against B3_tuned) to 95x (bf16 N 32768 k 4 against B2_twolevel), unsupported at fp32 N 32768 where its oracle kernel needs 270 KB of dynamic shared memory against the 227 KB opt-in limit (a harness item: an unlaunchable oracle kernel is counted as a failure where an unlaunchable regime counts as unsupported), and the merge of four presorted runs stays the cheapest sorted route where the input arrives as runs (the sorted-runs table). Status per adapter in the pass (cells, the N5 failures are the contract mismatch and the B6 failures the unlaunchable shape): B6_radix 144 passed, 12 failed, 0 unsupported, N5_rows_f 0 passed, 38 failed, 38 unsupported, N5_rows_fs_sub 0 passed, 34 failed, 34 unsupported, W1_eager_same_sub 136 passed, 0 failed, 0 unsupported, W1_level 152 passed, 0 failed, 0 unsupported, W1x_vimnmx_eager 76 passed, 0 failed, 38 unsupported, W4_chunk4 72 passed, 0 failed, 36 unsupported, W4_wmerge 42 passed, 0 failed, 52 unsupported, W5_v4 60 passed, 6 failed, 40 unsupported.


The pre-phase-3 picture (the card matrices of wave 1 and 2 and the paragraphs that read them) was removed from this section when the passes landed, the card numbers stay in sections 11 to 23 and the ledger (8) has the dates.

## 17. N8, block size as a tuning dimension of the block primitives (wave 2)

Status of every number: opt-N8's cards, B200 umbriel-b200-020 (sm_100, nvcc 13.4.92, harness 0.4, 2026-10-25), 904 cells with a passing oracle (13 cases per shape) and the control in the same lock section. All seven cards verified by verify-N8 (2026-10-25, by 17:50 UTC), rebuilt from the cards' commits with resources identical in every cell: `N8_b1idx_b200_20261025_1` (umbriel-b200-068, 96 of 144 cells, the N 4096 and 8192 cells not rerun), `N8_b2packed_b200_20261025_1` (068 for N 1024 and 2048 with the kill cell 1422, 1182, 1380 identical on both nodes and the 64-thread throughput 819 against 494 Gelem/s, the N 4096 cells on the card's node), `N8_b2packed_b200_20261025_2` and `N8_b3tuned_b200_20261025_1` (068, full grids, within 3 percent), `N8_b2packed_b200_20261025_3` (the two-node repeat, confirmed by an independent holder on a third node, umbriel-b200-018, which matches 047 and 068 so that three of four B200 nodes agree and 020 alone is slow), and `B2_twolevel_b200_20261025_2` and `B1_n6b_b200_20261025_2` verified after opt-N8's amendments: both are accurate records of umbriel-b200-020 (reproduced there within 1.25 percent), but the same 128-thread two-level binaries run 7 to 51 percent faster on 068 and the 255-register N6b kernels 3.5 to 6.4 percent faster, so the verdicts that flip on a healthy node are marked node dependent in the findings and below. Two precision corrections from the verifier: the fp32 round fit holds within 5 cycles at N 1024 only (71.8 cycles at both 64 and 128 threads at N 2048 against fits of 88 and 80), and 1024 threads lead 512 by 1 to 2 percent at bf16 N 32768 k 1024, so "never wins" reads "never by more than the noise". umbriel-b200-020 is retired (10.6, T58). Absolute latencies at N 8192 and above carry the harness chain-16 artifact (2.10), the ratios inside a lock section do not. Report: `$SWARM/approaches/N8/REPORT.md`, catalogue T43, T44, T45, T46 (correction to T43), T50.

### 17.1 What it is

Not a new primitive. The wave 1 block primitives B2_packed, B2_twolevel, B1_idx, B3_tuned and B1_n6b measured at block sizes other than the 256 threads of the focus grid, through three thin adapters that widen the support predicates and inherit the primitives unchanged (`N8_b2packed` with 64 threads allowed and the list capped at 64 words, `N8_b1idx` with 64 and 1024 threads, `N8_b3tuned` with 128, 512 and 1024). B2_twolevel and B1_n6b already accepted 128 and 512 threads. The harness launches any block size that divides N. The idea card had two halves: fewer warps per block make the B2 round cheaper (a 2-warp block predicted at a 58-cycle round and 1060 cycles at bf16 N 1024 k 16), and more threads per block halve the per-thread sweep of the radix passes at N 16384 and 32768 where the 256-thread kernels sit at 255 registers. The first half failed, the second held, and the one-wave throughput regime turned out to invert the latency verdict.

### 17.2 Contract

Unchanged per primitive (sections 13 to 15). Thread counts: B2_packed 64 to 512 with IPT at most 64 for both key types, B1_idx 64 to 1024, B3_tuned 128 to 1024, B2_twolevel and B1_n6b 128 to 512. `prefer_smaller_index` stays free for B2 (within 1.0 percent of `not_guaranteed` at every block size) and for B3 (within 0.3 percent at 512 threads, verified), and unsupported by B1_idx. The block size changes which warp posts or scatters an item, not the result set.

### 17.3 Where the time goes

- The B2 round against warps per block W (T43, T46). Round slopes from consecutive k at bf16 N 1024: 83.5 cycles at 8 warps and IPT 4, 69.5 at 4 warps and IPT 8, 77 at 2 warps and IPT 16 (fp32 within 5). N 2048: 91, 78, 80 at IPT 8, 16, 32 (bf16 only, the fp32 round at N 2048 is 71.8 at both 64 and 128 threads against fits of 88 and 80, verify-N8). The six bf16 rounds fit about 48 + 4 W + IPT, and the hardware researcher's list-free rounds on umbriel-b200-091 (barrier 15.9, 20.0, 28.0, 44.5 cycles at 2, 4, 8, 16 warps, the aggregated round 61.7, 65.7, 74.5, 91.5) read the fit as barrier (12 + 2 W) plus a flat 46 for the post and read back plus about IPT for the list shift, whose IPT minus 1 predicated selects issue in program order before the next post. Halving the warps saves 4 cycles and doubling the list costs 8 back, so 2 warps gain nothing over 4. At 16 warps the fit undershoots: 175 to 203 measured against 128 to 176 predicted (the 44-cycle barrier plus 16 posts serialized on one slot, open).
- The B2 prologue against warps per block. The sort of IPT words costs 43, 74, 102 cycles for sort4, sort8, sort16 (256, 128, 64 threads at N 1024), 92, 200, 585 for sort8, sort16, sort32 at N 2048, about 3080 for sort64 at 512 threads and N 32768. A 4-warp block issues its sort with one warp per scheduler and is 1.4x to 1.8x faster per thread than an 8 or 16-warp block on the same network (540 against 950 for sort32). The prologue decides the ties toward 128 threads at N 1024 and toward 512 threads for k 4 at N 8192.  (cards `N8_b2packed_1` and `_2`, verified).
- The radix pass against threads per block (T45, verified). B1_idx's pass is a dependent sweep of IPT predicated shared atomics per thread, a 256-bin scan and two barriers, the index-only epilogue one shared atomic with return per selected item serialized per warp. At N 32768, 512 threads halve the sweep (64 items), run 16 warps per SM at the same one block per SM and split the epilogue over twice the warps: 1.24x to 1.36x bf16 and 1.32x to 1.45x fp32 in latency, growing with k, 100 to 103 registers and 0 spill against 248 to 250 (so the gain is not removed spills). 1024 threads quarter the sweep but run the scan on a quarter of the threads with 32-warp barriers under a 64-register cap: 2 to 12 percent behind 512 at N 32768 (one cell, bf16 k 1024, 1 to 2 percent ahead, inside the noise) and far behind at smaller N.
- Throughput at equal threads per SM (T44, verified on 068: 819 against 494 Gelem/s at bf16 N 1024 k 16). 32 blocks of 64 threads, 16 of 128 and 8 of 256 all put 2048 threads on an SM, and the 64-thread B2 processes 1.2x to 1.66x the elements per second of the 256-thread one at N 1024 to 2048 (B1_idx 1.2x to 1.6x). The per-SM work is identical, so the effect is the barrier wait: a 2-warp block's round waits for one other warp and 32 independent blocks interleave, an 8-warp block idles 8 warps until its slowest arrives. The gain goes away where the register tier drops the block count (fp32 at 128 registers and above).

### 17.5 Ideas not yet tried

- 384 and 768-thread blocks for B1_idx at N 4096 to 8192, where 512 ties or loses (the harness takes any multiple of 32 that divides N).
- The device-agent question: the one-wave regime says 64 threads at N at most 2048 and 512 from N 16384 with the input reloaded from L1 or L2 per call. A `segmented_topk` run with the block size as a parameter would confirm it for a kernel that loads each tile once.
- B1_det and B2_twolevel_any were not swept. A 512-thread B1_det doubles its per-warp tie table and halves its slots.
- B3's compaction threshold is per items per thread, with the block size free it may want to be per N.
- A two-level list at 64 threads (shift from 15 to 3 selects) recovers at most the 8 cycles of the shift and would still trail 128 threads.

### 17.6 Determinism and ordering

Unchanged per primitive: B2 inherent (same binary for both requirements at every block size, within 1 percent), B3 inherent (within 0.3 percent at 512 threads, verified), B1_idx `not_guaranteed` only at every block size, N6b measured under `not_guaranteed` only. The block size changes which warp posts or scatters an item, not the result set, and B2's sorted output is unchanged.

### 17.7 Open items

- Still single node: the 64-thread k 64 kernels of card 1 (7736 and 11320 instructions, the N 4096 cells of that card were rerun on 020 only). The 512-thread kernels of card 2 and the 512-thread B1_idx are on two nodes (verify-N8 found the 248 to 250-register 256-thread radix kernels 2 to 3 percent faster on 068). The 128-thread two-level and the 255-register N6b kernels are node dependent (17.4), so every 020 latency number of those two cards carries the caveat and the final evaluation re-measures them on a healthy node.
- The 16-warp B2 round (175 against the list-free 91.5 at 16 warps) stays open, the hardware researcher's micro at 64 to 512 threads would separate the 44-cycle barrier from the 16 serialized posts.
- The two-node repeat found the node term (T50), the mechanism is with the hardware researcher (2.10).
- Absolute latencies at N 8192 and above were measured under harness 0.4 and carry the chain-16 artifact (2.10), to be re-measured under 0.6 in the final evaluation.
- The W5 and N7 cards of section 18 were verified on their own node only, the N8 cards on two or three nodes.

## 18. W5, threshold filter with a certified lower bound and a packed W2 finish, with N7, the two-level per-lane warp list (wave 2)

Status of every number: opt-W5's cards, B200 umbriel-b200-028 (sm_100, nvcc 13.4.92, 2026-10-25), every oracle passed, every latency fit R squared 1.0000, canary 32.001 cycles per op at both ends of every batch, controls in the same lock section. verify-W5 (2026-10-25 16:00 to 16:10 UTC, umbriel-b200-028, the optimizer's node, adapters rebuilt from the cards' commits) verified `W5_v4_b200_20261025_1` (the W5 result), `W5_base_b200_20261025_1`, `W5_v2_b200_20261025_1` and `N7_twolevel_b200_20261025_1` to the cycle on every latency cell, variant and control, with both kill criteria holding (bf16 N 1024 k 32 1837 against 2481, fp32 N 2048 k 16 2293 against 4391) and `prefer_smaller_index` identical to `not_guaranteed` with identical instruction streams. `W5_v3_b200_20261025_1` is refuted on contract, not on numbers: its cells reproduce to the cycle, but its flag used the sign bit of a minus t after W2's twiddle had mapped minus zero to plus zero, so items equal to minus zero were dropped when the threshold was plus zero, failing the exact index check in 32 of 32 signed-zero seeds under `prefer_smaller_index` (v2 and v4 pass all 42 cases in the same session, 2.10, harness 0.7). No second-node run exists for these cards. The phase profiles of 18.3 come from `approaches/W5/phase_probe.cu` and `phase_probe3.cu`, dependent clock stamps per phase on one warp outside the harness under the GPU lock, the only numbers in the W5 report that are not harness batches. Report: `$SWARM/approaches/W5/REPORT.md`, catalogue T48 and T49 (contributed), T1, T3, T4, T7, T12, T17, T18, T19, T21, T28, T32, T36, T41 (adopted). Phase 3b (2026-10-26, 16 and 2.10): W5_v4's fp32 sorted output under prefer_smaller_index is refuted as built (equal keys inside the top k in buffer-position order, six cells at k 16 and 64 of N 512 to 4096), the selected set exact, the bf16 route sorted by construction, the unsorted winners unchanged, the fix a generalized tie rewrite in the fp32 emission.

### 18.1 What it is

W5. Every lane holds IPT striped items. A certified lower bound on the k-th largest key comes from grouped lane statistics: the 32 lanes form G groups of 32 over G adjacent lanes, every lane selects its J largest keys with a small network, the group's J largest are formed with log2(32 over G) xor-shuffle merge steps, and the threshold is the warp minimum (one redux) of the groups' J-th largest. Because G x J is at least k, at least k keys are at or above the threshold, so the filter never under-selects and there is no retry. One compare per item gives the per-lane candidate count, log2(IPT + 1) ballots of the count bits give the exclusive prefix over lanes and the total, every lane writes its candidates as one run into a shared buffer of 32 x D words, and after a `__syncwarp` lane t reads positions t, t + 32 and so on, so each lane holds at most D candidates. The candidates are packed into W2's unique word (bf16 twiddled key above the inverted source index, fp32 the top 32 minus log2(32 D) key bits above the inverted buffer position, N4), sorted per lane, and W2's packed rounds (one redux per round, T19, T41) emit the k largest. fp32 keeps W2's next-word tie check and resolves the tie class of the k-th truncated key on full keys from the buffer. When more than 32 x D candidates pass (tie floods, periodic patterns, never on random input) an exact bit-serial radix select over the register tile with ballot-ticket emission in index order takes over. (G, J, D) per k from the host simulation `sim_gj.py` (4000 trials per cell, candidate counts nearly independent of N): k at most 16 G next_pow2(k), J 1, D 4 or 8. k 17 to 32: 16 x 2, D 8 (80 candidates on average, 198 to 212 max). k 33 to 64: 16 x 4, D 8 (126 mean, 241 to 259 max). k 65 to 128: 16 x 8, D 16 (208 to 214 mean, 354 to 388 max). `seed_check.cu` confirms that none of the 16 measured inputs of any cell overflows (worst 185 of 256 at k 64, 284 of 512 at k 128). Variants: `W5_base` packs every item before the filter with serial count and position chains, `W5_v2` filters on raw float keys and packs only the gathered candidates with a tree count, `W5_v3` removes every per-item predicate (sign-bit flags, integer count tree, unconditional stores to a per-lane dummy slot), `W5_v4` makes the bf16 gather predicate free (minus zero normalized at load by one `FADD`, compare-free twiddle, unconditional loads masked by the sign of p minus tot).

N7. W2's rounds on a two-level per-lane list: a sorted head of 8 words and an unsorted remainder, the prologue sorting block 0 and merging every further block of 8 into the head with a write-back merge so the remainder holds exactly the words not in the head. Rounds run in blocks: at the top of block b every lane tests whether its head was below full at the end of block b minus 1 and still has remainder words, one `__any_sync` decides, and the refill (every remainder block sorted again and merged into the head) runs under that warp-uniform branch after the block's rounds. Three builds: block loop unrolled (n7a), rolled with blocks of 4 (n7b), rolled with blocks of 2 (n7c, the card).

### 18.2 Contract

W5: warp of 32 lanes, striped input in registers, N a power of two from 256 to 65536 (bf16) or 32768 (fp32), k in [4, 128] with 2k at most N, keys bf16 or fp32, int32 indices derived from the position, both unsorted requirements by the same code (the finish word and the fallback tickets are `prefer_smaller_index` by construction), output k indices in descending key order on the filter path (fp32 by truncated key with the tie class of the k-th key rewritten in key then index order), ticket order on the fallback. Temp storage 32 x D words (bf16) or pairs (fp32), 512 bytes to 4 KB per warp. Sync: redux, ballot and shuffles over the full warp mask and one `__syncwarp`, no barriers. NaN as W2, minus zero equals plus zero. N7: as W2_packed_v3 (blocked input, no temp storage, `redux.sync` and one vote per block of rounds), IPT at least 16, k a multiple of 4 in [4, 256].

### 18.3 Where the time goes

- The structure of the base cost (W5_base): the round is 27 cycles with the list of 4 or 8 at every N (T19, T41), the fixed part about 750 at IPT 16, 1190 at IPT 32 and 2100 at IPT 64, about 27 cycles per item per lane. SASS of the bf16 throughput kernel at N 1024 k 8: 1624 instructions against 664 for W2 (about 600 of them the cold fallback), the bf16 pack 8 instructions per item, the count and the scatter position two serial IADD chains of IPT steps.
- Removing instructions did not move the latency (W5_v2): 300 fewer instructions per call, latency within 5 percent either way (bf16 N 1024 k 32 2279 against base 2162 and W2 2481), throughput up 4 to 34 percent, the fixed cost still 23 cycles per lane item. On one warp the filter is latency bound.  (card `W5_v2`, verified).
- Phase profile of v2 at bf16 N 1024 k 32 (cycles, mean over 256 chained calls): loads 260, per-lane top 2 188, group merge and threshold 79, compare and count 314, ballot prefix 187, scatter 341, syncwarp and gather 446, sort 70, rounds 863 (total 2748). N 2048 k 32: 450, 326, 75, 836, 193, 465, 452, 74, 840. Compare-and-count and scatter cost about 10 cycles per lane item for 3 to 5 instructions per item (predicate serialization, 2.10), the gather 430 to 520 cycles for 8 loads at every N (the drain of the 32 scattered stores before the first load may issue).
- v3 profile at the same cell: loads 258, top 2 162, threshold 80, count 98 (v2 314), prefix 216, scatter 332 (v2 341), gather 440 (v2 446), sort 74, rounds 837, total 2497. The scatter costs about 10 cycles per shared store instruction at every IPT whether or not the stores are predicated (184 to 191 for 16 stores, 332 to 352 for 32, 611 to 668 for 64), the bf16 gather 430 to 530 against 170 to 220 for fp32 (W2's twiddle with a compare per gathered word plus the position predicate), which v4 attacks. Registers rose to 128 at N 1024 and 249 to 255 at N 2048 (v2 72 and 128 to 168) because the unconditional scatter lets ptxas hoist the flag and address arithmetic of every item.
- v4: the bf16 cells drop 190 to 400 cycles against v3 (N 1024 k 32 1837 against 2116, k 64 2936 against 3303, N 2048 k 32 2485 against 2675), fp32 unchanged (its gather was already predicate free). Stacked at bf16 N 1024 k 32 with W2_packed_v3 at 2481 in every session: base 2162, v2 2279, v3 2116, v4 1837. Registers 64, 127 to 128 and 254 to 255 at N 512, 1024, 2048, no spill, no local memory. What is left of the W5-specific fixed cost: the shared store instructions (32 at IPT 32, about 10 cycles each), the per-lane top 2 (160), the ballot prefix (216).  (card `W5_v4` against `W5_v3`, verified).
- N7's round: 45 to 74 cycles against 27 for W2's pruned list in the card build (slopes over k: bf16 N 1024 k 16 to 32 47, k 32 to 64 71, k 64 to 128 59). The refill is rare, the rest is block control, one vote, two taken branches and runtime round indices per block of 2 rounds (T49). Registers 49 to 62 (bf16), 64 to 102 (fp32 N 512 to 1024), 168 to 210 at fp32 N 2048 where W2's slow path keeps the keys live.

### 18.5 Ideas not yet tried

- The shared store instructions of the scatter (about 10 cycles each on one warp, 32 per call at IPT 32, 64 at IPT 64, the same whether predicated or unconditional): two candidates per 64-bit store when the lane's run start is even, or a fused count-and-scatter into per-lane private runs with a prefix-free gather, would halve or remove them.
- The per-lane top 2 (160 cycles at IPT 32) by a cheaper pair tree, the ballot prefix (216) by fewer count bits (a per-lane count above 15 means the 16 x 2 bound is loose and could go to the fallback).  (phase probe numbers of 18.3).
- Registers: a per-chunk ordering pin (T24) to give the throughput regime its occupancy back (128 at N 1024, 255 at N 2048 against 72 and 128 for v2).
- N7: two blocks per loop iteration with the refill out of line, or a head of 16 with blocks of 4, to bring the round from 45 to about 35, and a same-session A/B against W5 at fp32 N 1024 k 32 and N 2048 k 16.
- A second rung of the fallback (the exact k-th largest of the certified set, then refilter) to keep tie floods on the fast structure. Two bf16 keys per word in the filter (HSET2 compares, T18). N 4096 cells (IPT 128) against W1_reference. An `sm_100f` variant with `redux.sync.max.f32` for the bound (saves the twiddle of the per-lane bound, 18 cycles, T2, not worth a card).

### 18.6 Determinism and ordering

Exact by construction for base, v2 and v4: the finish orders by the unique word, the fallback emits ties in index order. Both requirements run the same code and measured identical cycles (W5_v4, 12 pairs, identical instruction streams per verify-W5). Output in descending key order on the filter path. N7 inherits W2's ordering. v3 is the exception: its sign-bit flag against a twiddled threshold breaks the tie semantics at zero (2.10), which is why v4's load normalization is part of the contract and not an optimization.

- W5_v4 is the sorted route at warp scope up to k 64 (section 23, verified): in W4's session W5_v4 beats the warp truncated merge sort 1.9x to 3.4x at k 64 (bf16 N 1024 2926 against 5416, N 2048 4177 against 9036, fp32 4773 against 11342 and 5532 against 19146), and W4 takes over from k 256 where W5 has no cells.
- Against N6 at warp scope (section 22, verified on B200, the numbers identical to the GB200 cards): at k 64 W5_v4 is ahead at N 1024 (2936 against 3682) and N 2048 (4163 against 11854), N6w is ahead at N 256 to 512 (1879 against W5_v4's 2556 at N 512 across sessions) and owns k 128 and above where W5 was not measured, both against the same W2 control in their sessions. Cross-session on one arch now, a same-session pairing on one B200 belongs to the final evaluation.
- A second-node run of the four verified cards (verify-W5 ran on the optimizer's node, the lead decided against it before the final evaluation). The harness 0.7 signed-zero battery passed on `W5_v4` in all 36 supported cells of the focus grid under both requirements (umbriel-b200-018, 23 cases per cell, 2.10), as it did on W2_packed_v2 and v3, B2_tiecheck and both references, zero oracle failures anywhere.

- The phase profilers run outside the harness and are not batches.
- The W2 numbers W5 was measured against include the v2 fp32 regressions of 12.4 (fp32 N 2048 k 16 4391 in the W5 session against 3948 for `W2_packed` in the W2 card), so the fp32 N 2048 k 16 margin over the best W2 form is about 1.7x.

## 19. N10, fp32 keys-only network with index recovery by ballot rank (wave 2)

Status of every number: opt-N10's cards, B200 (sm_100 build, x86 docker), CUDA 13.4.92, 2026-10-25, oracle battery passed on every cell before any number was read, control in the same lock section. Batches 1 and 2 on umbriel-b200-020, batch 3 (the combined adapter) on umbriel-b200-028. All nine cards (`N10_u32`, `N10_u32_trunc`, `N10_u32_ltrunc`, `N10_ge1_trunc`, `N10_ge1_ltrunc`, `N10_ge1_trunc_sub`, `N10_ge1_ltrunc_sub`, `N10_auto`, `N10_auto_sub`, each `_b200_20261025_1`) were verified by verify-N10 on umbriel-b200-068 (a second node, 2026-10-25 by 17:45 UTC, rebuilt from the cards' commits, resources identical in every matched cell, the oracle battery with the all_equal cells passed): every headline latency identical to the cycle (661 against 934 and 720 at N 64 k 32, 960 against 1918 and 1649 at N 128 k 16, 1341 against 4650 and 4547 at N 256 k 64, the router shape 1409 against 5299 and 4189), throughput over the u64 word 1.40x to 2.11x, N10_auto ahead of the best `not_guaranteed` route in all 13 warp cells in both regimes, the exact tie recovery confirmed by reading the code and the host model, T51 and T52 confirmed in one lock section with the note that T51's per-stage saving is 23 to 40 cycles at two cells rather than 30 to 35. One node caveat on four LW 4 N 128 cells of `N10_auto_sub` (chains of 2500 to 15000 cycles) where the controls read 7 to 19 percent faster on 068 while the variant stays within 5 percent, ratios 0.225 to 0.231, 0.353 to 0.417 and 0.618 to 0.689, direction unchanged. Numbers are `prefer_smaller_index` latency cycles per call unless marked, the same code and cycles serve `not_guaranteed` (within 0.04 percent in every measured pair). Report: `$SWARM/approaches/N10/REPORT.md`, catalogue T51 and T52 (contributed), T1, T4, T7, T17, T28, T34, T36, T37, T40, T42 (adopted).

### 19.1 What it is

The fp32 `prefer_smaller_index` route for the W1 family without an index in the network. The fp32 key is twiddled into an order-preserving u32 word (sign flip, minus zero mapped to plus zero) and the words run through opt-W1's template recursion with the min and max exchange (`approaches/W1/warp_level_topk.cuh`, `word_ops<uint32_t>`, T42): one shuffle and two predicated `VIMNMX` per shuffle stage, no index moves. The network's only job is the k-th largest word t. Three forms get it: `N10_u32` runs the eager tile loop with the complete final merge and shuffles position k minus 1, `N10_u32_trunc` stops the final merge of tile 0 after the half-cleaner stages with stride at least k, so positions [0, k) hold the k largest words as a bitonic sequence and t is their minimum by one `redux.sync.min` over the full warp with the identity in the other lanes (T37, T40, T51), `N10_u32_ltrunc` is the same truncated merge on the level tile order. Then every lane compares its original words (kept in registers) against t and the indices are recovered by ballot rank in slot-major index order (slot i of lane l is source index i x LW + l), so `prefer_smaller_index` is exact by construction: the two-class recovery (`N10_u32*`) ballots "above t" and "equal to t" per slot and popc prefixes place the items above t in index order and the tied items after them until k, the one-ballot recovery (`N10_ge1_*`) ballots "at least t" per slot and the prefix rank is the slot when the group holds exactly k such words (the random fp32 case), with a warp-uniform branch (one any-vote at sub-warp scope) into the two-class pass otherwise (T52). `N10_auto` combines the measured best per cell at compile time: truncated merge always, level order from four tiles, two-class recovery up to IPT 2, one-ballot recovery above. Controls: `W1_eager_same` (the fp32 u64 index-in-key word, the must-beat), `W1_minmax_pairs` (the best `not_guaranteed` fp32 route), `N10_ctrl_u64rec` (the u64 word in N10's own recursion, isolating the code structure) and `W1x_vimnmx_eager` on bf16 (the identical u32 network with the index packed, the network-only cost).

### 19.2 Contract

Input: N fp32 keys striped over a logical warp of LW lanes (4, 8, 16, 32) in registers, the index of item i of lane l is i x LW + l (the harness's index array is not read). MaxK = max(LW, next_pow2(k)) divides N and N over MaxK is a power of two. Output: the k selected source indices, unsorted (items above t in index order, then the tied items in index order). Requirements: `not_guaranteed` and `prefer_smaller_index`, same code, same cycles. Temp storage none. Synchronization: shuffles, ballots and one redux on the full warp mask, no barrier. Contract assumption: all lanes of the hardware warp call the primitive together (the logical warps of a sub-warp instantiation run in lockstep, the assumption T37 and T40 make and the harness guarantees), which a CUB header would have to state. NaN twiddles above plus infinity (positive sign) or below minus infinity (negative sign) and is selected or dropped deterministically by its sign bit, minus zero and plus zero are one word. The truncated merge applies to a power of two k, other k take the complete merge and the shuffle. Sorted output is not available from the truncated forms.

### 19.3 Where the time goes

Measured decomposition at warp scope, `prefer_smaller_index`, batch 1 on umbriel-b200-020, all in one session:

| Cell | Network only (bf16 `W1x_vimnmx_eager`, the same u32 network) | Plus twiddle, shuffle of t and two-class recovery (`N10_u32`) | Truncated merge (`N10_u32_trunc`) | Level order (`N10_u32_ltrunc`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| N 32 k 4 | 512 | 570 | 504 | 504 |
| N 32 k 16 | 512 | 570 | 442 | 442 |
| N 64 k 32 | 695 | 815 | 661 | 678 |
| N 128 k 16 | 1017 | 1238 | 1106 | 953 |
| N 128 k 64 | 912 | 1110 | 933 | 952 |
| N 256 k 16 | 1718 | 2105 | 1966 | 1422 |
| N 256 k 64 | 1322 | 1704 | 1566 | 1435 |

- The network costs what it costs on bf16 words (512, 695, 1017, 1718 cycles at N 32 to 256 for k at most 32), the twiddle is three ALU operations per item before it.  (card `N10_u32` against `W1x_vimnmx_eager`, batch 1, verified).
- The two-class recovery after a complete merge costs 58, 120, 221, 387 cycles at IPT 1, 2, 4, 8 (about 48 per slot from IPT 4 on, well above the pipelined-ballot model of T17, because the SASS interleaves the second ballot of each slot with the popc and add chains of the earlier slots: two `VOTE`, four `POPC`, two `LOP3`, four `IADD`, one `ISETP`, one `IMAD`, one predicated `STS` per slot, issue bound on one warp).
- Truncating the final merge saves 30 to 35 cycles per skipped shuffle stage in the card (23 to 40 at two cells in verify-N10's rerun, two stages at k 4, four at k 16, five at k 32, six at MaxK 64) and replaces the shuffle of position k minus 1 by a redux of the same cost: 66, 128, 154, 132, 177, 139, 138 cycles in the rows above (T51).
- The level order gains nothing at one or two tiles (0 to 17 cycles of schedule noise), 13 percent at four tiles (N 128, 953 against 1106) and 27 percent at eight (N 256, 1422 against 1966), as the codegen experiment predicted for one word per tile (11.4, R12's open question, confirmed on fp32 words). At sub-warp LW 8 and 16 N 128 it is worth 28 to 37 percent.
- The one-ballot recovery (batch 2, `N10_ge1_trunc` against `N10_u32_trunc`): plus 58 to 65 cycles at IPT 1 and 2 (the branch, the popc sum and the reconvergence cost more than the two ballots saved), minus 53 to 81 at IPT 4, minus 104 to 129 at IPT 8. On the level order at N 256 the one-ballot form wins 10 to 12 percent latency and 23 percent throughput (339 to 344 against 276 to 280 Gelem/s).
- Price of excess ties at rank k (patterns pivot_tie and all_equal against random, one-ballot form): N 64 k 16 781 against 747, N 128 k 16 1149 against 1053, N 256 k 16 2126 against 1862, 34 to 264 cycles or 5 to 14 percent when every item ties, the two-class form measures the same on every pattern. Random fp32 never takes the tie path (ties at rank k 1.0 in every measured cell).
- Registers 30 to 64 in the latency kernel at warp scope (`W1_eager_same` 24 to 59), 23 to 40 in the throughput kernel, occupancy 8 blocks per SM up to N 128 and 6 at N 256 for the one-ballot forms (8 for the two-class forms). Sub-warp N 128: 79 (LW 16), 118 (LW 8), 168 (LW 4, 32 items per lane) in the latency kernel, throughput occupancy 4, 2, 1, one 32-byte stack frame with 11 `STL` at LW 4 N 128 k 4 in the level form (ptxas reports it as 84 spill bytes), no local memory anywhere else (T36 check).

| Item | Verdict | Card, status |
|---|---|---|

Both must-beat cells pass (815 at N 64 k 32 against a threshold of 840, 1238 at N 128 k 16 against 1720). The recovery is 58, 120, 221, 387 cycles at IPT 1 to 8 against the card's 110 + 8 IPT model, cheaper at IPT 1, dearer from IPT 4 | `N10_u32`, verified |

Tie cost plus 34, 96, 264 at N 64, 128, 256, zero for the two-class form. The reconvergence region does not hurt the network (ptxas keeps the eight ballots back to back). Warp scope against the u64 word 0.84x, 0.74x (N 32 k 4, 16), 0.80x, 0.75x, 0.71x (N 64 k 4, 16, 32), 0.52x to 0.43x (N 128 k 4 to 64), 0.34x to 0.29x (N 256 k 4 to 64), throughput 1.40x to 2.1x, against the `not_guaranteed` pairs 0.99x to 0.27x. Sub-warp against the u64 word LW 16 0.65x to 0.24x, LW 8 0.69x to 0.18x (N 128 k 8: 1409 against 5299), LW 4 0.87x to 0.23x, N 8 1.06x to 1.23x (a loss). Against the sub-warp pairs LW 16 and 8 0.82x to 0.25x, LW 4 N 32 k 4 and 8 1.20x and 1.28x (a loss), LW 4 N 128 0.73x to 0.35x. The fp32 `prefer_smaller_index` route of the W1 family at warp scope for N 32 to 256 and at sub-warp scope from N 32 (LW 8, 16) or N 128 (LW 4), and the fastest fp32 route measured under either requirement there | `N10_auto`, `N10_auto_sub`, verified (134 of 138 sub-warp cells to the cycle, four LW 4 N 128 cells with the node caveat) |

The W1 family's u64 word is 1.6x to 2.2x slower than the bf16 u32 word at N 128 to 256 (two shuffles and a compare-select chain per stage), N10 removes that gap and the tie problem at once | batch 1 sections s4 and s5 |

### 19.5 Ideas not yet tried

- An asm barrier between the ballot block and the popc block of the two-class pass, to reach the pipelined-ballot cost (up to 100 cycles at IPT 4 on that form, which the combined adapter no longer uses there).  (an estimate from the 19.3 decomposition).
- Sorted output by one shuffle per selected item into the network positions (needs the complete merge), for the sorted-output pass.
- Sub-warp LW 4: a two-step shuffle-xor min inside the segment (about 72 cycles) instead of eight pipelined full-warp redux (about 88, T40), and two slots per ballot word at LW 4 and 8.
- Register cut at IPT 16 and 32 (sub-warp N 128): recompute the twiddled originals after the network or run the recovery slot by slot without the ballot arrays.
- Two rows per call (the N5 rows arrangement) for throughput, the recovery is per row. `sm_100f`: `redux.sync.min.f32` would allow the native float network without the twiddle (T2). A GB200 cross-check of the recovery (the network is host independent by T42, T20 asks for a same-host comparison first). A `prefer_larger_index` mirror (lanes above me in the rank), not built.

### 19.6 Determinism and ordering

Deterministic: a fixed network and a rank pass with no atomics. `prefer_smaller_index` is exact by construction and costs the same as `not_guaranteed` in every measured cell (within 0.04 percent, same instantiation). The price of the requirement relative to the best `not_guaranteed` fp32 route of wave 1 is negative at every measured warp cell (0.99x to 0.27x of the pairs) and at every sub-warp cell except LW 4 N 32 k 4 and 8 (1.20x, 1.28x) and N 8 (1.27x to 1.53x). Output order: class then index, not sorted by key.

### 19.7 Open items

- A GB200 run and the device router kernel remain unmeasured, every B200 claim is now on two nodes.
- The two-class pass is issue bound at about 50 cycles per item per lane in the measured SASS, the pipelined-ballot model predicts half, a scheduling barrier is untested.
- N 8 and LW 4 N 32 lose to the pairs network, the route table should keep the pairs there under `not_guaranteed` and N10 under `prefer_smaller_index`.
- Registers at sub-warp N 128 (118 at LW 8, 168 at LW 4 in the latency kernel, throughput occupancy 2 and 1) are a register-tier risk for batched router kernels, the device kernel has to be measured (BRIEF section 4). The u64 word of wave 1 sat at 167 registers in the same cell.
- The eager one-ballot kernel's throughput loss at N 256 (272 against 339 for the level order at the same occupancy) is unexplained, a schedule effect of the T28 kind.
- The card schema's `input_pattern` had no `all_equal` value when the cards were written (added in harness 0.6), the all_equal cells of the tie sections are quoted in the findings and dropped from the cards.

## 20. N5, two bf16 rows per lane in bf16x2 words, one SIMD compare-exchange for both (wave 2, throughput)

Status of every number: opt-N5's six cards (`N5_rows`, `N5_rows_f`, `N5_rows_fs_sub`, `N5_split`, `N5_split_f`, `N5_split_f_sub`, each `_b200_20261025_1`), B200 umbriel-b200-018 (sm_100 build, nvcc 13.4.92, harness 0.5 with rows per group, 2026-10-25), every oracle passed (13 battery cases per cell, both requirements), canaries 32.001 cycles per op and 3779 to 3781 us in every lock section, controls in the same lock section. All six cards were verified by verify-N5 on the cards' node umbriel-b200-018 (latency to the cycle, throughput within 0.2 percent), rebuilt from the cards' commits in a separate source tree, one lock section per batch with canaries at both ends: `N5_rows_f` (all 52 cells, plus a same-session run against the card's reference `W1_eager_same`, 480.1 against 267.5 Gelem/s at N 128 k 32, so the 1.4x threshold holds at 1.80x in one section), `N5_split_f` (all 44), `N5_rows_fs_sub` (all 72), `N5_rows` (32 of 52, N 32 and 64 not rerun), `N5_split` (20 of 44, k 4 and 16 not rerun), `N5_split_f_sub` (32 of 72, the N 64 rows and k 4 not rerun). The per-row oracle was shown live with planted-duplicate probes, the index recovery read and found sound, and the harness load ceiling never binds (at most 0.33 of the no-op ceiling). The card's reference control `W1_eager_same` was re-measured in session (268 Gelem/s at N 128 k 32), the stronger control is the T42 one-word min and max network `W1x_vimnmx_eager` (358). Report: `$SWARM/approaches/N5/REPORT.md` (section 9 has every table), catalogue T53 (contributed), T7, T17, T18, T28, T34, T36, T37, T40, T42, T47, T52 (adopted). Phase 3b (16): the N5 forms are not sorted routes by contract (the greater class is emitted in lane order), the sorted check fires on every bf16 row by design, fp32 is unsupported, the 37 unsorted throughput cells stand.

### 20.1 What it is

The W1 eager bitonic top-k network (opt-W1's template recursion, the min and max exchange of T42) run on 32-bit words that hold two bf16 keys, one per 16-bit half. Every stage of a sorting network compares position i with position j of the same sequence, so the halves never interact: `max.bf16x2` and `min.bf16x2` (one `HMNMX2` each) are the compare-exchange for both halves at once and one shuffle moves both. The network is keys only and the indices are recovered afterwards from the k-th key of every half (the greater keys placed by one inclusive lane scan, the tie class filling the remaining slots, in index order when the requirement asks for it). Two fillings: `N5_rows` runs two independent rows of N keys per call (the harness 0.5 `packed_rows` arrangement, the batched MoE router shape), `N5_split` splits one row into its even and odd items and finishes with one more bitonic merge on one-key words (a drop-in for the single-row route). Policies: exchange `ops_bf16` (the SIMD calls inside the ternary, a predicated `HMNMX2` pair, T53), `ops_sel` (both results then a select), recovery `recover_exact` (ballot rank for the tie class in every call) and `recover_fast` (both classes by the lane scan, the exact ballot rank only when the tie class overflows the remaining slots, a hardware-warp-uniform branch). The `_f` adapters are the fast recovery, `_fs` the select exchange with the fast recovery.

### 20.2 Contract

bf16 keys only (two per word, fp32 does not fit, N10 is the fp32 analogue). Input in registers, `packed_rows` (two rows) or `packed_striped` (one row), N = LW x IPT with MaxK = round_up(k, LW) dividing N (rows) or N over 2 (split) and a power-of-two tile count, k in [1, N over 2], logical warps 8, 16, 32. Output: k indices per row in shared memory, unsorted (greater keys in lane order, then the tie class), both unsorted requirements with the same code, `prefer_smaller_index` exact by construction. Temp storage none. Sync: shuffles, ballots and one any-vote over the full hardware warp, so the whole hardware warp is in the call (T37, T40, T52). Minus zero orders below plus zero in the network (`max.bf16x2`, T18) and the recovery classifies by IEEE comparison, and the selected set is the oracle's in every case (the signed-zero battery passes under both requirements). NaN is never selected (with fewer than k non-NaN keys output slots stay unwritten). Registers 30 to 32 at N 32 (8 blocks of 256 per SM), 36 to 48 at N 64 to 128 (5 to 6), 48 to 64 at N 256 (4 to 5), no spills, no local memory.

### 20.3 Where the time goes

- Throughput kernel at N 128 k 32, SASS per call against the one-word control (336 instructions for one row): the two-row kernel issues 75 shuffle stages and 153 `HMNMX2` as the control issues 75 shuffles and 153 `VIMNMX`, plus about one to two direction predicate instructions per stage (`ISETP` 67, `LOP3` 47 against 5 and 24 hoisted in predicate registers) and the index recovery (8 `HSET2`, a lane scan, about 20 per word), 528 instructions exact or about 500 executed fast. Per row that is about 250 to 265 instructions against 336, 1.3x, and the measured gain is 1.28x to 1.33x at this cell. T47 says why the gain is not the 2x of the shuffle and exchange count: min, max, `LOP3` and `HSET2` all issue at half rate and the stage is pipe bound from two warps per scheduler, so the rebuilt direction predicate and the recovery's `HSET2` and `LOP3` land on the exchange's pipe. The recovery is 90 to 100 instructions per row at N 128 and about 180 at N 256, a third of the per-row budget.
- The split form saves shuffles (48 against 75) and exchange instructions (82 `HMNMX2` against 153 `VIMNMX` for one row) but pays the one-word final merge (about 20 instructions on half-empty words) and the recovery, and lands at 1.03x to 1.09x at N 128 and 1.16x to 1.26x at N 256 against the T42 control.
- Latency (chain slope, one warp): the two-row call costs 1.3x to 1.6x one one-word row (774 to 2367 against 512 to 1718 at N 32 to 256), 0.65x to 0.8x per row, the split call 1.1x to 1.5x the one-word row below N 256 and 0.92x at N 256 k at most 32. Neither is a latency primitive at warp scope, as the card predicted. At sub-warp scope the two-row call beats the control's one-row call in latency from N 64 on (1.14x to 2.06x per call at LW 8 and 16), the one scope where the card's "no latency gain" was wrong: the sub-warp control is a chain of short in-register and shuffle stages whose count the SIMD word halves.

| Exchange codegen, three source forms (static SASS of a 32-stage chain, `approaches/N5/micro/exchange_forms.cu`) | u32 reference 128 instructions, bf16x2 with the SIMD calls inside the ternary 144 (predicated `HMNMX2` pair plus about half an instruction per stage of predicate rebuilding), both results then a select 152, the predicate inside one asm block far worse (424 against 352 in the N 128 k 32 split kernel), operand negation never folded. On the GPU: select form 445 against ternary form 458 Gelem/s at N 128 k 32 (control 358 in both sections), the ternary form adopted at warp scope at the price of 16 more registers and three fewer blocks per SM. At sub-warp instantiations (LW 16, IPT 8) ptxas turns the ternary into four instructions per stage word, so the select form is the sub-warp line (T53) | `N5_rows` (b1 select form, b2 ternary form), verified (458.2 against 358.2 at N 128 k 32 in the rerun) |
| Direction words (`net_dir`, lane-bit parities precomputed once per call) | No change in the instruction count (600 against 592 at N 128 k 32, `ISETP` 90 in both): the predicate work is the if-conversion's, not the direction arithmetic's. Dropped, not measured on the GPU | none |
| Index recovery, exact against fast (batch 2) | Fast wins 4 to 8 percent from N 128 on (N 128 k 32 477 against 458 Gelem/s, N 256 k 64 452 against 417) and ties below. The fast form compiles different fallbacks and the `prefer_smaller_index` kernel carries 3 to 16 more registers and 2 to 4 percent lower throughput in most cells (up to 9 percent at LW 8 N 128) from code that is never executed (T28) | `N5_rows`, `N5_rows_f`, verified |

The ternary form is 5 to 6 percent slower at N 128 with 14 more registers at LW 16. The split form reaches 1.07x to 1.61x in throughput and 1.16x to 2.63x in latency. ### 20.5 Ideas not yet tried

- T51's truncated final merge (stop at stride k, the per-half minimum by two integer reductions on twiddled halves, about 10 instructions against about 18 saved), 2 to 3 percent at k 32, more at k 64.
- A one-class "at least t" pass as in T52's one-ballot form, replacing the two `HSET2` per word by one when the group holds exactly k keys at least t, 4 to 8 instructions per word.
- `__launch_bounds__` or `-maxrregcount 32` to restore 8 blocks per SM for the ternary form (T47 expects a small gain, the stage is pipe bound from 2 warps per scheduler).
- Twiddled u16x2 keys (`VIMNMX.U16x2`, same pipe and rate) with integer compares in the recovery, minus the `HSET2`, plus the minus-zero canonicalization at the input.
- fp32: two fp32 keys do not fit one register, N10 is the fp32 analogue. The latency regime at warp scope: no gain expected and none found.

### 20.6 Determinism and ordering

Deterministic (fixed network, fixed recovery order). Output unsorted: the greater class in lane order, the tie class in lane order (fast path) or index order (overflow and exact paths). `prefer_smaller_index` is exact at zero cost in the exact form (identical throughput to 0.1 percent) and at a 2 to 4 percent register and schedule cost in the fast form (never-executed fallback code). Sorted output is not provided (it would cost one shuffle per selected key).

### 20.7 Open items

- One node (umbriel-b200-018) for cards and verification, the SM-level costs transfer by T16 and the chain minima are data oblivious, a second node was not asked for.
- The direction predicate rebuilt per stage by the if-conversion (about half an instruction per stage on the half-rate pipe) is the one codegen cost left between the bf16x2 exchange and the u32 shape, a ptxas peephole question, not code.
- Register growth of the ternary form (32 to 48 at N 128) and the occupancy drop to 5 blocks per SM, a maxrregcount build would show whether throughput hides there.
- Sub-warp codegen: the four-instruction phi form at LW 16, and the harness's sub-warp kernels carry a `BRA.DIV` fast and slow copy of the call body (variant and control alike), so sub-warp instruction counts are twice the executed path.
- GB200 not measured.

## 21. B4, exact-bound prefilter, compaction and a small finish at block scope, with N9, the packed W2 finish on N6's pivot bin (wave 2)

Status of every number: opt-B4's eight cards (`B4_base_b200_20261025_1` against B3_tuned, `_2` against B1_idx, `B4_v2_b200_20261025_1` against base, `B4_v3_b200_20261025_1` against v2, `B4_v4_b200_20261025_1` against v3, `B4_v4_b200_20261025_2` against B3_tuned, `N9_w2fin_b200_20261025_1` and `N9_w2fin2_b200_20261025_1` against B1_n6b), B200 umbriel-b200-047 (sm_100, nvcc 13.4.92, harness 0.4, 256 threads, 2026-10-25), eight batches, every oracle passed (13 cases per cell, both requirements), control in the same lock section. verify-B4 (2026-10-25, 348 cells on umbriel-b200-018 and the cards' node 047, harness 0.8 against the cards' 0.4, the 22-case battery passed on every cell, the bound's exactness and the fallback confirmed by reading): `B4_base_2` (against B1_idx, 2.34x fp32 and 1.94x bf16 at N 32768 k 256), `B4_v3_1`, `B4_v4_1`, `N9_w2fin_1` and `N9_w2fin2_1` verified, every B4 value within 1.9 percent and both nodes within 1.5 percent. `B4_v4_2`, `B4_base_1` and `B4_v2_1` refuted on a harness-version effect, not on B4: since harness 0.6 the no-op control launches with the variant's dynamic shared memory, so B3_tuned's net latency at fp32 N 16384 and 32768 is 9 to 11 percent lower than the 0.4-era cards show, and the 0.8 wrapper compiles the bf16 N 4096 latency kernels to 119 instead of 80 registers with a striped-load no-op slope of 654 instead of 254 cycles (a 0.4 build on the same node reproduces the cards to 0.1 percent). The B4 numbers and every conclusion reproduce, throughput within 0.6 percent. Current margins under harness 0.8 (the card values in parentheses as history): fp32 N 32768 k 256 7026 against 18278 (2.60x, card 2.87x), bf16 N 32768 k 256 8145 against 18369 (2.26x, card 2.37x), fp32 N 16384 k 64 2.00x (card 2.24x), bf16 N 16384 k 64 2.09x (card 2.12x), N 8192 k 64 1.32x and 1.38x (card 1.35x and 1.42x), the kill cell of B4_base 1.94x (card 2.15x), B4_v2 at bf16 N 4096 k 16 1.34x (card 1.46x). The three cards are amended with the 0.8 ratios in their findings (merged at 3e09890223) and under the lead's harness-version ruling of section 9 verify-B4 flipped them to verified at 6940547756, so all eight B4 and N9 cards are verified, the three with the current-version ratios recorded next to the card's own. Absolute latencies at N 8192 and above in the cards carry the harness chain-16 artifact (2.10), ratios inside a section do not. The phase profiles come from clock64 stamps inside the primitive (`approaches/B4/profile_phases.cu`, median of 32 calls on one block, about 15 percent above the harness numbers), not from harness batches. Report: `$SWARM/approaches/B4/REPORT.md`, host simulation `sim_bound.md`, catalogue T56 and T57 (contributed).

### 21.1 What it is

The minimum over the threads of the per-thread maximum is an exact lower bound on the k-th largest key for every k up to the thread count (the thread maxima are k or more distinct items at or above it), and it costs one max per item, two `redux.sync` per warp and the barrier the histogram zeroing needs anyway (the N6 bracket, T25). On random input about 1500 items of the tile are at or above it at every N from 4096 on (31 percent of N 4096, 4.7 percent of N 32768, host simulation and GPU agree), so B4 replaces the first histogram pass of a radix or bucket selection (one shared atomic per item) by a compare sweep that compacts the candidates into shared memory in thread order and runs the selection over a few candidates per thread: one pass over the fixed-point image of the candidates (N6's digit over the bracket, 256 bins) leaves a pivot bin of 2 to 24 items for k 16 to 256, which one warp resolves exactly by redux rounds on (key, inverted index) words (the W2 round, T19), while the items above the pivot bin are emitted by position. For k above the thread count the per-thread items are split into m groups and the bound is the minimum over all group maxima, accepted at run time only when the candidate count reaches k. Overflow (tie floods, all-equal input, a blocked-sorted tile) or a short count fall back to the same pass machinery over the register tile on the key bytes. Four builds of the same algorithm: `B4_base`, `B4_v2` (per-slot structure fixed after a phase profile, T56), `B4_v3` (value-domain sweeps, single-pass ticket emission), `B4_v4` (branch-free atomics in the pass and the emission).

N9 is one change to B1_n6b's primitive (`block_topk_n6_w2.cuh`): when the pivot bin after the first pass holds at most 32 items and the pass did not end the selection, the items above the pivot bin are scattered by ticket and the pivot bin finishes on warp 0 by the redux rounds instead of a second pass over the tile. Two builds differing in the ticket spelling (constant-address tickets, then B1_idx's class-indexed counter), the same-kernel A/B T39 asked for (T57).

### 21.2 Contract

Input: a full striped tile of N = 256 x IPT keys in registers, fp32 or bf16, k in [1, N) at run time with a compile-time hint for the group count and the buffer size, select the largest. Output: k source indices in the caller's shared array, unspecified order. Requirements `not_guaranteed` and `prefer_smaller_index` in one code path (the tie-class exit differs). Temp storage: two 256-bin histograms, the bin prefix, scan storage, bracket and reduction partials, the dummy words (v4), a 32-word pivot buffer and the candidate buffer of 4096 words (bf16, key and inverted index in one word) or 4096 pairs (fp32) for k up to 256, 8192 up to k 1024, 16384 above, never more than N: 22.9 KB (bf16) and 39.3 KB (fp32) at k up to 256 and N 32768 (B1_idx 2.1 KB, B3_tuned 69.7 KB). Sync: `__syncthreads` inside (bracket, count scan, compaction, three per finishing pass, one before the warp finish, more in the multi-pass emission and the lazy tie test), the caller separates consecutive calls by a barrier. N up to 32768 (16-bit count fields), 128 and 512 threads accepted and untested. The striped arrangement is a requirement: sorted input compacts a striped tile to exactly 256 candidates at every N and a blocked tile to N minus IPT plus 1 (fallback). NaN as the sign bit orders it, the all-ones pattern collides with the warp finish's sentinel (unsupported). Signed zero: equal in the value-domain sweeps, folded onto plus zero under `prefer_smaller_index`, kept distinct under `not_guaranteed`. The input keys are left intact. N9: B1_n6b's contract (13.2).

### 21.3 Where the time goes

Phase profile (cycles, clock64 stamps, bf16 N 4096 k 16 and fp32 N 32768 k 256, base then v2 then v3): prologue and bracket 412, 420, 360 and 1366, 1371, 462. Candidate count sweep and block scan 557, 669, 758 and 1577, 1747, 2472. Compaction store sweep and barrier 294, 470, 265 and 1771, 3078, 1892. Candidate loads and image 226, 717, 976 and 212, 805, 692. First pass 1708, 898, 920 and 1753, 950, 989. Emission 3863, 2027, 1108 and 4260, 2729, 1792. Warp finish about 150 and about 300. Totals 7199, 5306, 4452 and 11495, 11153, 8904. Three facts decided the iterations:

- In the base build every candidate slot was its own uniform branch (`if (j < slots)`) around a body of 36 SASS instructions, so the finish ran 16 dependent chains back to back whether the slot was live or not, about 240 cycles per slot (T56). Grouping the slots by four, computing the image once per candidate at load time and dropping the min and max tracking atomics halved the pass and the emission (v2).
- The three register sweeps at IPT 128 cost 4700 to 6200 cycles in every build, about 10 cycles per item slot for 7 to 10 instructions per item, an issue rate of about one instruction per four cycles per warp at two warps per scheduler, half of the nominal. v3 removed the twiddle sweep (1400 to 1800 cycles) by comparing fp32 values and storing raw bits, the floor of the sweeps is about 3300 cycles at IPT 128 with this instruction count, and fewer instructions per item (packed bf16 compares, T18) is the only lever left.
- After v2 the structure was the cost: four block scans at 350 to 430 cycles each (cub's BlockScan), nine barriers, a divergent branch per slot around the candidate loads (700 cycles for 16 loads) and around the emission tickets (1100 to 1800 cycles in v3). v3 took each selected item's position from the exclusive bin prefix plus a per-bin ticket, v4 made the tickets and the pass atomics branch-free (every slot issues its atomic, to a private dummy word when it takes no part, T32's proposal): the branch-free pass saves 200 to 270 cycles and the branch-free emission costs 170 to 270 more (sixteen `atomicAdd` with return per thread, ten of them to dummy words, the returned value on the critical path of the store), so T32's dummy word pays for fire-and-forget histogram increments and not for tickets with return.

Cost model after four builds: about 3300 cycles of finish (candidate loads, one pass with its scan and barriers, the emission, the warp finish) plus about 37 cycles per item of the tile for the three register sweeps, so B4 is 3300 plus 37 x IPT and crosses B1_idx and B3_tuned between N 4096 and N 8192. Registers 61 to 64 at IPT 4, 125 to 128 at IPT 16, 128 to 153 at IPT 32, 254 to 255 from IPT 64 (B1_idx 32, 47, 64, 250), spill 1.4 to 1.6 KB at IPT 128 in v3 and 2.5 KB under `prefer_smaller_index` (the index passes of the fallback are a third pass body in the same function), one block per SM from IPT 64 against B1_idx's two.

The host simulation (`sim_bound.py`, 300 rows per cell): the m1 bound's candidates are set by the 256 maxima and hardly depend on N (mean 795 at N 1024, 1300 at N 4096, 1530 at N 32768, p99 1900 to 2700, maximum 2100 to 3400), a buffer of 4096 never overflowed on random, quantized or outlier rows and 2048 overflows 0.3 to 10 percent. Tie floods and relu_quantized at k 1024 and above put the whole tile at or above the bound (fallback). For k above 256 the half-group bound (m = ceil(k over 512), valid when the count reaches k) leaves 3300 items at N 32768 and was never short of k in 300 rows. The float image leaves a pivot bin of 1.5 to 3 items at k 16, 7 to 12 at k 256, 28 to 62 at k 1024 to 2048 (a second pass in half of the rows), an integer offset digit collapses whenever the bracket spans the sign.

### 21.5 Ideas not yet tried

- Packed bf16 sweeps on the `packed_striped` arrangement (`HMNMX2` maxima and `HSET2` compares halve the count and max sweeps and hold 128 keys in 64 registers, T18), predicted about 1000 cycles at N 32768 and two blocks per SM at IPT 64.
- The fallback out of the fast path's function (a `__noinline__` tile fallback or a re-read of the keys from the caller's memory), to take the index-pass and key-pass bodies out of the register allocation (255 registers at IPT 64, 1.4 to 2.5 KB of spill at IPT 128).
- A custom block scan (warp shuffle scan, warp totals in shared memory, one barrier, about 230 cycles) for the count scan and the pass scan in place of cub's BlockScan (350 to 430 here).  (the 21.3 phase profile.)
- The deterministic path's 4 to 17 percent: T65's per-candidate lookup (B1_det3) names B4's emission as in scope, and T66's pinned shared address applies to its tickets.
- The exact per-thread m-th largest as the group bound when IPT is small, a 2048-word buffer with the fallback at 0.3 to 10 percent of the rows, 128 and 512 threads (accepted, unmeasured), the W2 finish on a 64-word buffer for k 1024 to 2048, and N6 and B4 as one primitive (N6's first pass for IPT at most 16, B4's compaction above, the warp finish in both).

### 21.6 Determinism and ordering

Set-deterministic and index-deterministic by construction in every build: the items above the pivot set are an exact set, the pivot set is resolved by the warp finish (key descending, index ascending), by the tie-class rule or by index passes. `prefer_smaller_index` is the same code path with two differences (the minus-zero fold at the candidate load, the index passes instead of "any k_rem" for a tie class larger than the warp finish) and costs 4 to 17 percent, all of it register pressure from the third pass body. Output order unspecified, sorted output a post-sort of k. N9 inherits N6's determinism and replaces the warp-redundant rank under `prefer_smaller_index`.  (the 4 to 17 percent is card `B4_v3`, verified.)

- All eight cards verified (three under the harness-version ruling with the 0.8 ratios recorded next to the card's own), the B4 values stand on two nodes.
- The same-session comparison with B1_idx exists for B4_base only, the later variants chain through it, and the k 16 cells against B2_twolevel were not measured in one session.
- One node (umbriel-b200-047), one lease for the whole session, builds on the node's CPU while earlier batches measured, every batch from a committed worktree state (`harness/SOURCE_COMMIT`), one profiler run of the base build outside the lease with no batch running.
- The harness measures whole calls, the phase stamps found every structural cost of this lane (T56 records the method), a per-phase option in the harness would make this cheaper for every optimizer.

## 22. N6 at warp scope, equi-width bucket select with a shared atomic histogram, with W3 (warp radix select) as its fallback and N1 (bit-serial select) as the one-bit row (wave 2)

Status of every number: opt-N6w's seven cards (`N6_w_ballot_gb200_20261025_1`, `N6_w_v2_gb200_20261025_1`, `N6_w_atom32_gb200_20261025_1` against N6w_v2, `N6_w_atom32f_gb200_20261025_1` against N6w_atom32, `N6_w_atom32w2_gb200_20261025_1` against W2_packed_v3 (the card of record), `W3_d4c_gb200_20261025_1` against N6w_v2, `N1_bitserial_gb200_20261025_1` against W2_packed_v3), all GB200 gb200-nvl4-ts2-105 (sm_100 build, aarch64 host, enroot container, nvcc 13.4.92, harness 0.4, 2026-10-25), one warp per group, random normal input, canary 32.001 cycles per op at both ends of every batch, controls in the same lock section, the oracle battery passed on every measured cell under both requirements. All seven cards were verified by verify-N6w on umbriel-b200-028 (B200, x86 docker), rebuilt from the cards' commits with the harness of those commits, latency identical to the cycle between the archs in every cell up to N 2048 (worst delta 0.3 percent, the N 4096 local-memory cells 3 to 4 percent faster on the B200 for variant and control alike, ratios within 4 percent), throughput 6 percent lower on the B200 for variant and control alike, direct GB200 reruns of `N6_w_atom32` and `N6_w_atom32w2` on the cards' node also to the cycle, SASS counts identical on the x86 and aarch64 builds (T16 holds, no T20 gap for ballot, redux, shuffle, atomic and min max code), the T59 step reproduced (3001 against 4631 at bf16 N 1024 k 16), the harness 0.8 battery with the five signed-zero seeds passing on all 120 headline cells of the final adapters, and the T60 failure of the v1 card reproduced on the B200 with the card's exact failure texts and fixed from v2 on. The GB200 numbers below are therefore B200 numbers as well, and section 16 places them in its warp tables. No B200 node was free during the optimizer's own session. Report: `$SWARM/approaches/N6w/REPORT.md`, host simulation `sim_warp_bins.md`, host model of the device arithmetic `model_device.py`, catalogue T59 and T60 (contributed), T1, T4, T5, T7, T9, T10, T17, T19, T21, T24, T25, T28, T30, T32, T33, T36, T38, T41 (adopted).

### 22.1 What it is

One state serves all three approaches: a warp-uniform range of order-preserving key images (the twiddled float, bf16 keys keep their 16 bits in the high half), the number n of candidates inside it and the number r still needed. A pass refines the range and subtracts the candidates above the new range from r, items above the range are selected and items below dropped without per-item state, and the final emission compares every item's image against a threshold and writes the selected items at their rank in index order (one ballot per item slot), so `prefer_smaller_index` costs nothing beyond the tie-rank ballots that run only when a tie class is cut.

- N6 at warp scope (`N6w_ballot`, `N6w_v2`, `N6w_v3`): bracket [lo, hi] with hi the warp maximum and lo the minimum over lanes of the lane's m-th largest key (m = ceil(k over 32) rounded to a power of two, exact for k at most 256, the full range above), one pass of 32 equal-width bins over the bracket (per item one `FADD`, one `FFMA` with the 2^23 constant and two `IMNMX`), the histogram by five ballots per item (ballot variants) with a 5-step shuffle suffix scan to the pivot bin and two redux for the pivot bin's exact image range. Finish: all candidates when n equals r, index rank when the pivot bin is one tie class, else the packed W2 rounds on at most 64 (IPT at most 16) or 128 compacted (image, inverted index) words in shared memory, else another bracket pass over the candidates' exact range (at most four), then radix digit passes as the last resort.
- `N6w_atom32`, `N6w_atom32f`, `N6w_atom128`: the same with the histogram counted by one fire-and-forget shared atomic per item into a per-warp histogram of 32 or 128 words (data-dependent addresses, items outside the range add to a per-lane dummy word so the atomic stays unconditional, T32). `atom32f` trims the finish (13 fewer instructions per item, rounds unrolled by four).
- W3 (`W3_d4c`, `W3_d5c`, `W3_d4`): digit passes over the key image from the top with 4 or 5 bit digits, one ballot per digit bit plus one for the candidate flag, lane j counts bucket j, the same suffix scan, early exit when the pivot bucket holds exactly r, with compaction (`c`) of the candidates to shared memory in index order once they fit N over 4 slots.
- N1 (`N1_bitserial`): one pass per key bit, one ballot per item of (candidate and bit set), the popcounts summed, no scan, no shared memory.

The host simulation (`sim_warp_bins.md`, 200 rows per cell) predicted and the GPU confirmed the decisive statistic: with the exact bound the pivot bin after one 32-bin pass holds 8 to 11 items at k 64, 12 to 21 at k 128, 24 to 39 at k 256 (max 61) at N 256 to 4096, so the packed W2 finish runs on at most 64 compacted candidates in 85 to 97 percent of the rows with about n over 2 rounds and the rest exit whole. k 1024 with the full bracket leaves 175 to 300 items and needs the second bracket pass, which leaves 6 to 11.

### 22.2 Contract

Input blocked in registers (lane t holds items t IPT to t IPT + IPT minus 1), N a multiple of 32 up to 32768 (the scan packs two 16-bit counts into one shuffle word, W3 with compaction needs N at least 128), k in [1, N over 2], keys bf16 or fp32 (widened to fp32 in registers, minus zero folded onto plus zero), int32 indices from the position. Output k indices in the harness's shared array, unsorted (index order for N6's exit paths, W3 and N1, items above the pivot bin in index order then the finish's descending key order for N6's W2 path). Temp storage: the ballot variants 256 or 512 bytes per warp (the finish buffer), the atomic variants 256 or 640 bytes more (histogram and dummy words), W3 with compaction N over 2 words per warp (8 KB at N 4096), `W3_d4` and N1 none. Synchronization: ballots, `redux.sync`, shuffles and `__syncwarp` over the full mask, shared atomics in the atomic variants, no barriers, the caller separates calls that reuse the temp storage or the output by a `__syncwarp`. NaN unsupported (its image is unordered), infinities and denormals are ordinary keys after the bracket guard of T60. Both requirements run the same code.

### 22.3 Where the time goes

Measured decomposition at bf16 N 512 k 64 (IPT 16), from the variant differences in the same sessions and the SASS:

| Component | Cycles | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Whole call, ballot histogram (`N6w_ballot`, `N6w_v2`) | 2412 and 2429 (control W2_packed_v3 2704) | cards `N6_w_ballot`, `N6_w_v2` |
| Whole call, atomic histogram (`N6w_atom32`) | 1879 (control N6w_v2 2429) | card `N6_w_atom32` |
| Five ballots per item plus bucket match and popc, against one shared atomic per item | 550 (34 per item) | `N6w_v2` minus `N6w_atom32`, T59 |
| Trimmed finish (13 fewer instructions per item, rounds unrolled by four) | minus 35 (slower) | card `N6_w_atom32f` (1914 against 1879) |
| k dependence k 16 to 256 (per-lane top-m bound plus W2 rounds) | 920 (1644 to 2564) | card `N6_w_atom32` |
| Per added item at k 64 (ballot variant), IPT 8 to 16, 16 to 32, 32 to 64 | 105, 183, 179 | card `N6_w_ballot` |

- The first build measured about 75 instructions per item on the common path (pass 28, post-scan 10, finish 35) and the SASS showed ptxas already interleaving the ballots across items (`VOTE` every five to six instructions in a 6160-instruction kernel with 678 `VOTE`, 1656 `LOP3`, 262 `POPC`), so the pass was issue bound at the ballot throughput floor (five ballots at 6 to 7 cycles each, T17, T33). One shared atomic per item instead took 1.2x to 1.5x off every cell up to IPT 32 and 1.3x to 1.9x in throughput (T59).
- Below IPT 64 what remains is dependent fixed cost, not per-item issue: two redux and a float division for the bracket, the 5-step shuffle scan (about 190 cycles), the pivot ballot, popc and shuffle (about 80), the serial per-item min and max chain of the pivot bin's images (about 10 cycles per item), two `__syncwarp` with their loads and about n over 2 redux rounds. Trimming 13 instructions per item from the finish changed nothing at IPT 8 to 32 (`atom32f`), which is the evidence.  (cards `N6_w_atom32` and `N6_w_atom32f`, verified.)
- Registers 64 at IPT 8, 80 to 116 at 16, 128 to 168 at 32, 255 at IPT 64 (8 to 28 bytes of spill) and 255 with a 4 to 5 KB stack frame, 600 to 800 bytes of spill, about 400 `STL` and 700 `LDL` at IPT 128 (N 4096), where every variant runs 60 to 80 thousand cycles against W2's 6.5 to 48 thousand, a local-memory artifact of the float tile plus the per-item bins plus the finish arrays (T36), not the algorithm. At IPT 64 the schedule of a full register file decides between variants (the trimmed finish 1.2x to 1.5x faster for fp32 and 5 to 9 percent slower for bf16 at N 2048 on the same instruction stream, T28).

| `W3_d4c` against the pre-fix N6w_ballot (batch w3a) | Superseded by w3b, same ratios, the control carried the T60 bug | batch only, no card |

### 22.5 Ideas not yet tried

- The dependent fixed costs that dominate below IPT 64: a ballot scan over the count bits instead of the 5-step shuffle scan (about 70 cycles), an approximate reciprocal for the bracket scale (any positive scale keeps the map monotone), tree-reduced per-item min and max accumulators for the pivot bin (about 100 cycles at IPT 16), a redux-based pivot search.  (estimates from the 22.3 decomposition).
- The register footprint at IPT 64 and 128: a two-half item loop over the tile, or recomputing the image from a packed 16-bit key for bf16 (half the tile registers), which decides whether N6w can own N 2048 to 4096 at all.
- Candidate compaction before N6's second bracket pass (k 1024: at most N over 8 candidates remain, the pass still sweeps the whole tile with six ballots per item), the W3 compaction code applies as is.
- Built and not measured within the eight batches: `N6w_atom128` (four times smaller pivot bins), `W3_d5c` (5-bit digits), `W3_d4` (no compaction), `N6w_v3`, W3 with the atomic histogram and 8-bit digits.
- The W2 finish list depth as a function of IPT was set from the cost model and not swept. An `sm_100f` variant has no lever. A sub-warp form of N1 (ballots serve every logical warp at once, T37, T40) for LW 8 N 128 k 64.

### 22.6 Determinism and ordering

Set deterministic by construction (exact selection). `prefer_smaller_index` is inherent in every path: the W2 finish orders by (key, inverted index), the tie-class emission ranks by index in lane-major order on the blocked arrangement (or slot-major on a compacted array, which is index order there), and the paths that take a whole bin need no tie handling. Both requirements compile the same code and measured identical cycles in every cell pair of every batch. Sorted output is a post-sort of k. Signed zero one tie class. NaN unsupported by contract.

### 22.7 Open items

- The arch pair is settled: the B200 rerun reproduces every cell to the cycle below N 4096, the B200 is 3 to 4 percent faster in the N 4096 local-memory cells and 6 percent slower in throughput on both sides. Open lead: atom32f at fp32 N 4096 (2.8x to 3.1x over atom32 in the card's own data, 22.4), which may give N6w an N 4096 cell under W2 for fp32, one same-section batch away.
- Three correctness bugs were found and fixed during the session, each caught by the oracle battery and never by random input: lanes beyond 2^d buckets double counting in a 4-bit digit pass (an infinite loop), items above the range dropped by the W3 compaction, and the degenerate bracket turning infinite items into NaN images (T60, found with `model_device.py`, which reproduces the device arithmetic and should accompany any bracket code). T60 names the block N6, B4 and N9 brackets as in scope, their batteries passed inf_denorm at the measured cells.
- Eight measurement batches (n6w1, w3a, n6w2, w3b, n1b, atom32b, atom32fb, atom32w2), seven built and not measured (atom128b, w3d5b, w3nc, v3b, v3w2, atom32fw2, n6w1b).
- The cross-arch picture against W5 (section 18, B200): at k 64 W5_v4 is ahead at N 1024 (2936 against 3682) and N 2048 (4163 against 11854) and N6w is ahead at N 256 to 512 and at k 128 and above where W5 was not measured, a same-session pairing on one node is for the final evaluation.
- The adapters assume the harness separates calls by a `__syncwarp` (it does), nothing was asked of the evaluation engineer.
