## 23. W4, k-truncated merge of sorted runs at block and warp scope (wave 2, the sorted routes)

Status of every number: opt-W4's five cards (`W4_chunk4_b200_20261025_1` against B3_tuned and B6_radix, `W4_chunk8_b200_20261025_1` against B3_tuned, `W4_chunk4q_b200_20261025_1` against W4_chunk4 and B3_tuned, `W4_wmerge_b200_20261025_1` against W2_packed and W5_v4, `W4_wmerge2_b200_20261025_1` against W4_wmerge), B200 umbriel-b200-091 (sm_100, nvcc 13.4.92, harness 0.8 with `--runs M` sorted-runs input, 2026-10-25, one lease 16:30 to 17:20 UTC), random normal input, every oracle passed, every fit R squared 1.0000, canary 32.001 cycles per op at both ends of every batch, controls in the same lock section. All five cards were verified by verify-W4 on the cards' node umbriel-b200-091 under the cards' harness 0.8 (commit 7dd0b746dc): 110 cells within 0.6 percent (bf16 N 8192 k 2048 chunk merge 4259.6 against B3_tuned 6496.3, fp32 6559.0 against 7094.5, 3.18x and 4.96x under B6_radix, the warp sorted bf16 N 2048 k 1024 19786.6 against W2's 150080.5 and fp32 42599 against 154785, the k 64 losses to W5_v4 as the card), resources identical, T62 reproduced by rebuilding the two earlier commits (2.72x and 1.57x). Sorted output: harness 0.8 had no order check in code, so the verifier added an exact key-descending index-ascending check over all k slots and ran it oracle-only over the 22-case battery, every W4 variant passes in every cell, B6_radix passes, and B3_tuned fails 20 of 44 cases as the negative control, so the check discriminates (now the `--check-sorted` flag of harness 0.10, used by phase 3b). The sorted-runs input is a genuine merge input (runs non-increasing, ascents only at the run boundaries, the whole row never sorted). The chunk cards were built from the v2 batches (commit 6f4b9c2190), the warp cards from the final build v4 (0ba868b00d). Report: `$SWARM/approaches/W4/REPORT.md` (section 6 holds the cheapest-sorted-route tables, copied into section 16), catalogue T62 (contributed), T7, T28, T29, T34, T36, T42, T48, T49, T54, T55 (adopted). Phase 3b (16): on unsorted rows W4_wmerge is the sorted route at k 256 and 1024 from N 512 (fp32) or N 2048 (bf16) and at fp32 N 512 and 2048 k 64 under prefer_smaller_index, W5_v4 at k 4 to 64 from N 512, the W1 level network elsewhere, and the merge of presorted runs stays the cheapest sorted route when the input arrives as runs.

### 23.1 What it is

Every item is one unsigned word whose integer order is (key descending, index ascending): fp32 keys a 64-bit word (order-preserving twiddle of the key in the high half, the bit-inverted source index in the low half), bf16 keys a 32-bit word (16 twiddled key bits above the inverted 16-bit index), minus zero folded onto plus zero, the all-zero word an absent item. The merge tree takes R sorted runs of RunLen words and merges adjacent pairs level by level, every merged run truncated to min(k, 2 x in) items, so no level carries more than k items per run and the last level emits the k largest of the union, sorted. A pair's output is split evenly over its threads: thread j owns output positions [j x OPT, (j + 1) x OPT), finds the merge-path split of its end diagonal with a binary search of a compile-time step count (predicated body, the static search of `reference/WARP_MERGE_SORT_RESULTS.md` section 4), takes the start split from its predecessor (one shared exchange and barrier at block scope, one `__shfl_up_sync` at warp scope), and merges in chunks of C (16, or OPT when smaller): up to C words from each side are loaded, the C largest of the 2C taken with one half-cleaner stage (also counting the wins of the first run) and a bitonic merge of log2(C) stages on static register indices, the chunk stored, both heads advanced by the counted wins. The merge-path comparator takes the first run on ties, which is the word order, so the result is exact under both requirements. Two forms share the tree: `W4_chunk4` and `W4_chunk8` (block, 256 threads, the blocked tile of N keys as M = 4 or 8 consecutive runs of N over M that arrive sorted, as an exact per-chunk top-k emits them, log2(M) levels with 2 barriers each) and `W4_wmerge` (warp, lane l holds items [l x IPT, (l + 1) x IPT), sorts them in registers with a Batcher odd-even merge sort (543 exchanges at IPT 64), writes its run, then five merge levels truncated at k with one `__syncwarp` between levels). `W4_wmerge2` sorts per lane in two halves of 32 with one more search-free merge level, `W4_chunk4q` is a 4-ary merge path for the block form.

### 23.2 Contract

Keys bf16 or fp32, int32 indices from the blocked position, both unsorted requirements by the same code (the word order is the tie-break), output k indices in the harness's shared array sorted by (key descending, index ascending), slot r holds rank r, keys never materialized, the input tile not modified. NaN twiddled bitwise (a positive NaN above plus infinity, the all-ones negative NaN collides with the filler word, focus-set inputs contain none). `W4_chunk<M>`: 256 threads, blocked input whose M runs of N over M consecutive items arrive sorted (not checked, an unsorted run gives a wrong result), N over M a power of two and a multiple of IPT, k a power of two with 2k at most N such that every level splits evenly, N at most 32768 (fp32) or 65536 (bf16). Temp storage two word buffers plus 256 ints: 66960 bytes for fp32 N 8192 M 4 (50464 bf16), 75264 for fp32 N 8192 M 8, 38400 for fp32 N 4096 M 8. Sync 2 log2(M) + 1 `__syncthreads` per call, the caller separates consecutive calls by a barrier. `W4_wmerge`: one warp, blocked input of N = 32 x IPT keys with IPT a power of two in [2, 64] (the per-lane network under the local-memory threshold of T34), k a power of two in [32, N over 2]. Temp storage two word buffers: 39936 bytes for fp32 N 2048 k 1024, 20064 for bf16 N 2048 k 1024, 9824 for bf16 N 512 k 256 (8 warps times the fp32 N 2048 storage exceed the 227 KB block limit of the throughput regime, those cells are latency only). Sync six `__syncwarp` and five `__shfl_up_sync` per call, no barriers. Host test `approaches/W4/host_test.cpp`: the per-thread phases in a loop over the threads with the barriers emulated, both word widths, six patterns, three seeds per shape, 234 cases exact before the first node build.

### 23.3 Where the time goes

Block chunk merge (cards `W4_chunk4` and `W4_chunk8`, B3_tuned and B6_radix in the same section):

| Cell (runs x run length, k) | W4 | B3_tuned | Ratio | B6_radix | W4 against B3 throughput (Gelem/s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bf16 N 8192, 4 x 2048, k 2048 | 4266 | 6501 | 1.52x | 13511 | 525 against 426 |
| fp32 N 8192, 4 x 2048, k 2048 | 6571 | 7100 | 1.08x | 32578 | 319 against 338 |
| bf16 N 8192, 8 x 1024, k 1024 | 5194 | 6318 | 1.22x | (B6 card 13505) | 448 against 449 |
| fp32 N 8192, 8 x 1024, k 1024 | 8151 | 6714 | 0.82x | (B6 card 32513) | 280 against 360 |
| bf16 N 4096, 8 x 512, k 512 | 3869 | 3790 | 0.98x | (B6 card 8068) | 369 against 516 |
| fp32 N 4096, 8 x 512, k 512 | 5230 | 4310 | 0.82x | (B6 card 21207) | 291 against 413 |

- Phase profile of the block form (`approaches/W4/phase_probe.cu`, one block, 256 calls, clock64 stamps with a barrier after each phase, not a harness batch): bf16 M 4 stage 1245, level 0 search plus split exchange 1497 and merge 1066, level 1 931 and 557 (5298 with the probe syncs), fp32 M 4 2454, 1016 and 2693, 1083 and 1490 (8737), fp32 M 8 adds a third level of 997 and 822 (10716). At 256 threads the two or three static searches with their exchange barrier cost about 1000 cycles each (12 steps of two dependent loads, about 85 cycles per step with the barrier) and are the largest phase for bf16, the chunk merges are issue bound (a 48 or 20 exchange network per thread, 2 `VIMNMX` per exchange on the bf16 word, 2 `ISETP` and 4 `SEL` on the fp32 word) and cost 2.5x more for fp32. Registers 116 to 123 (64 for M 8 at N 4096), no spill, 13 and 17 `BAR.SYNC`, temp storage 50 to 67 KB (M 4) and 30 to 75 KB (M 8), occupancy 2 (N 8192) or 4 blocks per SM.
- Warp truncated merge sort (card `W4_wmerge`, final build v4): bf16 N 2048 k 1024 19786 against W2_packed 150078 (7.59x), fp32 42598 against 154818 (3.63x), bf16 N 2048 k 256 13538 against 40422 (2.99x), fp32 29581 against 42915 (1.45x), bf16 N 1024 k 256 8408 against 21859, fp32 17190 against 21315, bf16 N 512 k 256 4875 against 12361, fp32 9327 against 12616, and against W5_v4 at k 64: bf16 N 1024 5416 against 2926 (0.54x), N 2048 9036 against 4177 (0.46x), fp32 N 1024 11342 against 4773 (0.42x), N 2048 19146 against 5532 (0.29x). Throughput bf16 N 2048 k 1024 102.7 against W2's 16.1 Gelem/s, N 2048 k 256 189 against 60, the fp32 N 2048 cells latency only.
- Phase profile of the final warp build (one warp, 256 calls): bf16 N 2048 k 1024 prepare (words, Batcher sort of 64, run store) 6439, searches 451 to 1122, merges 1864, 2389, 2932, 3581 and 1178 (total 21986). fp32: prepare 15697, merges 4657 to 8791 and 2446 (47067). bf16 N 2048 k 64: prepare 6404 then merges 1140, 529, 310, 213, 65 (the truncation shrinks the outputs per lane to 32, 16, 8, 4, 2). Three things set the warp cost: the chunk merge is instruction bound at one warp (per chunk of 16 outputs 32 or 64 shared loads with their address arithmetic, a 48-exchange network and 16 or 32 stores, 2 to 3 cycles per instruction with nothing to hide the dependent latencies), the fp32 per-lane sort of 64 words (29k cycles in the v3 probe for 543 exchanges, 9 cycles each, at 255 registers ptxas serializes the network), and the searches are a minor term (about 10 percent), unlike at block scope. The merge of the same 64 outputs per lane grows from level 0 to level 3 (1864 to 3581 bf16) because the skewed layout spreads runs over banks and not over the lanes of one pair (16-way conflicts at level 3).

|---|---|---|
| v1, the first build (block and warp, controls B3_tuned, W2_packed, W5_v4) | Block bf16 N 8192 k 2048 4251 against 6497, fp32 8123 against 7115. The block merge beats the unsorted selection on bf16 at once, the warp form is slow and the fp32 word is the problem on both | batches v1, no card |
| v2, swizzle on the absolute buffer position, fp32 key and index halves in separate arrays with the level 0 index implicit, no per-run pad | The v1 swizzle on the run-relative position put the 16 lane pairs of level 0 on the same banks (16-way conflict) at warp scope. Block fp32 8123 to 6571 (1.08x over B3_tuned, temp storage 100 KB to 67 KB), bf16 4251 to 4266. Warp bf16 N 512 k 256 8340 to 7160, N 2048 k 1024 bf16 59199 (2.54x over W2), fp32 112403 (1.38x, the 2x bar missed). Kept | `W4_chunk4`, `W4_chunk8` (built from these batches), verified (4259.6 against 6496.3 and 6559.0 against 7094.5 at N 8192 k 2048, sorted output checked) |
| v3, the chunk loop rolled (`#pragma unroll 1`, static register indices) and a tree sum of the win flags (T62) | bf16 N 2048 k 1024 59199 to 21783 (SASS 21080 to 10216 instructions, the 200-byte stack frame gone), fp32 112403 to 71573 (39048 to 19320), fp32 N 2048 k 256 83688 to 56320, cells with one chunk per level unchanged. The unrolled four chunks per level made 20k to 40k instruction kernels that paid instruction fetch (T48's mechanism). Kept | batches v3, T62 |
| `W4_wmerge2`, the per-lane sort in two halves of 32 with one more search-free merge level | Against v3 0.80x to 0.91x in 8 of 10 cells, 1.31x and 1.06x at fp32 N 2048 k 256 and k 1024 where the 64-word fp32 sort is at its worst. Against the final build in one section 0.91x to 0.98x in all 10 cells (bf16 N 2048 k 1024 20779 against 19780, fp32 43326 against 42596). Not adopted | `W4_wmerge2`, verified |
| v4, the warp form's layout and loads: linear addressing with an odd run stride instead of the xor swizzle (one instruction with an immediate offset per access instead of three), unconditional chunk loads into a 16-word zero pad after every run instead of loads predicated by the exact consumed count (no end split, no split exchange, no shuffle) | bf16 N 512 k 256 7095 to 4875, N 2048 k 1024 21783 to 19786, fp32 N 512 k 256 14074 to 9327, N 1024 k 256 22221 to 17190, fp32 N 2048 k 1024 71573 to 42598 (3.63x over W2, the 2x bar passed on both key types), fp32 N 2048 k 256 56320 to 29581 (1.45x over W2 where v3 lost), registers 168 to 255 down to 64 to 128. At one warp the address instructions and the predicate per load were the cost, not the bank conflicts they addressed. The block form keeps the swizzle and the exact counts (its lanes stride through one run and its loads are half the traffic at 8 warps). The card of record | `W4_wmerge`, verified (19786.6 against 150080.5 bf16 and 42599 against 154785 fp32 at N 2048 k 1024, the T62 builds rebuilt at 2.72x and 1.57x, sorted output checked) |
| `W4_chunk4q`, a 4-ary merge path for the block form (three probes per side per step, 6 steps instead of 12) | Against W4_chunk4 in one section at N 8192 k 2048 bf16 5035 against 4250 (0.84x), fp32 7387 against 6581 (0.89x), against B3_tuned 1.29x and 0.96x. 36 probe loads per thread instead of 24 through a shared memory pipe that 8 warps share, plus the select cascade and three address computations per step, cost more than the six saved dependent steps, as the k-ary search lost at warp scope in July (`WARP_MERGE_SORT_RESULTS.md` section 14.1). Not adopted | `W4_chunk4q`, verified |

Both bars of the idea card are met: the chunk merge of 4 sorted runs of 2048 (the DSA V3.2 merge shape) emits the sorted top 2048 of 8192 candidates at 1.52x (bf16) and 1.08x (fp32) under B3_tuned's unsorted selection in the same section, 3.2x and 5.0x under B6's full sort and about 3x under select plus post-sort, so it is the cheapest sorted route at every merge cell measured (M 4 and 8, k 512 to 2048) and beats the unsorted selection at M 4 (at M 8 1.22x bf16 at N 8192 and 0.82x fp32, 0.98x and 0.82x at N 4096). The warp truncated merge sort passes its 2x bar at 7.6x and 3.6x over W2 at N 2048 k 1024, takes every k 256 sorted cell from W2 (1.24x to 3.0x) and loses to W5_v4 at k 64 by 1.9x to 3.4x as the card expected, so the sorted-output crossover at warp scope sits between k 64 (W5 plus its sorted finish) and k 256 (W4). The tables of the cheapest sorted route per cell are in section 16.

### 23.5 Ideas not yet tried

- The fp32 per-lane sort at IPT 64 (29k of 72k cycles at N 2048 k 1024 in v3): sort in four pieces of 16 and merge them with two lane-local levels, or a key-only u32 network with the index as payload and a stable tie rule, or an `sm_100f` build where the key half stays a float and uses `FMNMX`.
- A hybrid run layout for the deeper levels where the 16 lanes of a pair read one run 32 words apart (predicted 15 to 20 percent at k 1024), a 4-way merge path per level for M 8 (halving the levels and the barriers of the block form), `STS.128` staging with a quad-preserving swizzle, the fp32 level 1 words as one 64-bit array (one `LDS.64` per item).  (predictions from the 23.3 profile).
- N 4096 at warp scope (IPT 128): an in-lane merge level of two sorted 64-runs before the five cross-lane levels, so the per-lane network stays at 64 (T34).
- The throughput regime of the warp form at fp32 N 2048 (a 16-bit index half would bring N 2048 k 256 inside the 227 KB limit). Runs shorter than N over M (per-run lengths in the merge path and the pads).

### 23.6 Determinism and ordering

Inherent. The word order is (key descending, index ascending), the merge path takes the first run on ties (its indices are smaller) and the chunk network compares whole words, so the output is the exact `prefer_smaller_index` result in sorted order and both requirements run the same binary, identical to the cycle in every measured pair (4266 against 4258, 19786 against 19787, 6571 against 6585). Minus zero equals plus zero (folded before the twiddle, the signed_zero pattern passes under the exact requirement). `prefer_larger_index` would store the index uninverted and swap the tie rule to the second run, not built. Sorted output is the primitive's output, at no extra cost.  (cards `W4_chunk4` and `W4_wmerge`, verified.)

### 23.7 Open items

- Verified on the cards' node only (one healthy B200, harness 0.8), no GB200 card, no `sm_100f` variant.
- The chunk merge's contract needs the runs sorted and full. A device agent whose chunk results come from B1 to B4 or N6 (unsorted) needs W5, W2 or a sort for the chunks first, or the unsorted B3_tuned over the M k candidates (7100 against 6571 for fp32 and 6501 against 4266 for bf16 at M 4).  (cards `W4_chunk4` and `W4_chunk8` against `B3_tuned`, verified.)
- Against N6 at warp scope (section 22, GB200) the sorted premium of W4 at k 256 and 1024 is unmeasured, the two lanes ran in parallel.
- Four build-and-measure rounds (v1 to v4) of 26 `run.py` batches plus three phase-probe runs on one lease, every oracle passed. The chunk cards carry the v2 commit, the block code of v4 is the same algorithm (the rolled loop does not apply at one chunk per level).
